- धीतिभिः (*dhītibhiḥ*)—supporting
- मातृभिः (*mātṛbhiḥ*)—by the waters वसतीवरीभिः (*vasatīvarībhiḥ*)
- स्वे (*sve*)—your
- दमे (*dame*)—house-like
- दमे (*dame*)—in the sacrifice
- सं मर्जयसि (*saṃ marjayasi*)—you are purified
- परावतः (*parāvataḥ*)—from a very distant place
- साम न (*sāma na*)—as the sound of the sāman is heard
- यत्र (*yatra*)—in which sound of pressing
- धीतयः (*dhītayaḥ*)—the sacrificers, supporters of the rite
- रणंति (*raṇaṃti*)—rejoice
- तत् (*tat*)—that sound of yours is heard by all
- रोचमानः (*rocamānaḥ*)—the shining Soma
- अरुषीभिः (*aruṣībhiḥ*)—with the glittering
- त्रिधातुभिः (*tridhātubhiḥ*)—with the waters that sustain the three worlds
- वयः (*vayaḥ*)—food
- दधे (*dadhe*)—gives
- वयः (*vayaḥ*)—food
- दधे (*dadhe*)—provides

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Soma, you take the famous wealth of cattle carried off by the Paṇis. You are purified in your house, the sacrifice, by the waters that support the sacrifice. As the sound of the sāman is heard from a very distant place, that sound of your pressing, in which the sacrificers rejoice, is heard by all. The shining Soma, together with the glittering waters that sustain the three worlds, gives food to the sacrificers.

##### English translation
You did discover the wealth of the Panis, and are washed in your own house at the sacrifice by the mothers, the supporters of the sacrifice ; as the hymn (is heard) from afar, so it (is heard by all, the hymn) in which the supporters (of the rite) rejoice ; the shining (Soma) with its brilliant (waters), the supporters of the three (worlds), gives food (to the worshippers.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> पूर्वा॒मनु॑ प्र॒दिशं॑ याति॒ चेकि॑त॒त्सं र॒श्मिभि॑र्यतते दर्श॒तो  
> रथो॒ दैव्यू॑ दर्श॒तो रथः॑ ।  
> अग्म॑न्नु॒क्थानि॒ पौंस्येंद्रं॒ जैत्रा॑य हर्षयन् ।  
> वज्र॑श्च॒ यद्भव॑थो॒ अन॑पच्युता स॒मत्स्यन॑पच्युता ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 139

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पूर्वां । अनु । प्रदिशं याति । चेकितत् । सं । शश्मिभिः । यतते । दर्शतः ।  
> रथः । दैव्यः । दर्शतः । रथः ।  
> अग्मन् । उक्तानि । पौंस्या । इंद्रं । जैत्राय । हर्षयन् ।  
> वब्रः । च । यत् . भवथः । अनपच्यता । समत्सु । अनपच्युता ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> चेकितज्ञानानः सोमः पूर्वां प्राचीनं प्रदिशं प्रकृष्णां दिशमनु याति ।
> अनुगच्छति । किंच दर्शतः सर्वैर्दर्शनीयो दैवॊ देवेषु चवत्तन रथः सूर्यरश्मिभिः सं यतते । संगच्छते । पुनर्दर्शतो रथ इत्यावरार्णं । ततः पौंस्या
> पुंस्तावगमान्युक्थानि स्तोत्राण्यग्निः । इंद्रं गच्छंति । राहा जैत्राय जयार्थं
> तानि स्तोत्राणींद्रं हर्षयन् । हर्षयंति । तथा तस्य वजश्च तमिंग्रं गृति ।
> यद्यदा समत्सु संग्रामेत्वनपच्युतानपच्युता शत्रुभिरपराजित् सोम त्वं
> चेंद्रश्च युनां सह भवथः तदा स्तोत्रागमनादीनि जंति । पुनरनपच्युतॆ -
> त्यादरार्थं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Soma, the wise (चेकितत् (*cekitat*)), goes toward the eastern, foremost quarter. The chariot, visible to all and renowned among the gods, joins the sun's rays; the repetition दर्शतो रथः (*darśato rathaḥ*) is for emphasis. Then the manly hymns go to Indra and gladden him for the sake of victory; and his vajra also goes to Indra. When, in battles, you, Soma, and Indra are together unconquered by enemies, then the coming of the hymns and the rest takes place; the repetition अनपच्युता (*anapacyutā*) is for emphasis. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- चेकितत् (*cekitat*)—Soma, the wise
- पूर्वां प्रदितं (*pūrvāṃ praditaṃ*)—to the excellent eastern quarter
- अनु याति (*anu yāti*)—goes and joins
- दर्शतः (*darśataḥ*)—visible to all
- दैन्यं (*dainyaṃ*)—renowned among the gods
- रथः (*rathaḥ*)—Soma's chariot
- रश्मिभिः (*raśmibhiḥ*)—with the sun's rays
- सं यतते (*saṃ yatate*)—joins
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- समत्सु (*samatsu*)—in battles
- अनपच्युता भवथः (*anapacyutā bhavathaḥ*)—you and Indra are unconquered by enemies
- पौंस्या (*pauṃsyā*)—endowed with manly power
- उक्तानि (*uktāni*)—hymns
- अग्मन् (*agman*)—join Indra
- जैत्राय (*jaitrāya*)—for gaining victory
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- हर्षयन् (*harṣayan*)—gladden
- वज्रश्च (*vajraśca*)—the vajra weapon too joins Indra

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The wise Soma goes and joins the excellent eastern quarter. The chariot of Soma, visible to all and renowned among the gods, joins the sun's rays. O Soma, when you and Indra are unconquered by enemies in battles, then the hymns endowed with manly power join Indra and gladden him for the sake of victory. The vajra also joins Indra.

### Printed p. 140
The intelligent Soma proceeds to the eastern quarter; (your) beautiful chariot, (your) celestial beautiful chariot meets the sun's rays; the manly praises advance to Indra and simulate him to victory: (his) thunderbolt too (advances to him); so that you are invincible, invincible in battle,

## Sūkta 112 (Maṇḍala 9)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> नानानमिति चतुर्यचं नवमं सूक्तमांगिरसस्य शिशुनाम्न आर्षं । पंचपदा पंक्तिश्चंदः । पवमानः सोमो देवता । तथा चानुक्रांतं । नानानं चतुष्कं
> शिशुः पांक्तं हीति ॥ गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This sūkta, beginning with नानानं (*nānānaṃ*), has four Ṛks. Its seer is Śiśu Āṅgirasa; the metre is Pañcapadā Paṅktiḥ; the deity is Pavamāna Soma. Sāyaṇa cites the Anukramaṇī to the same effect. He adds that the viniyoga has been stated already.

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning नानानं (*nānānaṃ*) is the ninth sūkta in the seventh anuvāka. It has four Ṛks. Its seer is Śiśu Āṅgirasa, its deity is Pavamāna Soma, and its metre is Pañcapadā Paṅktiḥ. In the Anukramaṇī it is stated: नानानं चतुष्कं शिशुः पांक्तं हि (*nānānaṃ catuṣkaṃ śiśuḥ pāṃktaṃ hi*). The viniyoga has been stated only once.

##### Particulars

- Sūkta—112
- Maṇḍala—9; Anuvāka—7; Sūkta—112
- Aṣṭaka—7; Adhyāya—5; Varga—25 **NOTE**
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta—4
- Seer—Śiraḥ **NOTE**
- Deity—Pavamāna Soma
- Metre—Pāktiḥ **NOTE**

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ना॒ना॒नं वा उ॑ नो॒ धियो॒ वि व्र॒तानि॒ जना॑नां ।  
> तक्का॑रि॒ष्टं रु॒तं भि॒षग्ब्र॒ह्मा सु॒न्वंत॑मिच्छ॒तींद्रा॑येंदोः॒ ॑ 
> परि स्रव ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 141

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> नानानं । वै । ऊं इति । नः । धियः । वि । व्रतानि । जनानां ।  
> तक्षा । रिष्टं । रुतं । भिषक् । ब्रह्मा । सुन्वंतं । इच्छति । इंद्राय । इंदो इति ।  
> परि । स्रव . १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऋषिरेतदादिभिस्त्रिभिः सूक्तैरपरिस्रवतः सोमस्याजामित्वाय मनसो
> विनोदनं कुर्वन्नाह । हे सोम नोऽस्माकं धियः कर्माणि नानानं नानाजातीयकानि बहूनि भवंति । वैशब्दः प्रसिद्धर्थद्योतनार्थः । उ इति पूरणः ।
> तथान्येषामपि जनानां व्रतानि कर्माणि विविधानि भवंति । तक्षा त्वष्टा रिष्टं दारुतक्षणमिच्छति । तथा भिषगैद्यश्चित्सि को रुतं रोगमिच्छति । ब्रह्मा ब्राह्मणः सुन्वंतं
> सोमाभिषवं कुर्वंतं यजमानमिच्छति । तथाहं त्वत्परिस्रवणमिच्छामि । तस्माद्दे
> इंदो सोमेंद्रायेंद्रार्थं परि स्रव । परितः क्षर ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

With this and the next two sūkta-verses the seer, to console his mind about Soma not flowing, speaks. O Soma, our acts (धियः (*dhiyaḥ*)) are of many kinds; so too the acts of other men are varied. The carpenter (तक्षा (*takṣā*), the wood-cutter) desires timber-cutting; the physician desires illness (रुतं (*rutaṃ*)); the Brahmā priest desires the sacrificer who presses Soma. In the same way I desire your flowing. Therefore, O Indu, flow around for Indra, that is, stream on all sides. The particle वै (*vai*) marks a well-known fact; उ (*u*) is a filler.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Soma-deva) नः (*naḥ*)—our
- धियः (*dhiyaḥ*)—acts
- नानानं (*nānānaṃ*)—are of many kinds
- जनानां (*janānāṃ*)—of men
- व्रतानि (*vratāni*)—acts
- वि (*vi*)—are of many kinds
- तक्षा (*takṣā*)—the wood-worker
- रिष्टं (*riṣṭaṃ*)—wood-work
- इच्छति (*icchati*)—desires
- भिषक् (*bhiṣak*)—the physician
- रुतं (*rutaṃ*)—desires disease
- ब्रह्मा (*brahmā*)—the Brahmin
- सुन्वंतं (*sunvaṃtaṃ*)—desires the sacrificer who presses Soma
- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O Soma-deva
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra's drinking
- परि स्रव (*pari srava*)—flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Soma-deva, our acts are of many kinds. The acts of men are of many kinds. The carpenter desires wood-work. The physician desires disease. The Brahmin desires the one who presses Soma. O Soma-deva, flow for Indra's drinking.

##### English translation
Various are our acts, (various) are the occupations of men; the carpenter desires timber, the physician disease, the Brahman a worshipper who effuse Soma : flow, Indu, for Indra.

### Printed p. 142
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> जर॑तीभि॒रोष॑धीभिः प॒र्णेभिः॑ शकु॒नानां॑ ।  
> का॒र्मा॒रो अश्म॑भि॒र्दुर्यभि॒र्हिर॑ण्यवंतमिच्छ॒तींद्रा॑येंदो॒ परि॑  
> स्रव ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> जरतीभिः । ओषधीभिः! पर्णॆभिः । शकुनानां ।  
> कार्मारः । अश्वभिः । द्युभिः हिरण्यवंतं । इच्छति । इंद्राय । इंदो इति ।  
> परि । स्रव ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> जरतीभिर्जीर्णाभिरोषधीभिरिषवः क्रियंते तथा शकुनानां पर्णॆभिरिषूणां
> पक्षभूतैः पर्णॆश्च क्रियंते तथा द्युभिर्दिप्ताभिरिषूणां तेजनार्थाभिरश्मिभिः
> शिलाभिश्च क्रियंते । एतैः कार्मारोऽयस्कारो हिरण्यवंतमाढ्यं पुरुषमिच्छति ।
> तथाहं त्वत्परिस्रवणमिच्छामि । तस्मादिंदो इंद्राय परि स्रव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Arrows are made with old dried plants (जरतीभिः ओषधीभिः (*jaratībhiḥ oṣadhībhiḥ*)); likewise the feathers of birds serve as the feathering of arrows; and with shining (द्युभिः (*dyubhiḥ*)) stones, that is, whetting stones or rays for sharpening the arrows, they are made. With these the smith (कार्मारः (*kārmāraḥ*), the iron-worker) desires a rich man (हिरण्यवंतं (*hiraṇyavaṃtaṃ*)). So I desire your flowing; therefore, O Indu, flow for Indra.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- जरतीभिः (*jaratībhiḥ*)—with the old
- ओषधीभिः (*oṣadhībhiḥ*)—with plants
- इषवः (*iṣavaḥ*)—arrows are made
- शकुनानां (*śakunānāṃ*)—of birds
- पर्णेभिः (*parṇebhiḥ*)—with feathers the feathers of arrows are made
- द्युभिः (*dyubhiḥ*)—shining
- अश्वभीः (*aśvabhīḥ*)—with stones they are also made
- कार्मारः (*kārmāraḥ*)—the goldsmith (or the carpenter) **NOTE**
- हिरण्यवंतं (*hiraṇyavaṃtaṃ*)—a wealthy man
- इच्छति (*icchati*)—desires
- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O Soma-deva
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra
- परि स्रव (*pari srava*)—flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Arrows are made with dried sticks. The feathers of arrows are made with the feathers of birds. They are also made with shining stones. The goldsmith and the carpenter desire a wealthy man. O Soma-deva, flow for Indra.

##### English translation
With dried plants (are arrows made), with the feathers of birds (and) with glistening stones; the smith seeks a man who has gold: flow, Indu, for Indra.

### Printed p. 143
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> का॒रुर॒हं त॒तो भि॒षगु॑पलप्र॒क्षिणी॑ न॒ना ।  
> नाना॑धियो वसू॒यवोऽनु॒ गा इ॑व तस्थि॒मेंद्रा॑येंदो॒ ॑ 
> परि स्रव ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कारुः, अहं । ततः । भिषक् । उपलप्रक्षिणी । नना ।  
> नानाधियः । वसुयवः । अनु । गाः इव । तस्तिम । इंद्राय । इंदो इति !  
> परि । स्रव ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऋषिः प्रवृत्तसोमयागः सन् दशापवित्रात्सोमे क्षरति प्रतिबंधकपापापनुत्तये पुरश्चरितानुकीर्तनपुरःसरमध्येषणां चकार । तावदहं कारुः स्तोमानां
> कर्तास्मि । तत इति संताननाम । तन्यतेऽस्मादिति ततः पिता । तन्यतेसाविति वा ततः
> पुत्रः । भिषग्येषजकृत् । यज्ञस्य ब्रह्मेत्यर्थः । सर्वं त्रय्या विद्यया भिषज्यतीति
> श्रुतेः । ततोपलप्रक्षिणी । उपलेषु वालुकासु प्रक्षणोति यवान्हिनस्ति भृज्जतीति ।
> यद्वा । दृषदादिषूपलेषु बृष्टान्यवान्हिनस्ति चूर्णयतीति । अथवा धानासक्तुकरंभादीनां कारिका वा । असौ नना माता दुहिता वा । नमनक्रियायोग्यत्वात् ।
> माता खल्वपत्यं प्रति स्तनपानादिना नमनशीला भवति । दुहिता वा शुश्रूषार्थं ।
> एवं सर्वेषां परिचरणेन नानाधियो नानाकर्माणो वसूयवो धनकामा
> वयमनु तस्थिम । लोकमन्वास्थिताः स्मः । तत्र दृष्टांतः । गा इव ॥ छांदसो जसः
> शसादेशः ॥ गावो यथा गोष्ठमनुतिष्ठंति स्वपयःप्रदानेन परिचरंति वा एवं
> वयमपि परिचरामः । एतज्ञात्वा हे इंदो सोम इंद्राय परि स्रव । दशापवित्रात् क्षर । एवमेषाध्येषणा सत्कारपूर्विका व्यापारणा । अत्र निरुक्तं : उपलप्रक्षिणीत्युपलेषु प्रक्षिणात्युपलप्रक्षेपिणी वा । कारुरहमस्मि कर्ता स्तोमानां ततो
> भिषक् तत इति संताननाम पितुर्वा पुत्रस्य वा । उपलप्रक्षिणी सक्तुकारिका नना
> नमतेर्माता पा दुहिता वा ! नानाधियो नानाकर्माणो वसूयवो वसुकामा
> अन्वास्थिताः स्मो गाव इव लोकं । नि. ६.५ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The seer, having begun a Soma sacrifice, and Soma not flowing from the ten-fold filter, made a request, preceded by a recital of his own family's occupations, to remove the sin that obstructs. "I am a कारुः (*kāruḥ*), a maker of praises. ततः (*tataḥ*) is a name for offspring: one from whom (the family) is extended is father (ततः (*tataḥ*)), or one who is extended is son. भिषक् (*bhiṣak*) is the one who does the healing: the Brahmā of the sacrifice, since the Śruti says that everything is healed by the threefold knowledge. उपलप्रक्षिणी (*upalaprakṣiṇī*) is one who throws barley on the stones (grinding stones) and crushes or parches it; or, in another explanation, one who crushes the parched barley on mortar-stones; or she makes fried grain, flour, gruel and so on. नना (*nanā*) is mother or daughter, since she is fit for bowing: a mother bows to her child in nursing and so on; or a daughter for service. Thus by serving all, we, of various acts (नानाधियः (*nānādhiyaḥ*)) and desiring wealth (*vasūyavaḥ*), have taken our place in the world (अनु तस्थिम (*anu tasthima*)). Example: like cows (गा इव (*gā iva*); the plural accusative is Vedic): as cows keep to the stall or serve with the gift of their milk, so we serve. Knowing this, O Indu Soma, flow for Indra from the ten-fold filter. Such is this request, preceded by respect." Sāyaṇa then quotes the Nirukta (Ni. 6.5): उपलप्रक्षिणीत्युपलेषु प्रक्षिणात्युपलप्रक्षेपिणी वा । कारुरहमस्मि कर्ता स्तोमानां ततो भिषक् तत इति संताननाम पितुर्वा पुत्रस्य वा । उपलप्रक्षिणी सक्तुकारिका नना नमतेर्माता पा दुहिता वा ! नानाधियो नानाकर्माणो वसूयवो वसुकामा अन्वास्थिताः स्मो गाव इव लोकं । नि. ६.५ । इति ॥ (*upalaprakṣiṇītyupaleṣu prakṣiṇātyupalaprakṣepiṇī vā | kārurahamasmi kartā stomānāṃ tato bhiṣak tata iti saṃtānanāma piturvā putrasya vā | upalaprakṣiṇī saktukārikā nanā namatermātā pā duhitā vā ! nānādhiyo nānākarmāṇo vasūyavo vasukāmā anvāsthitāḥ smo gāva iva lokaṃ | ni. 6.5 | iti ‖*).

### Printed p. 144

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- कारुः (*kāruḥ*)—am a praiser
- ततः (*tataḥ*)—my father
- भिषक् (*bhiṣak*)—is a physician
- नना (*nanā*)—our mother
- उपलप्रक्षिणी (*upalaprakṣiṇī*)—puts grain on the grinding stone and grinds
- गाः इव (*gāḥ iva*)—like cows resorting to the stall
- नानाधियः (*nānādhiyaḥ*)—having acts of many kinds
- वसुयवः (*vasuyavaḥ*)—and desirous of wealth, we
- अनु तस्थिम (*anu tasthima*)—have taken our place in the world
- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O Soma
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra
- परि स्रव (*pari srava*)—flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

I am a praiser. My father is a physician. Our mother puts grain on the grinding stone and grinds. Like cows resorting to the stall, we, having acts of many kinds and desiring wealth, have taken up various ways of life in this world. O Soma, flow for Indra.

##### English translation
I am the singer ; father is the physician, mother throws the corn upon the grinding stones; having various occupations, desiring riches we remain (in the world) like cattle (in the stalls): flow, Indu, for Indra.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

At the beginning of this sūkta the commentator says: ऋषिरेतदादिभिस्त्रिभिः सूक्तैः अपरिस्रवतः सोमस्याजामित्वाय मनसो विनोदनं कुर्पन्नाह (*ṛṣiretadādibhistribhiḥ sūktaiḥ aparisravataḥ somasyājāmitvāya manaso vinodanaṃ kurpannāha*). The seer Śiśu, seer of this sūkta, was performing a sacrifice. At the time of pressing Soma, though he crushed and pressed the Soma plants, the Soma juice did not flow out properly. Noticing this, thinking स्वदुष्कृताशंकया किलापरिस्रवति सोमे पापसंकीर्तनादात्मशुद्धिर्भविष्यतीति मन्यमान आत्मानमेव निर्दिष्टन्नाह (*svaduṣkṛtāśaṃkayā kilāparisravati some pāpasaṃkīrtanādātmaśuddhirbhaviṣyatīti manyamāna ātmānameva nirdiṣṭannāha*) **NOTE**, he says: "Because of the sins I have committed, Soma does not come out of the plants in this way. Therefore if I proclaim my sins, and the acts done by me and by those connected with me, so that others hear them clearly, that sin will be pacified." So he has described in the Ṛks of this sūkta the activities of himself and of his father and the rest.

On these Ṛks Yāska has given the Nirukta:
कारुरहमस्मि कर्ता स्तोमानां । ततो भिषक् तत इति संताननाम पितुर्वा पुत्रस्य वा । उपलप्रक्षिणी सक्तुकारिका । नना नमतेर्माता ना दुहिता वा नानाधियो नानाकर्माणो वसूयवो वसुकामा अस्वास्थिताः स्मोगाव इव लोकं । इंद्रायेंदो परि स्रव इत्यध्येषणा ॥ (*kārurahamasmi kartā stomānāṃ | tato bhiṣak tata iti saṃtānanāma piturvā putrasya vā | upalaprakṣiṇī saktukārikā | nanā namatermātā nā duhitā vā nānādhiyo nānākarmāṇo vasūyavo vasukāmā asvāsthitāḥ smogāva iva lokaṃ | iṃdrāyeṃdo pari srava ityadhyeṣaṇā ‖*) (Ni. 6-5)

### Printed p. 145
That is: I am a praiser, one who composes words of praise and offers them for the delight of the gods; my father is a physician who treats diseases. Here, since the word ततः (*tataḥ*) denotes offspring, the word may mean father or son. For the word भिषक् (*bhiṣak*) the Bhāṣyakāra and the commentators of the Nirukta have explained in another way, leaving aside the meaning "physician". Here "diseases" are the defects and omissions that occur in sacrifices and the like, and the priest called Brahmā, who corrects them by expiations and the like, is the physician. The word नना (*nanā*) also has two meanings, mother and daughter: since she is fit to be bowed to she is the mother; since she bows to oneself she is the daughter; so it can be explained in two ways. Therefore नना (*nanā*) means my mother or daughter, who grinds flour with the grinding stone. Therefore all of our household are engaged in various works for the sacrifice. And we, desiring wealth, are intent on our several duties as cows go to their stalls. Therefore, O Soma, flow quickly from the plants for Indra's drinking: such is the intent of the explanation (of the Nirukta) **NOTE**

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अश्वो॒ वोळ्हा॑ सु॒खं रथं॑ हस॒नामु॑पमं॒त्रिणः॑ ।  
> शेपो॒ रोम॑ण्वंत् भे॒दौ वारिन्मं॒डूक॑ इच्छ॒तींद्रा॑येंदो॒ परि॑ स्रव ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अश्वः । वोळ्हा । सुखं । रथं । हसनां । उपमंत्रिणः .  
> शेपः । रोमण् ऽवंतौ । भेदौ । वाः । इत् । मंडूकः । इच्छति । इंद्राय ।  
> इंदो इति । परि , स्रव ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वोळ्हा लक्ष्यं देशं प्रापयन्नश्वः सुखं कल्याणं रथमिच्छति । उपमंत्रिण
> उपमंत्रवंतो नर्मसचिवा हसनामुपहसनां वाचमिच्छंति । तथा शेषः । शेषो
> नैतस इति पुंसृजननस्येति यास्कः । नि. ३-२१ । यथा शेषो रोमण्वंतौ भेदाविच्छति । मंडूको वारित् ; इदवधारणे । उदकमेवेच्छति । तथाहं त्वत्परिस्रवणमिच्छामि । तस्मादिंद्रायेंदो परि स्रवेति ॥
> xxvii 10

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The horse (अश्वः (*aśvaḥ*)) that carries (वोळ्हा (*voḷhā*)) one to the intended place desires a good, comfortable chariot. The jesting companions (उपमंत्रिणः (*upamaṃtriṇaḥ*)), those who confer in jest, desire mocking speech (हसनां (*hasanāṃ*)). So also शेपः (*śepaḥ*): Yāska says शेषो नैतस इति पुंसृजननस्येति (*śeṣo naitasa iti puṃsṛjananasyeti*) (Ni. 3-21), i.e. the male organ of generation. As this desires the two hairy thighs (रोमण्वंत् भेदौ (*romaṇvaṃt bhedau*)), and the frog (मंडूकः (*maṃḍūkaḥ*)) desires only water (वारित् (*vārit*); इत् (*it*) is used in the sense of restriction), so I desire your flowing. Therefore, O Indu, flow for Indra.

### Printed p. 146

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वोळ्हा (*voḷhā*)—(the horse) able to bear a burden
- अश्वः (*aśvaḥ*)—the horse
- सुखं (*sukhaṃ*)—easy to draw
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—the cart
- इच्छति (*icchati*)—desires
- उपमंत्रिणः (*upamaṃtriṇaḥ*)—the jesting companions
- हसनां शेषः (*hasanāṃ śeṣaḥ*)—desire speech full of mirth
- मंडूक (*maṃḍūka*)—the frog
- वाः इत् (*vāḥ it*)—desires water alone
- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O Soma
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra's drinking
- परि स्रव (*pari srava*)—flow
**NOTE**

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The horse, able to bear a burden, desires a cart that is easy to draw. The jesting companions desire speech full of mirth. The frog desires water alone. O Soma, flow for Indra's drinking.

##### English translation
The draught horse (desires) a cart easy (to draw); those who invite guests (desire) merriment ; the frog desires water : flow, Indu, for Indra.

## Sūkta 113 (Maṇḍala 9)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> शर्यणावतीत्येकादशर्चं दशमं सूक्तं मारीचस्य कश्यपस्यार्षं । पूर्वत्र
> हीत्युक्तत्वादिदमुत्तरं च पांक्तं । पवमानसोमदेवताकं । तथा चानुक्रम्यते ।
> शर्यणावत्येकादश कश्यप इति ॥ गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"Śaryaṇāvatī…" is the tenth sūkta, of eleven ṛks; its seer is Kaśyapa, son of Marīci. This sūkta and the next are in the Pāṅkta metre (paṅkti). The deity is Pavamāna Soma. The Anukramaṇī says: शर्यणावत्येकादश कश्यप इति (*śaryaṇāvatyekādaśa kaśyapa iti*). The viniyoga has been stated already.

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning "Śaryaṇāvatī" is the tenth sūkta in the seventh anuvāka. It has eleven ṛks. Kaśyapa, son of the sage Marīci, is its seer; this sūkta and the one after it are in the paṅkti metre. Pavamāna Soma is the deity. The statement in the Anukramaṇī, शर्यणावत्येकादश कश्यपः (*śaryaṇāvatyekādaśa kaśyapaḥ*), has been given; the viniyoga was stated earlier.

##### Particulars

Sūkta 113. Maṇḍala 9; Anuvāka 7; Sūkta 113; Aṣṭaka 7; Adhyāya 5; Vargas 26, 27. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 11. Seer: Kaśyapa. Deity: Pavamāna Soma. Metre: Paṅkti.

### Printed p. 147
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> श॒र्य॒णाव॑ति॒ सोम॒मिंद्रः॑ पिबतु वृत्र॒हा ।  
> बलं॒ दधा॑न आ॒त्मनि॑ करि॒ष्यन्वी॒र्यं॑ म॒हदिंद्रा॑येंदो॒ परि॑  
> स्रव ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शर्यणावति । सोमं । इंद्रः पिबतु । वृत्रहा  
> बलं । दधानः । आत्मनि । करिष्यन् वीर्यं । महत् । इंद्राय । इंदो इति ।  
> परि । स्रव ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> शर्यणावति । शर्यणावन्नाम कुरुक्षेत्रस्य जघनार्धे सरः । तत्र स्थितं
> सोमं वृत्रहेंद्रः पिबतु । कीदृशः । आत्मनि बलं दधानो बलं निदधानः अत एव
> महद्वीर्यं करिष्यञ्यत्रून्प्रति । तस्मादिंद्रायेंदो परि स्रव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Śaryaṇāvat is the name of a lake in the rear half of Kurukṣetra. Let Indra, the slayer of Vṛtra, drink the Soma that stands there. How is he? He holds strength within himself, and therefore will perform great valour against his enemies. Therefore, O Indu, flow for Indra.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आत्मनि (*ātmani*)—in himself
- बलं (*balaṃ*)—strength
- दधानः (*dadhānaḥ*)—bearing
- महत् (*mahat*)—abundant
- वीर्यं (*vīryaṃ*)—valour
- करिष्यन् (*kariṣyan*)—about to show before his enemies
- वृत्रहा (*vṛtrahā*)—slayer of foes
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- शरणावति (*śaraṇāvati*)—grown in the lake called Śaraṇāvat
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—Soma
- पिबतु (*pibatu*)—let him drink
- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O Soma
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra's drinking
- परि स्रव (*pari srava*)—flow
**NOTE**

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Let Indra, who holds strength within himself, who will display abundant valour before his enemies, and who destroys foes, drink the Soma grown in the lake called Śaraṇāvat. O Soma, flow for Indra's drinking.

##### English translation
Let Indra, the slayer of Vritra, quaff the Soma on the Sharyan avat infusing strength into himself, about to show great prowess : flow, Indu, for Indra.

### Printed p. 148

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Śaryaṇāvati—Śaryaṇāvat is the name of a lake near Kurukṣetra. It is traditionally said that Soma plants grew abundantly in the regions near this lake. An account has also been given earlier that the horse-shaped head of the sage Dadhyañc fell near this lake.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ प॑वस्व दिशां पत आर्जि॒कात्सो॑म मीढ्वः ।  
> ऋ॒त॒वा॒केन॑ स॒त्येन॑ श्र॒द्धया॒ तप॑सा सु॒त इंद्रा॑येंदो॒ परि॑  
> स्रव ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । पवस्व । दिशां । पते । अर्जिकात् । सोम , माध्वः ।  
> ऋतवाकेन । सत्येन । श्रद्धया । तपसा । सुतः । इंद्राय।इंदो इति । परि । स्रवः

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे दिशां पते प्राच्यादीनां दिशां प्रकाशकत्वेन स्वामिन् हे मीड्वः
> कामानां सेक्तर्हे सोम आर्जिकात् । ऋजीकामदूरभव आर्जिको जनपदः ।
> तस्मादा पवस्व । अस्मद्यज्ञं प्रत्यागच्छ । यद्वा । आर्जिकादृजोरकुटिलात्पवित्रात्
> क्षर । कीदृशः । ऋतवाकेनर्तस्य वचनेन सत्येन । सत्यर्तयोरल्पो भेदो द्रष्टव्यः ।
> श्रद्धया तपसा युक्तैर्दीक्षितैः सुतोऽभिषुतस्तं पवस्व ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O lord of the directions, lord of the eastern and other directions as their illuminator; O showerer of desires; O Soma, from Ārjīka—Ārjīka is the country not far from Ṛjīkā—flow hither, come back to our sacrifice. Or else: flow from the Ārjīka, the straight, from the strainer. How is he? Pressed with the speech of ṛta and with satya (a slight difference between satya and ṛta is to be observed), by the initiated, endowed with faith and austerity. Flow thus pressed.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दिशां पते (*diśāṃ pate*)—guardian of all directions
- मीध्वः (*mīdhvaḥ*)—showerer of desires
- सोम (*soma*)—O divine Soma
- सत्येन (*satyena*)—true
- ऋतवाकेन (*ṛtavākena*)—with words of praise
- श्रद्धया (*śraddhayā*)—with faith
- तपसा (*tapasā*)—with austerity
- सुतः (*sutaḥ*)—you who are pressed
- अर्जिकात् (*arjikāt*)—from the Arjika country
- आ पवस्व (*ā pavasva*)—flow
- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O Soma
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra's drinking
- परि स्रव (*pari srava*)—flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O divine Soma, guardian of all directions and showerer of desires, pressed with faith and austerity together with true words of praise, flow from the Arjīka country. O Soma, flow for Indra's drinking.
**NOTE**

### Printed p. 149

##### English translation
Soma, lord of the four regions, sprinkler ( of benefits ) flow from Arjika effused by a truthful man) with faith and devotion : flow, Indu, for Indra.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Ārjikāt—यजीकानामदूरभव आर्जिको जनपदः । तस्मात् । (*yajīkānāmadūrabhava ārjiko janapadaḥ | tasmāt |*) Yajīkā is the name of a distant people; the region where they live is called Ārjika. So the commentator explains. Yāska, discussing rivers, says आर्जिकीयां विपाडित्याहुः (*ārjikīyāṃ vipāḍityāhuḥ*) (Ni. 9-26): Ārjikī is the name of a river, which has another name, Vipāṭ. Yajīka is a mountain, and the river that is born on it and flows is explained as named Ārjikā. It is well known that the Soma plants growing in the lands of the five peoples—Ārjīka, Pastāvat, Śaryaṇāvat, Suṣomā—are of the best kind and are obtained abundantly.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प॒र्जन्य॑वृद्धं महि॒षं तं सूर्य॑स्य दुहि॒ताभ॑रत् ।  
> तं गं॑ध॒र्वाः प्रत॑गृभ्य॒न्तं सोमे॒ रस॒माद॑धु॒रिंद्रा॑येंदो॒ ॑ 
> परि स्रव ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पर्जन्यवृद्धं । महिषं । तं । सूर्यस्य । दुहिता । आ : अभरत् ।  
> तं । गंधर्वाः । प्रति अग्नृभन् । तं । सोमे : रसं । आ । आदधुः । इंद्राय ।  
> इंदो इति । परि । स्रव ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सूर्यस्य दुहिता । श्रद्धा वै सूर्यस्य दुहितेति वाजसनेयकं । शत. १२-
> ७-३-११ । सा पर्जन्यवृद्धं पर्जन्यवत्समर्थं । यद्वा । पर्जन्येन तत्कार्यत्वेनोदकेन प्रवृद्धं । महिषं महांतं पूज्यं ना तं सोममाभरत् । आहरत् ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sūryasya duhitā is Śraddhā: श्रद्धा वै सूर्यस्य दुहितेति वाजसनेयकं (*śraddhā vai sūryasya duhiteti vājasaneyakaṃ*) (Śa. 12-7-3-11 **NOTE**). She brought that Soma, "grown by Parjanya", i.e. as capable as Parjanya; or else, grown by water, which is Parjanya's effect; *mahiṣa*, great and worthy of worship.

### Printed p. 150

> द्युलोकादाहृतवती । तमाह्रियमाणं सोमं गंधर्वा विश्वावसुप्रभृतयः
> प्रत्यगृभन् । प्रत्यगृह्णन् । प्रतिगृहीतवंतः ॥ हृग्रहोर्भः ॥ ततो गंधर्वाः प्रतिगृहीतं रसं सोमे प्रत्यक्षमाधधुः ॥ दधातेर्लजति ॥ तस्मान्परि स्रव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

She brought him from the heavenly world. The Gandharvas, Viśvāvasu and the rest, seized Soma as he was being brought; having seized him, the Gandharvas set the juice in the Soma openly. Therefore flow.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सूर्यस्य (*sūryasya*)—of Sūrya
- दुहिता (*duhitā*)—the daughter, Śraddhā
- पर्जन्यवृद्धं (*parjanyavṛddhaṃ*)—mighty like Parjanya
- महिषं (*mahiṣaṃ*)—worthy of worship
- तं (*taṃ*)—that Soma
- आभरत् (*ābharat*)—brought from the heavenly world
- गंधर्वाः (*gaṃdharvāḥ*)—the Gandharvas
- प्रत्यगृभन् (*pratyagṛbhan*)—received
- सोमे (*some*)—in that same Soma
- रसं (*rasaṃ*)—the juice
- आदधुः (*ādadhuḥ*)—placed
- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O Soma
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra's drinking
- परि स्रव (*pari srava*)—flow
**NOTE**

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Śraddhā, daughter of Sūrya, brought from the heavenly world Soma, mighty like Parjanya and worthy of worship. The Gandharvas received him and placed the juice in that same Soma. O Soma, flow for Indra's drinking.

##### English translation
The daughter of Surya brought the vast Soma large as a rain-cloud ; the Gandharvas seized upon it and placed the juice in the Soma : flow. Indu, for Indra.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Gandharvāḥ pratyagṛbhan—this ṛk says that the goddess Śraddhā, daughter of Sūrya, brought Soma from the heavenly world and that the Gandharvas received it. The matter is told slightly differently in other śākhās. For example in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā of the Yajurveda: तं सोममाह्रियमाणं गंधर्वो विश्वावसुः पर्यमुष्णात्स तिस्रो रात्रिः परिमुषितोऽवसत्तस्मात्रिस्रो रात्रिः क्रीतः सोमो वसति ते देवा अब्रुवन्स्कीकामा वै गंधर्वास्त्रिया निष्क्रीणामेति ते वाचग्स् स्त्रीयमेकहायनीं कृत्वा तया निरक्रीणन्ता रोहिद्रूपं कृत्वाःगंधर्वेभ्योऽपक्रम्यातिष्ठत् ॥ (*taṃ somamāhriyamāṇaṃ gaṃdharvo viśvāvasuḥ paryamuṣṇātsa tisro rātriḥ parimuṣito 'vasattasmātrisro rātriḥ krītaḥ somo vasati te devā abruvanskīkāmā vai gaṃdharvāstriyā niṣkrīṇāmeti te vācags strīyamekahāyanīṃ kṛtvā tayā nirakrīṇantā rohidrūpaṃ kṛtvāḥgaṃdharvebhyo 'pakramyātiṣṭhat ‖*) (Kṛ. Saṃ. 6-1-6--5)
**NOTE**

Formerly Soma was in heaven. When no one could bring it to earth, Gāyatrī went to heaven in the form of a hawk, disregarded the resistance of the guardians of Soma who watched it there—Svān, Bhrāja, Aṅghāri, Bambhāri, Hasta, Sudasta, Kṛśānu—and

### Printed p. 151
brought it to earth. While she was bringing it, a Gandharva named Viśvāvasu seized that Soma on the way and kept it with himself for three days. The gods considered how to get it back from him; since the Gandharvas are greatly attached to women and full of desire, they resolved to show them the prospect of a woman and so recover the Soma. For this they went to Vāc, the goddess of speech, told her everything, and with her consent decided that she should become a beautiful woman, be sold to the Gandharvas, and that Soma be brought in exchange. Accordingly, when Vāc took the form of a beautiful woman, they gave her to the Gandharvas and brought back the Soma they had carried off. When Soma had become the gods' possession, Vāc, who was in the form of the beautiful woman, took the form of a red antelope, escaped from the Gandharvas, ran and joined the gods. Therefore from that time it became the custom to buy Soma plants for use in sacrifices. This ritual part of the sacrifice, the purchase of Soma, is still performed in sacrifices. Moreover, since the best Soma plants grow in high mountain regions (the mountain Mūjavat is famous for the Soma plant), bringing it requires great toil. So it seems it became the custom to pay a fitting price to those who took the trouble, out of desire for wealth, to go to the mountains and bring Soma, and to buy it. Because Soma, when the Gandharva carried it off, stayed with him three nights, there is a rule that Soma bought must be kept three nights. In the present ṛk this kind of praise of the Soma plant is stated.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऋ॒तं वद॑न्नृतद्युम्न स॒त्यं वद॑न्त्सत्यकर्मन् ।  
> श्र॒द्धां वद॑न्सोम राजन्था॒त्रा सो॑म॒ परि॑ष्कृत॒ इंद्रा॑येंदो॒ परि॑ स्रव ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ऋतं । वदन् । ऋत्तद्युम्न । सत्यं । वदन् । सत्यकर्मस् !  
> श्रद्धां । पदन् ! सोम ! राजन् । धाता ' सोम । परिकृतः ! इंद्राय । इंदो  
> इति । परि । स्तव ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 152

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ऋतद्युम्नर्तेन द्योतमान सत्ययशस्व नासत्यकर्मन्यथार्थकर्मन् हे
> सोमाभिषूयमाण राजन् सर्वॆषां यज्ञनिष्पादकत्वेन स्वामिन् हे सोम
> ऋतं यज्ञं वदन् सत्यं वदन्नुचारयञ्चद्धां यजमानानामात्मनोपेक्षितां
> वदन् धात्रा कर्मणां धारकेण देवानां पोषकेण वा यजमानेन परिष्कृतोऽलंकृतस्त्वमिंद्राय परि स्रव ॥ संपर्युपेभ्य इति भूषणार्थे करोतेः सुट् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Ṛtadyumna, shining with ṛta, of true glory; O Satyakarman, of true action; O Soma being pressed; O king, lord of all as accomplisher of the sacrifice; O Soma, speaking ṛta, i.e. the sacrifice, uttering satya, speaking the faith that the sacrificers desire for themselves, adorned by the dhātṛ—the upholder of rites or the nourisher of the gods, i.e. the sacrificer—flow for Indra.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऋतद्युम्न (*ṛtadyumna*)—you who shine with truth
- सत्यकर्मन् (*satyakarman*)—you whose action is true
- सोम (*soma*)—O divine Soma
- राजन् (*rājan*)—lord of all
- सोम (*soma*)—O divine Soma
- यतं (*yataṃ*)—the sacrifice
- वदन् (*vadan*)—declaring
- सत्यं (*satyaṃ*)—truth
- वदन् (*vadan*)—reciting
- श्रद्धां (*śraddhāṃ*)—faith in the rite
- वदन् (*vadan*)—reciting
- धात्रा (*dhātrā*)—by the sacrificer, upholder of the rite
- परिष्कृतः (*pariṣkṛtaḥ*)—adorned
- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O Soma
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra's drinking
- परि स्रव (*pari srava*)—flow
**NOTE**

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Soma, shining with truth, of true action, lord of all, declaring the sacrifice, uttering truth, reciting faith in the rite, adorned by the sacrificer who upholds the rite, flow for Indra's drinking.

##### English translation
Soma, the utterer of truth, radiant with truth, truthspeaking, truthful in act, speaking faith, lord (of worshippers) you are decorated by the upholder (of the rite) : flow, Indu, for Indra.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒त्यमु॑ग्रस्य बृह॒तः सं स्र॑वंति संस्र॒वाः ।  
> सं यं॑ति र॒सिनो॒ रसाः॑ पुना॒नो ब्रह्म॑णा हर॒ इंद्रा॑येंदो॒ परि॑ स्रव ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 153

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सत्यं उग्रस्य । बृहतः । सं । स्रवंति । संस्रवाः ।  
> सं । यंति । रसिनः । रसाः । पुनानः । ब्रह्मणा । हरे । इंद्राय । इंदो इति ।  
> परि । स्रव ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सत्यमुग्रस्य संग्रामे सत्येन शत्रूणामुद्गारयितुः । यद्वा । उद्धर्णसत्यस्य । यद्वा । यथार्थभूतमुद्दूर्णं बलं यस्य तस्य ॥ पृषोदरादित्वाद्रूपसिद्धिः ॥ बृहतो महतः सोमस्य तव संस्रवाः सम्यक्स्रवणशीला धाराः सं
> स्रवंति । सम्यग्गच्छंति । किंच रसिनो रसवतस्तव स्वभूता रसाः सं यंति। संगच्छंते।
> तस्माद्दे हरे हरितवर्ण सोम ब्रह्मणा ब्राह्मणेन मंत्रेण वा पुनानः पूयमानः स त्वमिंद्रार्थं परि स्रव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa explains सत्यमुग्रस्य (*satyamugrasya*) as "of him who by truth makes his enemies vomit up (their strength) in battle"; or "of him whose truth is lofty"; or "of him whose strength is real and lofty". The form is accounted for by the rule of पृषोदरादि (*pṛṣodarādi*). The streams (संस्रवाः (*saṃsravāḥ*)), whose nature is to flow together, of you, the great Soma, flow together, that is, go together properly. Further, your own juices, the juices of you the juicy one, meet together. Therefore, O green-hued Soma, being purified by the Brāhmaṇa or by a mantra, flow for Indra.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सत्यमुग्रस्य (*satyamugrasya*)—of him who truly has a manifesting strength
- बृहतः (*bṛhataḥ*)—of the great Soma
- संस्रवाः (*saṃsravāḥ*)—flowing streams
- सं स्रवंति (*saṃ sravaṃti*)—flow
- रसिनः (*rasinaḥ*)—of the juicy Soma
- रसाः (*rasāḥ*)—juices
- सं यंति (*saṃ yaṃti*)—flow together
- हरे (*hare*)—green-hued
- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O Soma
- ब्रह्मणा (*brahmaṇā*)—by the mantra
- पुनानः (*punānaḥ*)—you who are purified
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra's drinking
- परि स्रव (*pari srava*)—flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The streams of Soma, who shines with true valour and is great, flow. The juices of the juicy Soma flow together. O green-hued Soma, purified by the mantra or by the Brāhmaṇa, flow for Indra's drinking.

##### English translation
The united sereams of you who are vast and truly formidable flow united ; the juices of you the juicy one meet together ; green-tinted (Soma) purified by holy prayer, Indu, flow for Indra.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यॆत्र॑ ब्र॒ह्मा प॑वमान छंद॒स्यां॒३॒॑ ३ वाचं॒ वद॑न् ।  
> ग्रावा॒ सोमे॑ मही॒यते॒ सोमे॑नानं॒दं ज॒नय॒न्निंद्रा॑येंदो॒ परि॑ स्रव ॥ ६ ॥

### Printed p. 154

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत्र । ब्रह्मा । प्रवमान । छंदस्यां । वाचं । वदन् ।  
> ग्रावा । सोमे । महीयते । सोमेन । आनंदं । जनयन् : इंद्राय ।  
> इंदो इति । परि । स्रव ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे पवमान पूयमान सोम त्वदर्थं छंदस्यां सप्तछंदोभिः कृतां तेषु
> भवां वाचं स्तुतिं वदन्नुच्चारयन् सोमे सोमाभिषवार्थं ग्राव्णाभिषवं
> कुर्वता युक्तस्तेनाभिषुतेन सोमेन देवानामानंदं संतोषं जनयन्नुत्पादयन्न ब्राह्मणो यत्र यस्मिन्देशे महीयते देवैः पूज्यते तत्र हे
> सोम त्वं परि स्रव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O purified Soma, for you the Brāhmaṇa (ब्रह्मा (*brahmā*)) utters a speech (वाचं (*vācaṃ*)), a praise, composed in the seven metres and consisting of them. He is engaged in pressing Soma with the pressing-stone, and by the pressed Soma he produces delight, contentment, for the gods. In whatever region he is honoured by the gods, there, O Soma, flow.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पवमान (*pavamāna*)—O Soma, who are purified
- छंदस्यां (*chaṃdasyāṃ*)—composed of the seven metres
- वाचं (*vācaṃ*)—the praise-speech
- वदन् (*vadan*)—reciting
- सोमे (*some*)—for the obtaining of Soma
- ग्राव्णा (*grāvṇā*)—with the pressing-stones, pressed
- सोमेन (*somena*)—with the Soma juice
- आनंदं (*ānaṃdaṃ*)—delight to the gods
- जनयन् (*janayan*)—producing
- ब्रह्मा (*brahmā*)—the Brāhmaṇa
- यत्र (*yatra*)—in whatever region
- महीयते (*mahīyate*)—is honoured, in that place
- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O Soma
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra's drinking
- परि स्रव (*pari srava*)—flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Soma-deva, who are purified: in whatever region the Brāhmaṇa is honoured who recites praise-speech composed in the seven metres and with Soma pressed by the pressing-stones for the obtaining of Soma produces delight for the gods, in that place flow for Indra's drinking.

##### English translation
Wherever the Brahman, O purified ( Soma ), reciting the holy rhythmical text, engendering the delight (of the gods) by the Soma (expressed) with the grinding-stone, is reverenced, flow, Indu, for Indra.

### Printed p. 155

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यत्र॒ ज्योति॒रज॑स्रं॒ यस्मि॑ल्लो॒के स्व॑र्हि॒तं ।  
> तस्मि॒न्मां धे॑हि पवमाना॒मृते॑ लो॒के अक्षि॑त॒ इंद्रा॑येंदो॒ ॑ 
> परि स्रव ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत्र । ज्योतिः । अजस्रं । यस्मिन् । लोके । स्वः । हितं ।  
> तस्मिन् । मां । धेहि । पवमान । अमृते । लोके । अक्षिते । इंद्राय । इंदोइति । हरि । स्तव ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे पवमान यत्र यस्मिल्लोके ज्योतिः सर्वं तेजोऽजस्रं सर्वदाविनश्वरं वर्तते । यस्मिंश्च लोके स्वरादित्याख्यं ज्योतिर्हितं निहितमस्ति । तस्मिन्न
> मृते मरणधर्मरहिते आत एवाक्षितेऽक्षीणे लोके मां सोमाभिषवं कुर्वंतं
> धेहि । निधेहि । तस्मान्मामुत्तमलोकं प्रापयितुं त्वमिंद्राय परि स्रव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Pavamāna, in the world where all light is unceasing and imperishable, and in which the light called the sun is placed, in that immortal, hence undecaying world, place me who am pressing Soma. To bring me to that highest world, flow for Indra.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पवमान (*pavamāna*)—O Soma-deva
- यत्र (*yatra*)—in which world
- ज्योतिः (*jyotiḥ*)—all radiance
- अजस्रं (*ajasraṃ*)—is eternal
- यस्मिन् लोके (*yasmin loke*)—in which world
- स्वः (*svaḥ*)—the radiance of the sun
- हितं (*hitaṃ*)—is established
- तस्मिन् (*tasmin*)—in that
- अमृते (*amṛte*)—deathless
- अक्षिते (*akṣite*)—and undecaying
- लोके (*loke*)—world
- मां (*māṃ*)—me
- धेहि (*dhehi*)—place
- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O Soma
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra's drinking
- परि स्रव (*pari srava*)—flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Soma-deva, in the world where all radiance is eternal, in which the sun's radiance is established, in that deathless and undecaying world place me. O Soma, flow for Indra's drinking.

##### English translation
Where light is perpetual, in the world in which the sun is placed, in that immortal imperishable world place me, Pavamana; flow, Indu, for Indra.

### Printed p. 156

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यत्र॒ राजा॑ वैवस्व॒तो यत्रा॑व॒रोध॑नं दि॒वः ।  
> यत्रा॒मूर्य॒ह्वती॒राप॒स्तत्र॒ माम॒मृतं॑ कृ॒धींद्रा॑येंदो॒ ॑ 
> परि स्रव ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत्र । राजा । वैवस्वतः । यत्र । अव्यरोधनं । दिवः ।  
> यत्र । अमूः । यह्वतीः । अपः । तत्र । मां । अमृतं । कृधि । इंद्राय । इंदो  
> इति । परि । स्वव ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यत्र यस्मिल्लोके वैवस्वतो विवस्वत्पुत्रो राजा भवति । यत्र च लोके
> दिव आदित्यस्यावरोधनं भूतानां प्रवेशनं । किंच यत्र लोके यह्वतीर्महत्योमूरिमा गंगाद्या अपस्तिष्ठंति । तत्र तादृशे लोके मामृतं मरणधर्मरहितं कृधि ! कुरु । मम देवत्वं प्रापयेत्यर्थः । ततः कारणात्परि स्रव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the world where Vaivasvata, the son of Vivasvat, is king; where there is the अवरोधनं (*avarodhanaṃ*) of the sun, the entering of beings; and where those great waters, the Gaṅgā and others, abide: in such a world make me immortal, free from the nature of death; that is, bring me to godhood. For that reason flow.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O Soma-deva
- यत्र (*yatra*)—in which world
- वैवस्वतः (*vaivasvataḥ*)—the son of Vivasvat
- राजा (*rājā*)—is lord
- यत्र (*yatra*)—in which world
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—of the sun
- अवरोधनं (*avarodhanaṃ*)—there is inner entry
- यत्र (*yatra*)—in which world
- यह्वतीः (*yahvatīḥ*)—of great nature
- अमूः आपः (*amūḥ āpaḥ*)—the purified waters of the Gaṅgā and other rivers are
- तत्र (*tatra*)—in such a world
- मां (*māṃ*)—me
- अमृतं (*amṛtaṃ*)—as deathless
- कृधि (*kṛdhi*)—make (establish)
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra's drinking
- परि स्रव (*pari srava*)—flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Soma-deva, in the world where the son of Vivasvat is lord, where the sun has inner entry, where the purified waters of the Gaṅgā and other rivers, of great nature, are, in such a world make me deathless. Flow for Indra's drinking.

### Printed p. 157

##### English translation
Where Vivaswat's son is king, where the inner chamber of the sun (is), where these great waters (are). there make me immortal; flow, Indu, for Indra.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

यत्र राजा वैवस्वतः (*yatra rājā vaivasvataḥ*)—The king who is the son of the well-known deity named Vivasvān is called Vaivasvata. It is customary also to call him Yama. Explanations concerning him are given at length in the later sūktas of this same part (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-10 etc.).

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यत्रा॑नुका॒मं चर॑णं त्रिना॒के त्रि॑दि॒वे दि॒वः ।  
> लो॒का यत्र॒ ज्योति॑ष्मंत॒स्तत्र॒ माम॒मृतं॑ कृ॒धींद्रा॑येंदो॒ ॑ 
> परि स्रव ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत्र । अनुकामं । चरणं । त्रिनाके । त्रिदिने । दिवः ।  
> लोकाः । यत्र । ज्योतिष्मंतः । तत्र । मां । अमृतं । कृधि । इंद्राय ; इंदो  
> इति । परि । स्त्रव ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यत्र त्रिदिवे तृतीयस्यां दिवि द्युलोके त्रिनाके । तत्राधरमध्यमोत्तमभावेन त्रीणि स्थानानि संति । तत्र तृतीये नाक उत्तमे स्थाने दिव आदित्यस्यानुकामं कामानुगुणं चरणमस्ति । किंच यत्र यस्मिल्लोके लोका ज्योतिष्मंतो
> ज्योतिर्युक्तास्तिष्ठंति । तत्र तादृरे लोके मामृतं कृधि । यथा ममोत्तमलोको भवति तथा त्वमिंद्राय परि स्रव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the third heaven (त्रिदिवे (*tridive*)), the heavenly world, the त्रिनाके (*trināke*), there are three places, lower, middle and highest. In the third, the highest place (नाके (*nāke*)), the sun has movement as he wishes (अनुकामं (*anukāmaṃ*)). Further, in whatever world the worlds stand endowed with light, in such a world make me immortal. That I may get the highest world, flow for Indra.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Soma-deva) त्रिनाके (*trināke*)—which has three places, lower, middle and highest
- यत्र (*yatra*)—in which
- त्रिदिवे (*tridive*)—in the third heavenly world
- दिनः (*dinaḥ*)—of the sun
- अनुकामं (*anukāmaṃ*)—independent **NOTE**

### Printed p. 158
- चरणं (*caraṇaṃ*)—movement is
- यत्र (*yatra*)—where
- लोकाः (*lokāḥ*)—all worlds
- ज्योतिष्मंतः (*jyotiṣmaṃtaḥ*)—are full of radiance
- तत्र (*tatra*)—in such a highest world
- मां (*māṃ*)—me
- अमृतं (*amṛtaṃ*)—deathless
- कृधि (*kṛdhi*)—make and establish
- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O Soma
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra's drinking
- परि स्रव (*pari srava*)—flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Soma-deva, in that third heavenly world having three divisions, lower, middle and highest, where the sun has independent movement, where all worlds are full of radiance, in such a highest world make me deathless and establish me. O Soma, flow for Indra's drinking.

##### English translation
Where in the third heaven, in the third sphere, the sun wanders at will, where the regions are filled with light, there make me immortal: flow, Indu, for Indra.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यत्र॒ कामा॑ निका॒माश्च॒ यत्र॑ ब्र॒ध्नस्य॑ वि॒ष्टपं॑ ।  
> स्व॒धा च॒ यत्र॒ तृप्ति॑श्च॒ तत्र॒ माम॒मृतं॑ कृ॒धींद्रा॑येंदो॒ ॑ 
> परि स्रव ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत्र । कामाः । निकामाः । च । यत्र । ब्रध्नस्य । विष्टपं ।  
> स्वधा । च । यत्र । तृप्तिः । च । तत्र । मां । अमृतं । कृधि । इंद्राय । इंदो  
> इति । परि । स्तव ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यत्र यस्मिल्लोके कामाः काव्यमाना देवा निकामा नितरामवश्यं
> प्रार्थ्यमाना इंद्रादयश्च विद्यंते । .... .... .... .... । यद्वा ! सूर्येण विना
> कर्माणि न घटंत इति सर्वेषां कर्मणां मूलभूतस्यादित्यस्य पिष्टपं सहस्थानं

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In whatever world there are कामाः (*kāmāḥ*), the gods who are desired, and निकामाः (*nikāmāḥ*), Indra and the others who are earnestly and necessarily entreated. (The passage is abridged in print.) Or else: since actions do not come about without the sun, the पिष्टपं (*piṣṭapaṃ*), the common abode, of the sun, who is the root of all actions …. **NOTE**

### Printed p. 159

> यत्र विद्यते तत्र लोके। स्वधान्नं स्वधाकारेण वा दत्तमन्नं तृप्तिस्तर्पणं हर्षणं
> हर्षश्च यत्र विद्यते तत्र लोके माममृतं कृधि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa's block, which completes the previous verse's commentary, says: in the world where there is svadhā-food, that is, food given with the cry svadhā, and satisfaction, refreshment and joy, in that world make me immortal.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत्र (*yatra*)—in which world
- कामाः (*kāmāḥ*)—desires
- निकामाः च (*nikāmāḥ ca*)—and excellent aims, are found
- यत्र (*yatra*)—in which world
- ब्रध्न स्य (*bradhna sya*)—of the Āditya, the impeller of rites
- विष्टपं च (*viṣṭapaṃ ca*)—and the abode is
- यत्र (*yatra*)—where
- स्वधा (*svadhā*)—food
- तृप्तिः च (*tṛptiḥ ca*)—and satisfaction are found
- तत्र (*tatra*)—there
- मां (*māṃ*)—me
- अमृतं (*amṛtaṃ*)—free from death
- कृधि (*kṛdhi*)—make and establish
- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O Soma
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra's drinking
- परि स्रव (*pari srava*)—flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In the world where there are desires and excellent aims, in the world where the abode of the Āditya, the impeller of rites, is, where there are food and satisfaction, there make me free from death and establish me. O Soma, flow for Indra's drinking.

##### English translation
Where wishes and desires (are), where the region of the sun (is), where food and delight (are) found, there make me immortal ; flow, Indu, for Indra.

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यत्रा॑नं॒दाश्च॒ मोदा॑श्च॒ मुदः॑ प्र॒मुद॒ आस॑ते ।  
> काम॑स्य॒ यात्रा॒स्ताः कामा॒स्तत्र॒ माम॒मृतं॑ कृ॒धींद्रा॑येंदो॒ परि॑ स्रव ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत्र । आनंदाः । च । मोदाः । च । मुदः । प्रमुदः । आसते ।  
> कामस्य । यत्र । अप्राः । कामाः । तत्र । मां । अमृतु । कृधि । इंद्राय ।  
> इंदो इति । परि । स्रव ॥ ११ ॥

### Printed p. 160

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यत्र यस्मिल्लोक आनंदादय आसते । तेषामल्पो भेदो द्रष्ट व्यः
> यत्र च लोके कामस्य काम्यमानस्य देवस्य सर्वे कामा आप्ताः प्राप्ता भवंति तत्र'
> माममृतं कृधि । एतत्तु त्वया विना न घटत इति हे सोम त्वं परि स्रव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the world where bliss and the rest abide (a slight difference among them is to be noted), and where all the desires of the god who is desired are fulfilled, there make me immortal. This cannot happen without you; therefore, O Soma, flow.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत्र (*yatra*)—in which world
- आनंदाः (*ānaṃdāḥ*)—the supreme bliss of Brahman
- मोदाः (*modāḥ*)—joys
- मुदः (*mudaḥ*)—means of happiness
- प्रमुदः (*pramudaḥ*)—experiences of happiness
- आसते (*āsate*)—are found
- यत्र (*yatra*)—where
- कामस्य (*kāmasya*)—of one who seeks happiness
- आप्ताः (*āptāḥ*)—desired
- कामाः (*kāmāḥ*)—wishes are fulfilled
- तत्र (*tatra*)—there
- मां (*māṃ*)—me
- अमृतं (*amṛtaṃ*)—free from death
- कृधि (*kṛdhi*)—make and establish
- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O Soma
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra's drinking
- परि स्रव (*pari srava*)—flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In the world where there are supreme bliss of Brahman, joys, means of happiness and experiences of happiness, where the wishes of one who seeks happiness are fulfilled so as to satisfy his desire, there make me free from death and establish me. O Soma, flow for Indra's drinking.

##### English translation
Where there is happiness, pleasures, joy and enjoyment, where the wishes of the wisher are obtained, there make me immortal; flow, Indu, for Indra.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Ānandāḥ, modāḥ, mudaḥ, pramudaḥ: all these words denote one kind of meaning, bliss. Though there are slight differences, the use of these words denoting one meaning is seen in many Śruti sentences such as मोदः प्रमोद आनंदः (*modaḥ pramoda ānaṃdaḥ*) and the like.

## Sūkta 114 (Maṇḍala 9)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: the sūkta य इंदोः (*ya iṃdoḥ*) has eleven (the printed text reads *ekāvaśaṃ*) ... it has four Ṛks; its first (seer, deity, metre) are as in the previous sūkta. The Anukramaṇī says य इंदोश्च तुष्कमिति (*ya iṃdośca tuṣkamiti*). The use of the sūkta has been stated.**NOTE**

### Printed p. 161

##### Anuvāda

The sūkta य इंदोः (*ya iṃdoḥ*) is the eleventh sūkta in the seventh anuvāka. It has four Ṛks. This sūkta has the same seer, deity and metre as the preceding sūkta. In the Anukramaṇī it is said: य इंदोश्चतुष्कं (*ya iṃdoścatuṣkaṃ*). The use of the sūkta was stated earlier.

Sūkta—114
Maṇḍala—9; Anuvāka—7; Sūkta—114
Aṣṭaka—7; Adhyāya—5; Varga—28
Number of Ṛks in the sūkta—4
Seer—Kaśyapa
Deity—Pavamāna Soma
Metre—Paṅkti

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> य इंदोः॒ पव॑मान॒स्यानु॒ धामा॒न्यक्र॑मीत् ।  
> तमा॑हुः सुप्र॒जा इति॒ यस्ते॑ सो॒मावि॑ध॒न्मन॒ इंद्रा॑येंदोः॒ ॑ 
> परि स्रव ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यः । इंदोः । पवमानस्य । अनु । धामानि । अक्रमीत् ।  
> तं । आहुः । सुप्रजाः । इति । यः । ते । सोम । अविधत् । मनः । इंद्राय ।  
> इंदो इति । परि । स्रव ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> पवमानस्य पूयमानस्येंदोः सोमस्येंदोः सोमस्य धामानि
> स्थानानि द्रोणकलशादीनि यद्वा धामानि तेजांसि यो ब्राह्मणोऽस्त्वक्रमीत् अनुक्रामति अनुगच्छति तं जनं सुप्रजाः शोभनप्रजननः कल्याणपुत्रादिप्रजायुक्त इत्याहुः ॥ नित्यमसिच् प्रजामेधयोरित्यसिच् समासांतः ॥ यः सोममभिषुणोति स पुत्रादियुक्तो भवतीत्यर्थः । किंच हे सोम यो मनुष्यस्ते
> त्वदर्थं मनोऽविधत् करोति । यद्वा ! ते त्वदीयं मनोऽविधत् परिचरति ।
> विधतिः परिचरणकर्मा ! तमपि कल्याण जननमित्याहुः । तस्मात्त्वं परि स्रव ॥
> xxv xxvii 11

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Of the purifying Soma, whose abodes are the Droṇa-vessel and the like (or else "abodes" means splendours), the Brāhmaṇa who follows them is called सुप्रजाः (*suprajāḥ*), blessed with fine offspring, sons and the like; that is, whoever presses Soma becomes endowed with sons and the rest. Further, O Soma, the man who directs his mind to you (or else: attends upon your mind, विधतिः (*vidhatiḥ*) being a root of service) is also called blessed with fine offspring. Therefore flow.

### Printed p. 162

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हनमानस्य (*hanamānasya*)—of the one who attains purity**NOTE**
- इंदोः (*iṃdoḥ*)—of Soma
- धामानि (*dhāmāni*)—the abodes
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Brāhmaṇa
- अनु अक्रमीत् (*anu akramīt*)—attends upon
- तं (*taṃ*)—that Brāhmaṇa
- सुप्रजाः (*suprajāḥ*)—as one with excellent sons and the like
- आहुः (*āhuḥ*)—people call
- सोम (*soma*)—O Soma
- यः (*yaḥ*)—who
- निन्न सेवॆगागि (*ninna sevègāgi*)—for your service
- मनः (*manaḥ*)—the mind
- अविधत् (*avidhat*)—resolves
- इति (*iti*)—him too they call blessed with good sons

- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for Indra's drinking
- परि स्रव (*pari srava*)—flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Brāhmaṇa who attends upon the abodes of the purified Soma, people call blessed with excellent sons and the like. O Soma, he who resolves his mind for your service they also call blessed with good sons. O Soma, flow for Indra's drinking.

##### English translation
(The Brahman) who attends to the stations of the filtered Soma-juice—him men call rich in children, who applies his mind to you, Soma ; flow, Indu, for Indra.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऋषे॑ मंत्र॒कृतां॒ स्तोमैः॒ कश्य॑पोद्व॒र्धय॒न्गिरः॑ ।  
> सोमं॑ नमस्य॒ राजा॑नं॒ यो ज॒ज्ञे वी॒रुधां॒ पति॒रिंद्रा॑येंदो॒ परि॑ स्रव ॥ २ ॥

> सोमं । नमस्य । राजानं । यः । जज्ञे । वीरुधां । पतिः । इंद्राय । इंदो  
> इति । परि । स्रव ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऋषिः स्मात्मानं प्रत्याह । हे ऋषे सूक्तद्रष्टर्हे कश्यपात्मन् त्वं मंत्रकृतामृषीणां स्तोमैः स्तोत्रैर्गिरः स्तुतिरूपा वाच उद्धर्धयन्नु पर्युपरि वर्ध-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The seer addresses himself: O seer, Kaśyapa, seer of the sūkta, increasing speech in the form of praise with the hymns (स्तोमैः (*stomaiḥ*)) of the mantra-making seers, ... (continues on the next page).

### Printed p. 163

> यन्प्राजानं सर्वेषां स्वामिनं तं सोमं नमस्य । पूजय ॥ नमसः पूजायां
> नमोवरिव इति क्यच् ॥ यः सोमो वीरुधां वनस्पतीनां पतिः पालको जज्ञे
> जातः तं नमस्य ॥ हे सोम यथात्मना सुतो भवसि तथा परि स्रव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

... worship that Soma, king and lord of all (नमस्य (*namasya*), from नमसः पूजायां (*namasaḥ pūjāyāṃ*), with kyac). The Soma who was born as lord and protector of the plants (वीरुधां (*vīrudhāṃ*)), the trees, worship him. O Soma, flow as you are pressed.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)
