May he, who as soon as manifested is vast and wise, and thus (universally) pervading, defend (me), his third (manifestation); and when (the worshippers) ask with their mouths for his own water, animated by one purpose, they praise him in this world.

### Printed p. 204

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

तृतीयं अभि पाति (*tṛtīyaṃ abhi pāti*)—तृतीयं त्रयाणां पूरकं मां अभिरक्षतु (*tṛtīyaṃ trayāṇāṃ pūrakaṃ māṃ abhirakṣatu*). It was said earlier that Ekata, Dvita and Trita are three brothers, that Trita is the last of them, and that he is the seer of this sūkta. Therefore the prayer is made here that Agni protect me, the third (the last) among the brothers.

आसा यत् अस्य पयः अक्रत स्वं (*āsā yat asya payaḥ akrata svaṃ*)—The intention is that the priests who chant praises beg this Agni for rain-water with their mouths, that is, with the hymns and the like that they recite with their mouths.

सचेतसः अभ्यर्चंति (*sacetasaḥ abhyarcaṃti*)—समानमनस्का भूत्त्वैनं अधिष्टुवंति (*samānamanaskā bhūttvainaṃ adhiṣṭuvaṃti*). These priests, being of one mind, that is, with one and the same desire that rain-water should come to them, praise Agni.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आत॑ उ त्वा सितु॒भृतो॒ जनि॑त्रीरन्ना॒वृधं॒ प्रति॑ चरं॒त्यन्नैः॑ ।  
> ता ईं॒ प्रत्ये॑षि॒ पुन॑र॒न्यरू॑पा॒ असि॒ त्वं वि॒क्षु मानु॑षीषु॒  ॑
> होता ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आतः । ऊं इति ; त्वा । पितुभृतः । जनित्रीः । अन्नवृधं । प्रति । चरंति । अन्नैः ।  
> ताः । ईं । प्रति । एषि । पुनः ! अन्यरूपाः । असि । त्वं । विक्षु । मानुषीषु  
> होता ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने अतः । उ इत्यवधारणे । अस्मादेव कारणात् पितुभृतः पितुनान्नेन
> सर्वस्य जतो धारयित्र्यः पोषयित्र्यो वा जनित्रीर्जनयित्र्य ओषधयोऽन्नावृधमन्नस्य वर्धकं त्वा त्वामन्नॆर्हेतुभिः प्रति चरंति । परिचरंति । अभिगच्छंति । ततस्तमीमेनास्ता ओषधीः प्रत्येषि । प्रतिगच्छसि । पुनरप्यन्यरूपा जीर्णा ओषधीर्दावभूतस्त्वं गच्छसि । किंच मानुषीषु मनुष्यजातिषु विक्षु प्रजासु । यद्वा । विश्वग्नि-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, अतः (*ataḥ*); उ (*u*) is in the sense of emphasis. For this very reason the herbs, which are पितुभृतः (*pitubhṛtaḥ*), that is, supporters or nourishers of all with food (पितु (*pitu*) being food), and जनित्रीः (*janitrīḥ*), producers, attend you who increase food, *annairhetubhiḥ*, by way of foods. Then you go back to those same herbs. Again you go, having other forms, as the fire that consumes the withered herbs. Further, among the human clans, the विक्षु (*vikṣu*) (peoples); or else among the human races that engage in actions such as Agnihotra, as

### Printed p. 205

> होत्रादिक्रियास्वभिनिविशंतीषु मानुषीषु मनुष्यजातिषु । होता देवानामाह्वाता होमनिष्पादको वासि । भवसि । मनुष्येषु स्थित इत्यर्थे ब्राह्मणं देवो
> ह्येष मर्त्येष्टिति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

...you are the Hotṛ, the invoker of the gods, or the accomplisher of the oblation. The meaning is that he abides among men. For this the Brāhmaṇa is cited: देवो ह्येष मर्त्येष्टिति (*devo hyeṣa martyeṣṭiti*). **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अतः (*ataḥ*)—therefore (O Agni)
- पितुभृतः (*pitubhṛtaḥ*)—those that nourish the whole world with food
- जनित्रीः (*janitrīḥ*)—and are the producers of fire, the herbs
- अन्नावृधं (*annāvṛdhaṃ*)—the increaser of food
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- अन्नैः (*annaiḥ*)—for the sake of food
- प्रति चरंति (*prati caraṃti*)—serve
- अन्यरूपाः (*anyarūpāḥ*)—having taken other forms
- ईं ताः (*īṃ tāḥ*)—those same herbs
- प्रत्येषि (*pratyeṣi*)—you go again and join
- मानुषीषु (*mānuṣīṣu*)—among the human
- विक्षु (*vikṣu*)—peoples
- होता असि (*hotā asi*)—you are of the form of the Hotṛ

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, the herbs, which nourish the whole world with food and are the producers of fire, serve you, the increaser of food, for the sake of food. You go again and join those same herbs after they have taken other forms. Among the peoples belonging to men you are of the form of the Hotṛ.

##### English translation
Therefore the genetrices (of all things, the herbs), the cherishers (of all) with food, wait on you who are the augmenter of food, with (sactificial) viands; you visit them again, when they have assumed other forms, you are amongst human beings the invoker (of the gods).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

अन्यरूपाः (*anyarūpāḥ*)—There are two kinds of herbs. The growing forest trees serve Agni for the sake of food; that is, they attend upon Agni to obtain rain-water, which is of the nature of food for them. This is one meaning. The second meaning is that you, Agni, burn the dried wood and the like among the herbs and grow strong. Thus two kinds of meaning are suggested here.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> होता॑रं चि॒त्रर॑थमध्व॒रस्य॑ य॒ज्ञस्य॑यज्ञस्य के॒तुं रुशं॑तं ।  
> प्रत्य॑र्धि० दे॒वस्य॑देवस्य म॒हा श्रि॒या त्व१॒॑ग्निमति॑थिं॒ जना॑नां ॥

### Printed p. 206

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> होतारं । चित्ररथं । अध्वरस्य । यज्ञस्ययज्ञस्य । केतुं । रुशंतं ।  
> प्रतिऽअर्धिं । देवस्यदेवस्य । मह्ना । श्रिया । तु । अग्निं । अतिथिं । जनानां ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आतिथ्यायां स्विष्टकृतोऽनुवाक्या होतारमित्येषा । सूत्रितं च । होतारं
> चित्ररथमध्वरस्य प्रप्रायमग्निर्भरतस्य शृण्व इति संयाज्ये । आ. ४-५ । इति ॥
> अध्वरस्य यज्ञस्य होतारमेतन्नामानमृत्विजं यद्वा होतारं देवानामाह्वातारं चित्ररथं नानारूपरथमध्वरस्य रक्षोभिरहिंसितस्य यज्ञस्य सर्वस्य
> यज्ञस्य केतुं प्रज्ञापकं पताकास्थानीयं वा रुशंतं श्वेतवर्णं मह्ना स्वमहत्वेन देवस्य देवस्य सर्वॆषां देवानामर्धिमिंद्रं प्रति भवंतं । यत्सर्वेषामर्धिमिंद्रं प्रतीति ब्राह्मणं । तादृशं जनानां यजमानानामतिधिवत्तूज्यमग्निं तु क्षिप्रं श्रिया श्रियोर्थं वयं स्तुमः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the Ātithyā (iṣṭi) this Ṛk, होतारं (*hotāraṃ*), is the anuvākyā of the Sviṣṭakṛt. It is laid down in the sūtra (Ā. 4-5), which ends with the words प्रप्रायमग्निर्भरतस्य शृण्व इति संयाज्ये (*praprāyamagnirbharatasya śṛṇva iti saṃyājye*). होतारं (*hotāraṃ*) is the priest of this name, of the sacrifice (अध्वर (*adhvara*)). Or the Hotṛ is the invoker of the gods. चित्ररथं (*citrarathaṃ*) is he who has a chariot of many forms. अध्वर (*adhvara*) is the sacrifice unharmed by the Rakṣas. यज्ञस्य यज्ञस्य (*yajñasya yajñasya*) means of every sacrifice. केतुं (*ketuṃ*) is the indicator, or what stands in the place of a banner. रुशंतं (*ruśaṃtaṃ*) is white in colour. मह्ना (*mahnā*) is by his own greatness. He is *ardhimiṃdraṃ prati bhavaṃtaṃ*, surpassing of all the gods; the Brāhmaṇa says यत्सर्वेषामर्धिमिंद्रं प्रतीति ब्राह्मणं (*yatsarveṣāmardhimiṃdraṃ pratīti brāhmaṇaṃ*). Such is the Agni, to be honoured like a guest by the sacrificers (जनानां (*janānāṃ*)); we praise him quickly, for the sake of prosperity (श्रिया (*śriyā*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अध्वरस्य (*adhvarasya*)—for the sacrifice
- होतारं (*hotāraṃ*)—of the form of the Hotṛ
- चित्ररथं (*citrarathaṃ*)—having a chariot of many forms
- यज्ञस्ययज्ञस्य (*yajñasyayajñasya*)—for all sacrifices
- केतुं (*ketuṃ*)—the indicator
- रुशंतं (*ruśaṃtaṃ*)—of white colour
- मह्ना (*mahnā*)—by his own greatness
- देवस्यदेवस्य (*devasyadevasya*)—to all the gods
- प्रति अर्धिं (*prati ardhiṃ*)—of surpassing might
- जनानां (*janānāṃ*)—for the sacrificers
- अतिथिं (*atithiṃ*)—worthy of honour
- अग्निं (*agniṃ*)—Agni
- तु (*tu*)—quickly
- श्रिया (*śriyā*)—for the sake of wealth we praise

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

We quickly praise, for the sake of wealth, Agni, who is the Hotṛ for the sacrifice, has a chariot of many forms, is the indicator of the sacrifice, is of white colour, has by his own greatness a might surpassing that of all the gods, and is worthy of honour for the sacrificers.

##### English translation
(We worship) Agni for prosperity, you, who are the invoker of the gods, the many-coloured conveyance of the sacrifice, the brilliant banner of every offering, the surpasser of every other deity in might, the guest of men.

### Printed p. 207

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

When the Sviṣṭakṛt offering is made in the Ātithya iṣṭi, this Ṛk beginning होतारं (*hotāraṃ*) is to be recited as the anuvākyā mantra. This is set out in the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra by the sūtra होतारं चित्ररथमध्वरस्य प्रप्रायमग्निर्भरतस्य शृण्व इति संयाज्ये (*hotāraṃ citrarathamadhvarasya praprāyamagnirbharatasya śṛṇva iti saṃyājye*) (Ā. 4-5).

यज्ञस्य यज्ञस्य, देवस्यदेवस्य (*yajñasya yajñasya, devasyadevasya*)—Such repeated words (words uttered twice) carry the meaning "each" or "all". The words above mean for every sacrifice or for all sacrifices, and for all the gods. Many other examples can be given, like दिवेदिने (*divedine*) and अहरहः (*aharahaḥ*).

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स तु वस्त्रा॒ण्यध॒ पेश॑नानि॒ वसा॑नोः अ॒ग्निर्नाभा॑ पृथि॒व्याः ।  
> अ॒रु॒षो जा॒तः प॒द इळा॑याः पु॒रोहि॑तो राजन्यक्षी॒ह  ॒
> देवान् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । तु । वस्त्राणि । अध । पेशनानि । वसानः । अग्निः । नाभा । पृथिव्याः ।  
> अरुषः । जातः । पदे । इळायाः । पुरःसहितः । राजन् । यक्षि । इह । देवान् ॥६॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे राजन्वीप्यमानाग्ने वस्त्राण्याच्छादकानि तेजांसि अधापि च पेशनानि
> तेषां तेजसां कार्षण्यशौक्ल्यादीनि रूपाणि । यद्वा । अधानंतरं पेशनानि ।
> पेश इति हिरण्यनाम । हिरण्यवद्धिरण्यसदृशानि भास्वराणि तेजांसि । वसान आच्छादयन्
> पृथिव्या नाभा नाभौ नाभिस्थानीय इळायास्पद उत्तरवेद्यां । एतद्वा इळायास्पदं
> यदुत्तरवेदीनाभिः ऐ. ब्रा. १-२८ । इत्याम्नानात् । तस्यां जातः समुत्पन्नोऽरुष
> आरोचमानो योग्निरेवंविधोऽसि स त्वं पुरोहितः पुरतो निहितः पूर्वस्यां
> दिश्याहवनीयायतने स्थापितः सन्निहास्मिन्यज्ञे देवानिंद्रादीन्यक्षि । यज । पूजय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O king, blazing Agni, वस्त्राणि (*vastrāṇi*) are the covering lights, and अध (*adha*) means "and also". पेशनानि (*peśanāni*) are the forms of those lights, such as blackness and whiteness. Or else, अध (*adha*) means "thereafter", and पेश (*peśa*) is a name of gold: bright lights like gold, resembling gold. Covering yourself with these, at the navel of the earth, in the navel-like place, the place of Iḷā, that is, the Uttaravedi (the Brāhmaṇa says एतद्वा इळायास्पदं यदुत्तरवेदीनाभिः (*etadvā iḷāyāspadaṃ yaduttaravedīnābhiḥ*), Ai. Brā. 1-28), you who are born there, अरुष (*aruṣa*), shining, such as you are, being पुरोहित (*purohita*), placed in front, established in the Āhavanīya's site in the eastern direction, worship (यज (*yaja*), honour) here in this sacrifice the gods, Indra and the rest.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अध (*adha*)—and
- राजन् (*rājan*)—O Agni, the lord
- वस्त्राणि (*vastrāṇi*)—covering for you
- पेशनानि (*peśanāni*)—the forms of light
- वसानः (*vasānaḥ*)—having put on
- पृथिव्याः (*pṛthivyāḥ*)—of the earth
- नाभा (*nābhā*)—

### Printed p. 208
- नाभा (*nābhā*)—(of the earth) which is the navel
- इळायाः पदे (*iḷāyāḥ pade*)—in the place of the Uttaravedi
- जातः (*jātaḥ*)—born
- अरुषः (*aruṣaḥ*)—radiant
- *agniḥ*—worshipful
- पुरोहितः (*purohitaḥ*)—being established in the place of the Āhavanīya
- इह (*iha*)—in this sacrifice
- देवान् (*devān*)—the gods, Indra and the rest
- यक्षि (*yakṣi*)—worship

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, lord, you who are clad in splendours that cover you like a garment, born in the place of the Uttaravedi, which is the navel of the earth, radiant, worshipful, and established in the place of the Āhavanīya, worship the gods, Indra and the rest, in this sacrifice.

##### English translation
Arrayed in splendid garments, that Agni (abides) on the navel of the earth ; do you, royal (Agni), who are radiant, born on the foot-mark of Ila, offer worship here as purohita to the gods.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

वस्त्राणि (*vastrāṇi*)—Here it is the usual way of speaking to say that for Agni splendour, that is, light, is itself the garment.

नाभा पृथिव्याः इळायाः पदे (*nābhā pṛthivyāḥ iḷāyāḥ pade*)—The meaning is: in the Uttaravedi of the sacrifice, which stands in the place of the navel of the earth. To establish that इळायाः पदे (*iḷāyāḥ pade*) means the Uttaravedi, the Bhāṣyakāra has quoted the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa sentence एतद्वा इळायास्पदं यदुत्तरवेदीनाभिः (*etadvā iḷāyāspadaṃ yaduttaravedīnābhiḥ*) (Ai. Brā. 1-28). In the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā too the word is used, with the sense of the Uttaravedi, in Ṛks such as इळस्पदे समिध्यसे स नो वसून्या भर (*iḷaspade samidhyase sa no vasūnyā bhara*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-191-1). Further, that the words नाभा पृथिव्याः (*nābhā pṛthivyāḥ*) mean the Uttaravedi, the navel-place of the earth, is stated in many places in the Ṛgveda itself. The well-known seer Dīrghatamas, son of Ucathya, asks

पृच्छामि त्वा परमंतं पृथिव्याः पृच्छामि त्वा भुवनस्य नाभिः। (*pṛcchāmi tvā paramaṃtaṃ pṛthivyāḥ pṛcchāmi tvā bhuvanasya nābhiḥ|*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-164-34)

and gives the answer to it in

इयं वेदिः परो अंतः पृथिव्या अयं यज्ञो भुवनस्य नाभिः ॥ (*iyaṃ vediḥ paro aṃtaḥ pṛthivyā ayaṃ yajño bhuvanasya nābhiḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-164-35)

in that very place. These two Ṛks given as examples are brahmodya, that is, Ṛks that examine the principle of Brahman. That is: on the middle day of the Aśvamedha sacrifice the Hotṛ questions the Yajamāna, and the Yajamāna answers with the second Ṛk.

### Printed p. 209

Such an inquiry into Brahman, between the Hotṛ and the Brahman priest, is customary in the principal sacrifices. In the present Ṛk the words नाभा पृथिव्याः (*nābhā pṛthivyāḥ*) are expressed in the quoted Ṛk by the words भुवनस्य नाभिः (*bhuvanasya nābhiḥ*). Therefore both kinds of words have one and the same meaning. It is necessary to examine closely here how the sacrificial altar is the navel of the world.

It was explained earlier that the word नाभि (*nābhi*) has different meanings: dwelling-place, a pervading connection, संनाहक (*saṃnāhaka*), that is, protector, binder, place of origin, and so on. If the sacrifice itself is said to be the navel in the present Ṛk, the use of this word can be shown in all these senses. This sentence stands as the answer to the question asked in the preceding Ṛk, पृच्छामि यत्र भुवनस्य नाभिः (*pṛcchāmi yatra bhuvanasya nābhiḥ*). The purport is that the sacrifice is the cause of the origin and the continuance of all the worlds and is the binding means that builds a mutual connection among all beings, gods, men and the rest; therefore it stands in the form of a navel.

देवान् भावयतानेन ते देवा भावयंतु वः । परस्परं भावयंतः श्रेयः परमवाप्स्यथ ॥ (*devān bhāvayatānena te devā bhāvayaṃtu vaḥ | parasparaṃ bhāvayaṃtaḥ śreyaḥ paramavāpsyatha ‖*)
(Bh. Gī. 3-11) **NOTE**

देवान् इंद्रादीन् भावयत वर्धयत अनेन यज्ञेन । ते देवा भावयंतु वृष्ट्वा दिना आप्याययंतु वः युष्मान् । एवं परस्परं अनोन्यं भावयंतः श्रेयः परं ब्रह्मविज्ञानप्राप्तिं क्रमेण अनाप्स्यथ । स्वर्गं वा परं श्रेयः अनाप्स्यथ । (*devān iṃdrādīn bhāvayata vardhayata anena yajñena | te devā bhāvayaṃtu vṛṣṭvā dinā āpyāyayaṃtu vaḥ yuṣmān | evaṃ parasparaṃ anonyaṃ bhāvayaṃtaḥ śreyaḥ paraṃ brahmavijñānaprāptiṃ krameṇa anāpsyatha | svargaṃ vā paraṃ śreyaḥ anāpsyatha |*)
(Śaṃ. Bhā.) **NOTE**

The Bhagavadgītā teaches that the sacrifice is as food for the gods; that men should offer this food in the form of sacrifice; and that the gods, satisfied by it, should become the cause of the sustenance and growth of men through rain and the like. From this it follows that the sacrifice is the means to every kind of welfare. Śaṅkara and others have explained that if one knows the principle that this sacrifice is the means of welfare for the whole world and performs it in the spirit of service, it becomes a means to the knowledge of Brahman also.

This principle of sacrifice makes known the unity of the universe and the principle that the ground of this unity is the Supreme Brahman. What the Upaniṣads and the like call the Supreme Brahman, the original principle, is called Prajāpati in the Saṃhitās and Brāhmaṇas; and identity between the sacrifice and Prajāpati is stated. The creation of the universe is the fruit of Prajāpati's sacrifice and austerity. Prajāpati, the cause of the world, who was one without a second before the creation of the world, had to offer himself as the oblation in order to carry out the sacrifice, the means of creation.

### Printed p. 210

य आत्मदा बलदा यस्य विश्व उपासते प्रशिषं यस्य देवाः । यस्य छायामृतं यस्य मृत्युः कस्मै देवाय हविषा विधेम । (*ya ātmadā baladā yasya viśva upāsate praśiṣaṃ yasya devāḥ | yasya chāyāmṛtaṃ yasya mṛtyuḥ kasmai devāya haviṣā vidhema |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-121-2)

As described in this and similar passages, Prajāpati offered himself in his own universal sacrifice and carried out the work of creation. All the gods and the rest carry out his command. Death and immortality are his shadows. The praise of the sacrifice's pre-eminence is: "Let us too, performing sacrificial rites, worship with puroḍāśa and the like that Prajāpati, the cause of the universe." The sacrifice is the root of creation, the cause of continuance, and the means to welfare. The Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa describes this very matter thus:

अथ देवाः । अनोन्यमस्मिन्नेव जुह्वतश्चेरुस्तेभ्यः प्रजापतिरात्मानं प्रददौ यज्ञो हैषामास यज्ञो हि देवनामन्नं । (*atha devāḥ | anonyamasminneva juhvataścerustebhyaḥ prajāpatirātmānaṃ pradadau yajño haiṣāmāsa yajño hi devanāmannaṃ |*)
(Śa. Brā. 11-1-8-2)

The gods performed the sacrifice and offered oblations helpful to one another. Then Prajāpati offered himself to them. Then the sacrifice became the very property of the gods. Therefore the sacrifice is called the food of the gods [that is, the means of food]. Moreover,

स एतेन यज्ञेन । देवेभ्य आत्मानं निरक्रीणीत स यद्व्रतमुपैति यथैव तत्प्रजापतिर्देवेभ्य आत्मानं प्रायच्छदेवमेवैष एतद्देवेभ्य आत्मानं प्रयच्छति तस्मादु संयत एवैताग्ं रात्रिं चिचरिषेद्यथा हविषा चरेदेवग्ं हविर्ह्येष देवानां भवति ॥ (*sa etena yajñena | devebhya ātmānaṃ nirakrīṇīta sa yadvratamupaiti yathaiva tatprajāpatirdevebhya ātmānaṃ prāyacchadevamevaiṣa etaddevebhya ātmānaṃ prayacchati tasmādu saṃyata evaitāgṃ rātriṃ cicariṣedyathā haviṣā caredevagṃ havirhyeṣa devānāṃ bhavati ‖*)
(Śa. Brā. 11-1-8-3)

Prajāpati, satisfied by the gods' sacrifice, offered himself to them; afterwards he again created his own counterpart, the representative of the sacrifice. Because he thus created his own counterpart for the sake of the sacrifice and reflected the sacrifice in it, the knowing call Prajāpati himself by the synonymous name "sacrifice".

अथ यद्यज्ञं तनुते । यज्ञेनैवैतद्देवेभ्य आत्मानं निष्क्रिणीते यथैव तत्प्रजापतिर्निरक्रीणीतैवं अथ यद्धविनिर्वपति हविषैवैतद्यज्ञं निष्क्रिणाति हविरनुवाक्यानुवाक्यामवदानेनावदानं याज्यया याज्यां वषट्कारेण वषट्कारं..................!! (*atha yadyajñaṃ tanute | yajñenaivaitaddevebhya ātmānaṃ niṣkriṇīte yathaiva tatprajāpatirnirakrīṇītaivaṃ atha yaddhavinirvapati haviṣaivaitadyajñaṃ niṣkriṇāti haviranuvākyānuvākyāmavadānenāvadānaṃ yājyayā yājyāṃ vaṣaṭkāreṇa vaṣaṭkāraṃ..................!!*)
(Śa. Brā. 11-1-8-4)

What is said in the Śatapatha, that whoever performs the sacrificial rite following Prajāpati's path, and discharges his debt and is freed from the debt to the gods, becomes a helper in Prajāpati's work of creation, is stated in the Bhagavadgītā also:

### Printed p. 211

इष्टान् भोगान् हि वो देवा दास्यंते यज्ञभाविताः । तैर्दत्तानप्रदायैभ्यो यो भुंक्ते स्तेन एव सः ॥ यज्ञशिष्टाशिनस्संतो मुच्यंते सर्वकिल्पिषैः । ते त्वघं भुंजते पापा ये पचंत्यात्मकारणात् ॥ (*iṣṭān bhogān hi vo devā dāsyaṃte yajñabhāvitāḥ | tairdattānapradāyaibhyo yo bhuṃkte stena eva saḥ ‖ yajñaśiṣṭāśinassaṃto mucyaṃte sarvakilpiṣaiḥ | te tvaghaṃ bhuṃjate pāpā ye pacaṃtyātmakāraṇāt ‖*)
(Bh. Gī. 3-12-13)

The gods, satisfied by the sacrifice, give men in return their desired ends. If, having received from the gods wealth such as food, one does not offer in return an oblation of sacrifice that satisfies the gods, he becomes a thief. The good men who perform the sacrificial rite are freed from sin (kilbiṣa); those who do not become sinners and experience the fruit of sin. For this sacrifice is the thread that shows one connection for the whole universe; this is also called the wheel of the universe. For example:

अन्नाद्भवंति भूतानि पर्जन्यादन्न संभवः । यज्ञाद्भवति पर्जन्यो यज्ञः कर्मसमुद्भवः ॥ कर्म ब्रह्मोद्भवं विद्धि ब्रह्माक्षरसमुद्भवं । तस्मात्सर्वगतं ब्रह्म नित्यं यज्ञे प्रतिष्ठितं ॥ एवं प्रवर्तितं चक्रं नानुवर्तयतीह यः । आघायुरिंदि.यारामो मोघं सार्थक स जीवति अघायुरिंद्रियारानो मोघं पार्थ स जीवति ॥ (*annādbhavaṃti bhūtāni parjanyādanna saṃbhavaḥ | yajñādbhavati parjanyo yajñaḥ karmasamudbhavaḥ ‖ karma brahmodbhavaṃ viddhi brahmākṣarasamudbhavaṃ | tasmātsarvagataṃ brahma nityaṃ yajñe pratiṣṭhitaṃ ‖ evaṃ pravartitaṃ cakraṃ nānuvartayatīha yaḥ | āghāyuriṃdi.yārāmo moghaṃ sārthaka sa jīvati aghāyuriṃdriyārāno moghaṃ pārtha sa jīvati ‖*)
(Bh. Gī. 3-13 to 16) **NOTE**

From the sacrifice, which is action, come Parjanya (rain); from Parjanya, food; from food, beings. This action in the form of sacrifice arose from Brahman. Therefore the Supreme Brahman, the root of the creation of the universe, of all kinds of dealings, and also of the universal wheel that recurs, is ever established in the sacrifice. The person who, not knowing this principle of the universe that is sacrifice, does not follow the course of this ordained wheel of the universe, and lives wilfully, is a sinner. Thus the principle of sacrifice, set forth by statements of śruti, is explained in the Gītā and elsewhere. This principle of sacrifice, described in the present Ṛk by "नाभ पृथिव्याः (*nābha pṛthivyāḥ*)", is praised in the same way in other parts of the Ṛk Saṃhitā also. For example:

यज्ञेन यज्ञमयजंत देवास्तानि धर्माणि प्रथमान्यासन् । ते ह नाकं महिमानः सचंत यत्र पूर्वे साध्याः संति देवाः ॥ (*yajñena yajñamayajaṃta devāstāni dharmāṇi prathamānyāsan | te ha nākaṃ mahimānaḥ sacaṃta yatra pūrve sādhyāḥ saṃti devāḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-164-50 and 10-90-16)

The gods worshipped Prajāpati, whose form is sacrifice, with the sacrifice that was their own resolve and meditation. This kind of sacrifice became the root of all worldly dharmas.

### Printed p. 212

These very dharmas of the sacrifice, established in the atmosphere, keep the gods who dwell in the heavenly world in their own places. Through the sacrifice itself, all the beings created by Prajāpati obtain food and strength, the cause of their continuance. Therefore, to show the necessity of the sacrifice, it is said in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā:

प्रजापतिः प्रजा असृजत तास्सृष्टाः क्षुधं न्यायंत्स एतं नवरात्रमपश्यत्तमाहरत्तेनायजत ततो वै प्रजाभ्योकल्पत यर्हि प्रजाः क्षुधं निगच्छेयुस्तर्हि नवरात्रेण यजेतेमे हि वा एतासां लोका अक्षृप्ता ......॥ (*prajāpatiḥ prajā asṛjata tāssṛṣṭāḥ kṣudhaṃ nyāyaṃtsa etaṃ navarātramapaśyattamāharattenāyajata tato vai prajābhyokalpata yarhi prajāḥ kṣudhaṃ nigaccheyustarhi navarātreṇa yajeteme hi vā etāsāṃ lokā akṣṛptā ......‖*)
(Kṛ. Saṃ. 7-2-4-1) **NOTE**

After Prajāpati created the creatures, they were afflicted with hunger. To still their hunger he saw the sacrifice consisting of nine nights and performed the sacrifice with it. By that the hunger of those creatures was stilled. Therefore it is fixed that the sacrifice should be performed for the stilling of hunger. That sacrifice keeps the three worlds in one state and under regulation. How?

कल्पंतेऽस्मा इमे लोका ऊर्जं प्रजासु दधाति त्रिरात्रेणैवेमं लोकं कल्पयति त्रिरात्रेणांतरिक्षं त्रिरात्रेणामुं लोकं यथा गुणे गुणमन्वस्यत्येवमेव तल्लोके लोकमन्वस्यति धृत्वा अशिथिलं भावाय............॥ (*kalpaṃte 'smā ime lokā ūrjaṃ prajāsu dadhāti trirātreṇaivemaṃ lokaṃ kalpayati trirātreṇāṃtarikṣaṃ trirātreṇāmuṃ lokaṃ yathā guṇe guṇamanvasyatyevameva talloke lokamanvasyati dhṛtvā aśithilaṃ bhāvāya............‖*)
(Tai. Saṃ. 7-2-4-2)

Through this sacrifice of nine nights, vitality came to the three worlds. Through one three-night, Prajāpati arranged by the power of this sacrifice the power of continuance for the earth; through another three-night for the atmosphere; through the remaining three-night for the heavenly world. Just as a cloth is made by joining thread with thread in warp and woof, so, to prevent slackness and to produce for the whole universe a power of one connection, so that the three worlds stand in their ordained state, he established by this sacrifice a warp-and-woof connection for the three worlds. This sacrifice is the pulse of the life of the universe. Describing it, the Śatapatha says:

सर्वेषां वा विष भूतानाग्ं सर्वेषां देवानामात्मा यद्यज्ञस्तस्य समृद्धिमनु यजमानः प्रजया पशुभिर्यध्यते । (*sarveṣāṃ vā viṣa bhūtānāgṃ sarveṣāṃ devānāmātmā yadyajñastasya samṛddhimanu yajamānaḥ prajayā paśubhiryadhyate |*)
(Śa. Brā. 14-3-2-1)

This sacrifice is the very self of all men and all gods. Whoever performs this sacrifice obtains good offspring and wealth of cattle. And further,

### Printed p. 213

स यदग्नौ जुहोति । तद्देवेषु जुहोति तस्याद्देवाः संत्यथ यत्सदसि भक्षयंति तन्मनुष्येषु जुहोति तस्मान्मनुष्याः संत्यथ यद्धविर्धानयोर्नाराशग्ंसाः सीदंति तत्पितृषु जुहोति तस्मात्पितरः संति ॥ (*sa yadagnau juhoti | taddeveṣu juhoti tasyāddevāḥ saṃtyatha yatsadasi bhakṣayaṃti tanmanuṣyeṣu juhoti tasmānmanuṣyāḥ saṃtyatha yaddhavirdhānayornārāśagṃsāḥ sīdaṃti tatpitṛṣu juhoti tasmātpitaraḥ saṃti ‖*)
(Śa. Brā. 2-2-2-25)

What is offered in the fire is given to the gods. Therefore the gods have maintained their state. By the (Soma) juice distributed in the Sadas there is the state of men. The oblation offered with the Nārāśaṃsa cup satisfies the Pitṛs. Therefore a man must discharge his debt to the gods and also his debt to the Pitṛs by the sacrificial rite alone. Without this there is no release for him from debt. In the sentence quoted earlier from the Taittirīya Saṃhitā,

"तल्लोके ह लोकमन्वस्यति धृत्या अशिथिलं भवाय (*talloke ha lokamanvasyati dhṛtyā aśithilaṃ bhavāya*) ।"
(Tai. Saṃ. 7-2-4-2) **NOTE**

it is shown that the sacrificial rite is necessary so that the existence of the universe may be firm and not slack, and so that the connection of the three worlds may be woven together like warp and woof. When the three debts are spoken of, this mutual bond is also described. For example, in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa:

ऋणग्ं हवै जायते योऽस्ति स जायमान एव देवेभ्य ऋषिभ्यः पितृभ्यो मनुष्येभ्यः ॥ (*ṛṇagṃ havai jāyate yo 'sti sa jāyamāna eva devebhya ṛṣibhyaḥ pitṛbhyo manuṣyebhyaḥ ‖*)
(Śa. Brā. 1-7-2-1)

Throughout the life of every person, the debt to the gods, the debt to the Ṛṣis and the debt to the Pitṛs, which come with his very birth, remain a burden upon him.

स यदेव ऋजेत । तेन देवेभ्य ऋणं जायते तद्धैभ्य एतत्करोति यदेभ्यो जुहोति ॥ (*sa yadeva ṛjeta | tena devebhya ṛṇaṃ jāyate taddhaibhya etatkaroti yadebhyo juhoti ‖*)
(Śa. Brā. 1-7-2-2) **NOTE**

Each person, in order to be freed from this debt, to take part also in the continuance of the universe, and to preserve his own existence, must perform the sacrifice, discharge the debt to the gods and be freed from that debt.

जायमानो वै ब्राह्मणस्त्रिभिर्ऋणवा जायते ब्रह्मचर्येणर्षिभ्यो यज्ञेन देवेभ्यः प्रजया पितृभ्य एष ना अनृणो यः पुत्री यज्वा ब्रह्मचारिवासी ॥ (*jāyamāno vai brāhmaṇastribhirṛṇavā jāyate brahmacaryeṇarṣibhyo yajñena devebhyaḥ prajayā pitṛbhya eṣa nā anṛṇo yaḥ putrī yajvā brahmacārivāsī ‖*)
(Tai. Saṃ. 6-3-11-5)

### Printed p. 214
The Kannada text continues from the previous page.

Moreover, a Brahmin is born carrying three kinds of debts from the moment of his birth: the debt to the ṛṣis, the debt to the gods, and the debt to the ancestors. The debt to the ṛṣis is discharged by dwelling in the teacher's household under the vow of celibacy and studying the Veda regularly, with instruction from the teacher. The debt to the gods is discharged by worshipping the deities through sacrifices and the like. The debt to the ancestors is discharged by begetting lawful offspring on a wife taken in marriage. So it is also said in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā (Yajurveda), and so on. Thus, if every individual is to attain a vigorous existence within the universal order, sacrifice is the thread that links these generalities and particulars. This sacrifice, which is such a thread, itself grows at one time along with the expansion of the universe, and contracts at another time along with its contraction. It adjusts its own course to the course of the universe. Explaining this point, the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa gives the etymology of the word *yajña*:

अथ यस्माद्यज्ञो नाम । घ्नंति वा एनमेतद्यदभिषुण्वंति तद्यदेनं तन्नते तदेनं जनयंति स तायमानो जायते स यन् जायते तस्माद्यज्ञो यञ्जॊ ह वै नामैतद्यज्ञ इति । (*atha yasmādyajño nāma | ghnaṃti vā enametadyadabhiṣuṇvaṃti tadyadenaṃ tannate tadenaṃ janayaṃti sa tāyamāno jāyate sa yan jāyate tasmādyajño yañjò ha vai nāmaitadyajña iti |*)
(Śa. Brā. 3-9-4-23)

At the time of pressing, they crush the soma plant and kill it. Then they make it come into being again. Because it comes into being with an expanding course, the name *yajña* became established. Thus it is shown that contraction and expansion also exist according to need. And since it accords with the state and course of the universe, it is a benefactor of the universe. How is that? —

भुज्युः सुपर्ण इति वै भुज्युः यज्ञो हि सर्वाणि भुनक्ति यज्ञो गंधर्वः..................॥ (*bhujyuḥ suparṇa iti vai bhujyuḥ yajño hi sarvāṇi bhunakti yajño gaṃdharvaḥ..................‖*)
(Śa. Cā. 9-4-1-11) **NOTE**

Sacrifice requires all beings for its offering, and again it spreads out for the benefit of all beings. Having thus shown the relation of identity between the universal principle and sacrifice, the text says that sacrifice is the cause of all worldly activity of the universe, that it alone is the protector, and that it alone is the relation in the form of a thread. This point, described in many Śruti passages, is described in the present Ṛk by the words नाभा पृथिव्याः (*nābhā pṛthivyāḥ*). For that very reason, to carry out this sacrifice, which is the navel of the universe, equal faith is shown to be absolutely necessary for gods, men and all the rest. **NOTE**

### Printed p. 215
यो वै श्रद्धामनारभ्य यज्ञेन यजते नास्यॆष्टाय श्रद्धधतेऽपः प्रणयति श्रद्धा वा आपः श्रद्धामेवारभ्य यज्ञेन यजत उभयेऽस्य देवमनुष्या इष्टाय श्रद्धधते ॥ (*yo vai śraddhāmanārabhya yajñena yajate nāsyèṣṭāya śraddhadhate 'paḥ praṇayati śraddhā vā āpaḥ śraddhāmevārabhya yajñena yajata ubhaye 'sya devamanuṣyā iṣṭāya śraddhadhate ‖*)
(Tai. Saṃ. 1-6-8-1)

The sacrifice of one who sacrifices without faith does not succeed. The sacrifice must be performed with the *ap* (water), which is faith, placed first. It is also shown that faith is necessary, for all, gods, men and the rest, take part in the sacrifice only through faith.

This faith that must be placed in the principle of sacrifice is not mere blind faith. This faith must have the power to grasp the spiritual principle. It must know the relation between sacrifice, the navel of the world, and the principle of Brahman. The sacrificer who has this faith, standing on the basis of spiritual knowledge, conceives an identity between himself and the sacrifice. Afterwards he comes to know the identity between sacrifice and Prajāpati. He comes to know that all the worldly activities of the Supreme Brahman in the form of Prajāpati lie hidden in this principle of sacrifice. And he himself also takes a share in that wheel of the universe.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ हि द्यावा॑पृथि॒वी अ॑ग्न उ॒भे सदा॑ पु॒त्रो न मा॒तरा॑ त॒तंथ॑।  
> प्र या॒ह्य च्यो॑श॒तो य॑वि॒ष्वाथा व॑ह सहस्ये॒ह दे॒वान् ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । हि । द्यावापृथिवी इति । अग्ने ! उभे इति । सदा । पुत्रः । न ! मातरा । ततंथ ।  
> प्र । याहि । अच्छ । उशतः । यविष्ठ । अथ । आ । वह । सहस्य । इह । देवान् ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने त्वमुभे द्यावापृथिवी द्यावापृथिव्य सदा सर्वदा ततंथ । आतेनिथ । स्वतेजोभिर्विस्तारयसि खलु ॥ बभूथा ततंधेति थलि निपातितः ॥ तत्र
> दृष्टांतः । पुत्रो न यथा पुत्रो मातरा मातापितरौ क्षीणौ धनैस्तनोति
> तद्वत् । किंच हे यविष्ठ युवतम स त्वमुशतः कामयमानानच्छाभिलक्ष्य प्र
> याहि । प्रकर्षणागच्छ । अथानंतरं हे सहस्य सहसो बलस्य पुत्र हे अग्ने त्वमिहास्यदीये यज्ञे देवानिंद्रादीना वह । प्रापय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, you have always stretched out both heaven and earth, extending them by your own radiances. He cites the example: as a son supports his parents, who have become worn out, with wealth. Further, O youngest one, you should go forth toward those who desire you, approaching them eagerly. Then, O son of strength (सहस्य (*sahasya*)), bring Indra and the other gods hither to this sacrifice of ours and cause them to arrive.

### Printed p. 216

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- पुत्रः (*putraḥ*)—a son
- मातरा न (*mātarā na*)—as he nourishes (supports) his parents with his wealth
- (त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you)
- उभे द्यावापृथिवी (*ubhe dyāvāpṛthivī*)—both heaven and earth
- सदा (*sadā*)—always
- आ ततंथ (*ā tataṃtha*)—you have caused to be extended
- यविष्ठ (*yaviṣṭha*)—O Agni, the youngest
- उशतः अच्छ (*uśataḥ accha*)—toward the sacrificers who desire you
- प्र याहि (*pra yāhi*)—come eagerly
- अथ (*atha*)—afterwards
- सहस्य (*sahasya*)—O Agni, son of strength
- इह (*iha*)—in this sacrifice of ours
- देवान् (*devān*)—the gods, Indra and the others
- आ वह (*ā vaha*)—bring and unite them

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, as a son nourishes and supports his parents with his wealth, you have caused both heaven and earth to be extended always. O Agni, the youngest, come eagerly toward the sacrificers who desire you. Afterwards, O Agni, son of strength, bring Indra and the other gods to this sacrifice of ours and unite them with it.

##### English translation
You have ever sustained, Agni, both heaven and earth, as a son (supports) his parents : come youngest (of the gods), to the presence of those desiring (you); Son of strength, bring hither the gods.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

पुत्रः मातरा न (*putraḥ mātarā na*)—Since the dual form of the word *mātṛ* is used here, the word मातरा (*mātarā*) means mother and father together. "As a son supports his parents" means here: as a young son supports with food and the like parents who have grown old and weak. Parents protect their son in childhood. In course of time, when the parents have grown old and are unable to do work, it is the son's duty, as a young man, to support and protect them. This very matter is explained here by the illustration पुत्रो न मातरा (*putro na mātarā*).

## Sūkta 2 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> पिप्रीहीति सप्तर्चं द्वितीयं सूक्तं । ऋष्याद्याः पूर्ववत् । पिप्रीहीत्यनुक्रांतं ॥ प्रातरनुवाकाशिवशस्त्रयोरुक्तः सूक्तविनियोगः ॥ दर्शपूर्णमासयो-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The second sūkta, beginning पिप्रीहि (*piprīhi*), has seven Ṛks. The seer and the rest are as before. The Anukramaṇī says पिप्रीहि (*piprīhi*). The use of the sūkta in the Prātaranuvāka and in the Āśvina-śastra has been stated. In the Darśapūrṇamāsa sacrifices the first Ṛk is the anuvākyā of the Sviṣṭakṛt. ()

### Printed p. 217

> राद्या स्विष्टकृतोऽनुवाक्या । सूत्रितं च । अथ स्विष्टकृतः पिप्रीहि देवा उशतो
> यविष्ठेत्यनुवाक्या । आ. १.६ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Continuing: and it is cited in a sūtra: "Now for the Sviṣṭakṛt: पिप्रीहि देवा उशतो यविष्ठ (*piprīhi devā uśato yaviṣṭha*) is the anuvākyā" (Ā. 1.6).

##### English translation
The Kannada Anuvāda: This sūkta beginning पिप्रीहि (*piprīhi*) is the second sūkta in the first anuvāka of the tenth maṇḍala. It has seven Ṛks. The seer, deity and metre of this sūkta are the same as those of the previous sūkta. In the Anukramaṇī it is said पिप्रीहि (*piprīhi*). It was already said that this sūkta is used for the Prātaranuvāka mantras and the Āśvina-śastra mantras. And it is explained in the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, in the sūtra अथ स्विष्टकृतः पिप्रीहि देवा उशतो यविष्ठेत्यनुवाक्या (*atha sviṣṭakṛtaḥ piprīhi devā uśato yaviṣṭhetyanuvākyā*) (Ā. 1-6), that the first Ṛk of this sūkta is to be recited as the anuvākyā mantra in the Svaṣṭakṛt offering at the Darśapūrṇamāsa sacrifices. **NOTE**

Particulars: Sūkta 2; Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 1; Sūkta 2; Aṣṭaka 7; Adhyāya 5; Varga 30. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 7. Seer: Trita Āptya. Deity: Agni. Metre: Triṣṭup.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> पि॒प्री॒हि दे॒वा उ॑श॒तो य॑विष्ठ वि॒द्वान् ऋ॒तूऋ॑तुपते  ॒
> यजेह ।  
> ये दैव्या॑ ऋ॒त्विज॒स्तेभि॑रग्ने॒ त्वं होत्य॑णाम॒स्याय॑जिष्ठः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पिप्रीहि । देवान् । उशतः । यविष्ठ । विद्वान् । ऋतून् । ऋतुपते । यज ।  
> इह ।  
> ये । दैव्याः । ऋत्विजः । तेभिः । अग्ने । त्वं । होत्यणां । असि । आयजिष्ठः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे यविष्ठ युवतमाग्ने उशतः स्तुतीः त्रोतुं कामायमानान्देवान्पिप्रीहि । हविर्भिः प्रीणय । हे ऋतुपत ऋतूतां देवयागकालानां स्वामिन्नग्ने

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, the youngest, propitiate with oblations the gods who desire to hear praises. O lord of the ṛtus, the seasons (times) of the sacrifices to the gods, you who know the times of sacrifice, worship them here in this sacrifice.

### Printed p. 218

> ऋतून्यागकालान्विद्वाञ्ज्ञानानस्त्वमिहास्मिन्यज्ञे तान्यज । पूजय । किंच हे अग्ने
> दैव्या देवेषु भवाः अग्निर्होताश्विनावध्वर्यू त्वष्टाग्नीन्मित्र उपवक्ता । तै. आ.
> ३-३ । इत्यत्रोक्ता य ऋत्विजः संति तेभिस्तैः सह देवान्यज । यद्वा । दैव्या
> देवेषु भवा ऋत्विज आश्वलायनेनोकाश्चंद्रमा ब्रह्मादित्योऽध्वर्युः पर्जन्य
> उद्दातेति ! आ. गृ. १.१३. ८,९ । तैः सह यज ! यस्मात्त्वं होत्यणां मध्य आय.
> जिष्ठ अभिमुख्येन देवानां यष्टृतमोऽसि भवसि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Further, O Agni, the divine priests (दैव्या (*daivyā*)), those born among the gods, are those named in the passage अग्निर्होताश्विनावध्वर्यू त्वष्टाग्नीन्मित्र उपवक्ता (*agnirhotāśvināvadhvaryū tvaṣṭāgnīnmitra upavaktā*) (Tai. Ā. 3-3); worship the gods together with them. Or else: the divine priests are those spoken of by Āśvalāyana: चंद्रमा ब्रह्मादित्योऽध्वर्युः पर्जन्य उद्दातेति (*caṃdramā brahmādityo 'dhvaryuḥ parjanya uddāteti*) (Ā. Gṛ. 1.13.8,9); worship together with them. For among the hotṛs you are the foremost, the best sacrificer to the gods. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यविष्ठ (*yaviṣṭha*)—O Agni, the youngest
- उशतः (*uśataḥ*)—who desire praises
- देवान् (*devān*)—the gods
- पिप्रीहि (*piprīhi*)—satisfy with oblations
- ऋतुपते (*ṛtupate*)—O Agni, lord of the times of sacrifice
- यतून् (*yatūn*)—the times of sacrifice
- विद्वान् (*vidvān*)—you who know
- इह (*iha*)—in this sacrifice
- यज (*yaja*)—worship them
- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- दैव्याः (*daivyāḥ*)—related to the gods
- ये ऋत्विजः (*ye ṛtvijaḥ*)—whatever priests there are
- तेभिः (*tebhiḥ*)—with them worship the gods
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- होतृणां (*hotṛṇāṃ*)—among the hotṛs
- आ यजिष्ठः (*ā yajiṣṭhaḥ*)—as the most excellent worshipper of the gods
- असि (*asi*)—you are

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, the youngest, satisfy with oblations the gods who desire praises. O Agni, the regulator of the times of sacrifice, you who know the times of sacrifice, worship them in this sacrifice. O Agni, worship the gods together with whatever priests belong to the gods. Among the hotṛs you are the most excellent worshipper of the gods.

##### English translation
Agni, youngest (of the deities), propitiate the desiring gods; lord of seasons, cognizant of seasons, here offer worship; worship with those who are the priests of the celestials; among the ministrant priests you are the best offerer.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

ऋतुपते, ऋतून् विद्वान् (*ṛtupate, ṛtūn vidvān*)—Yāska has said that the word *ṛtu* has the meaning "time" (Ni. 8-4 and 12-46). And, explaining the meaning of the word *ṛtu* and its formation, he says:

मुहूर्तो मुहुर्ऋतुः । ऋतुरर्तेर्गतिकर्मणः । मुहुर्मूहळ इव कालो यावदभीक्ष्णं चेति । अभीक्ष्ण मभिक्षणं भवति । क्षणः क्षणोतः प्रक्षुतः कालः । कालः कालयतेर्गतिकर्मणः ॥ (*muhūrto muhurṛtuḥ | ṛturartergatikarmaṇaḥ | muhurmūhaḷa iva kālo yāvadabhīkṣṇaṃ ceti | abhīkṣṇa mabhikṣaṇaṃ bhavati | kṣaṇaḥ kṣaṇotaḥ prakṣutaḥ kālaḥ | kālaḥ kālayatergatikarmaṇaḥ ‖*)
(reference printed garbled) **NOTE**

### Printed p. 219
*Muhūrta* means *muhuḥ* + *ṛtuḥ*: time that is attained again and again. As soon as one moment is over, another moment comes joined to it. That is, time is unbroken. The word *ṛtu* is formed from the root *ṛ-gat*, with the meanings "go", "pass", "elapse" and so on. Because it is passing away, *ṛtu* is called time. *Kṣaṇa* means a very small division of time. This word is formed from the root *kṣaṇ*, "injure, cut". *Kṣaṇa* is a piece of time (cut off), that is, a very small division. The word *kāla* also is formed from the root *kal* with the meaning "go", and means time that is passing away.

When sacrifices and offerings are to be performed, one should look at what is auspicious among months, lunar days, weekdays, constellations and the like, and perform them at that time. To explain that Agni knows such divisions of time and the nature of time, Agni is addressed here by the word ऋतुपते (*ṛtupate*); and ऋतून् विद्वान् (*ṛtūn vidvān*): to explain that Agni has full knowledge of which sacrifices are to be performed at which times, and which sacrifices the Brāhmaṇa and the other castes are to perform at which times, the words "knowing the ṛtus, knowing the nature of time" are given as Agni's qualities. **NOTE**

ये दैव्या ऋत्विजः (*ye daivyā ṛtvijaḥ*)—Among men there are priests such as the hotṛ and adhvaryu; it is said here that among the gods also there are priests.

On this matter the commentator has cited the Śruti passage of the Taittirīya Āraṇyaka:

अग्निर्होता । अश्विनाध्वयूर् । त्वष्टाग्नीत् । (*agnirhotā | aśvinādhvayūr | tvaṣṭāgnīt |*)
(Tai. Ā. 3-3)

Among the gods Agni is the priest called hotṛ, the Aśvins are the adhvaryus, Tvaṣṭṛ is the Āgnīdhra, and so on. Āśvalāyana too, in his Gṛhya Sūtra, explains: चंद्रमा ब्रह्मादित्योऽध्वर्युः पर्जन्य उद्गाता (*caṃdramā brahmādityo 'dhvaryuḥ parjanya udgātā*). Among the gods the moon is the priest called brahmā, the sun is the adhvaryu, and Parjanya is the priest called udgātṛ (Ā. Gṛ. 1-23-8, 9). **NOTE**

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वेषि॑ हो॒त्रमु॒त पो॒त्रं जना॑नां मंधा॒तासि॑ द्रविणो॒॒तु  ॑
> ऋ॒तावा॑ ।

### Printed p. 220

> स्वाहा वयं कृणवामा हवींषि देवो देवान्यजत्वग्निरर्हन् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वेषि । होत्रं । उत । पोत्रं । जनानां । मंधाता । असि । द्रविणःऽदाः । ऋतवा।  
> स्वाहा । वयं । कृणवाम । हवींषि । देवः । देवान् । यजतु । अग्नि: । अर्हन् ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने जनानां यजमानार्थं होत्रं होतुः कर्म तेन क्रियमाणं
> स्तोत्रं वेषि । कामयसे ! उतापि च पोत्रं पोतुः कर्म तेन क्रियमाणां
> स्तुतिं कामयसे । किंच मंधाता मेधाव्यृतवा सत्यवांस्त्वं द्रविणोदा असि ।
> धनस्य प्रधाता भवसि । अथ परोक्षकृतः । वयं देवेभ्यो हवींषि स्वाहा कृणवाम।
> स्वाहा करनाम । प्रयच्छाम । ततो देवो दीप्यमानोर्हन् प्रशंस्यो
> यजमानयोगो नाग्निर्देवान्यजतु । तैर्हविभिः पूजयतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Agni, you desire the होत्रं (*hotraṃ*), the Hotṛ's work, that is, the praise performed by him, for the sake of the people, that is, the sacrificers. You also desire the पोत्रं (*potraṃ*), the Potṛ's work, that is, the praise performed by him. Moreover you, being wise and truthful, are the giver of wealth, the bestower of riches." Then, changing to the third person (अथ परोक्षकृतः (*atha parokṣakṛtaḥ*)): "We offer oblations to the gods with svāhā; we make them (स्वाहा करनाम (*svāhā karanāma*)), we present them. Thereupon let the shining, praiseworthy Agni, who is joined with the sacrificer, worship the gods with those oblations."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- जनानां (*janānāṃ*)—for the sake of the sacrificer (O Agni)
- होत्रं (*hotraṃ*)—the praise of the Hotṛ
- वेषि (*veṣi*)—even if you should desire
- उत (*uta*)—or
- पोत्रं (*potraṃ*)—even if you should desire the praise of the Potṛ
- मंधाता (*maṃdhātā*)—wise
- ऋतवा (*ṛtavā*)—truthful, you
- द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*)—as the giver of wealth
- असि (*asi*)—you are
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- हवींषि (*havīṃṣi*)—the oblations
- स्वाहा कृणवाम (*svāhā kṛṇavāma*)—let us offer to the gods with svāhā
- देवः (*devaḥ*)—shining
- अर्हन् (*arhan*)—praiseworthy
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni
- देवान् (*devān*)—the gods
- यजतु (*yajatu*)—let him worship

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, even if you, as the sacrificer, should desire the praise of the Hotṛ, or should desire the praise of the Potṛ, you, wise and truthful, are the giver of wealth. Let us offer the oblations to the gods with svāhā. Let Agni, shining and praiseworthy, worship the gods.

##### English translation
Whether, Agni, you prefer the office of Hotri or Potri among men
you are wise. the giver of wealth, the observer of truth; let us offer the

### Printed p. 221
oblations with the (sacred) Swaha, and may the most excellent and divine
Agni present them to the gods.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*): Yāska has written an extended explanation of this word—द्रविणोदाः कस्माद्धनं द्रविणमुच्यते । यदेनदभिद्रवंति । बलं वा द्रविणं यदेदेनाभिद्रवंति । तस्य दाता द्रविणोदाः ॥ (*draviṇodāḥ kasmāddhanaṃ draviṇamucyate | yadenadabhidravaṃti | balaṃ vā draviṇaṃ yadedenābhidravaṃti | tasya dātā draviṇodāḥ ‖*) (Ni. 8-1). Here the derivation of the word द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*) is: द्रविणं ददातीति द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇaṃ dadātīti draviṇodāḥ*). The word द्रविण (*draviṇa*) has two meanings, wealth and strength. Therefore द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*) means one who gives wealth or strength. This is the meaning of the parts of the word. Besides this, Yāska has given the explanation (nirvacana) of the Ṛk द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*) as follows—
द्रविणोदा यस्त्वं । द्रविणस इति द्रविणसादिन इति वा द्रविणसानिन इति वा द्रविणसस्तस्मात् पिबत्विति वा । यज्ञेषु देवमीळते । याचंति स्तुवंति, वर्धयंति पूजयंतीति वा ॥ (*draviṇodā yastvaṃ | draviṇasa iti draviṇasādina iti vā draviṇasānina iti vā draviṇasastasmāt pibatviti vā | yajñeṣu devamīḷate | yācaṃti stuvaṃti, vardhayaṃti pūjayaṃtīti vā ‖*)
That is, the principal meaning is that the priests, desiring wealth, oblations and the like, pray in the sacrifices to you, called द्रविणोदा (*draviṇodā*), to drink the Soma. Here द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*) is nominative singular, yet how did it become an epithet of देवं (*devaṃ*), accusative singular? The words must be supplied so that the sense becomes: द्रविणोदा यः देवः तं देवं (*draviṇodā yaḥ devaḥ taṃ devaṃ*), "that god who is the giver of wealth." As for द्रविणसः (*draviṇasaḥ*), many meanings may be given: they ask for wealth or oblations for the worship of the deity, they praise, they worship.
तत्को द्रविणोदा इंद्र इति क्रौष्टुकिः । स बलधनयोर्दातृतमस्तस्य च सर्वा बलकृतिः ॥ (*tatko draviṇodā iṃdra iti krauṣṭukiḥ | sa baladhanayordātṛtamastasya ca sarvā balakṛtiḥ ‖*)
The meaning of the parts of the word द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*) has been stated. But who is this deity द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*)? Indra himself is called द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*); for Indra is the one who gives in the highest degree the strength and wealth that the word द्रविण (*draviṇa*) denotes, so Indra alone should be called द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*). This is the opinion of Krauṣṭuki.
अथाप्यग्निं द्रविणोदसमाहैष पुनरेतस्माज्जायते । यो अश्मनोरंतरग्निं जजान इत्यपि निगमो भवति ॥ (*athāpyagniṃ draviṇodasamāhaiṣa punaretasmājjāyate | yo aśmanoraṃtaragniṃ jajāna ityapi nigamo bhavati ‖*)

### Printed p. 222
He gives yet another meaning for this word. Indra created heaven and earth, and between them he created Agni as well. Therefore the word द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*) may also be said to mean Agni. To support this meaning he has given as an example the Ṛk अश्मनोरंतरग्निं जजान (*aśmanoraṃtaragniṃ jajāna*).
अथाप्यतुयाजेषु द्रविणोदसाः प्रवादा भवंति । तेषां पुनः पात्रस्येंद्रपानमिति भवति ॥ अथाप्येनं सोमपानेन स्तौति । अथाप्याह द्रविणोदाः पिबतु द्रविणोदस इति ॥ (*athāpyatuyājeṣu draviṇodasāḥ pravādā bhavaṃti | teṣāṃ punaḥ pātrasyeṃdrapānamiti bhavati ‖ athāpyenaṃ somapānena stauti | athāpyāha draviṇodāḥ pibatu draviṇodasa iti ‖*)
Because it is said that in the sacrifices offered to the seasons Indra drinks Soma from the vessel in the hands of the priests, and because Indra is praised with mantras relating to the drinking of Soma, there is a custom of calling the priests द्रविणोदस (*draviṇodasa*). All this is the view of Krauṣṭuki.
अयमेवाग्निर्द्रविणोदा इति शाकपूणिः । आग्नेयेष्टेव हि सूक्तेषु द्रविणोद साः प्रवादा भवंति ॥ देवा अग्निं धारयन्द्रविणोदामित्यपि निगमो भवति ॥ (*ayamevāgnirdraviṇodā iti śākapūṇiḥ | āgneyeṣṭeva hi sūkteṣu draviṇoda sāḥ pravādā bhavaṃti ‖ devā agniṃ dhārayandraviṇodāmityapi nigamo bhavati ‖*)
It is the opinion of Śākapūṇi that Agni himself is द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*); for in the hymns relating to Agni, when the priests praise Agni, they use the word द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*) as an epithet of Agni.
यथो एतत्स बलधनयोर्दात्ततम इति सर्वासु देवतास्वैश्वर्यं विद्यते । यथो एतदोजसो जातमुतमन्य एनमिति चाहेत्ययमप्यग्निरोजसा बलेन मध्यमानो जायते तस्मादेनमाह सहसस्पुत्रं सहसः सूनुं सहसो यहुं ॥ (*yatho etatsa baladhanayordāttatama iti sarvāsu devatāsvaiśvaryaṃ vidyate | yatho etadojaso jātamutamanya enamiti cāhetyayamapyagnirojasā balena madhyamāno jāyate tasmādenamāha sahasasputraṃ sahasaḥ sūnuṃ sahaso yahuṃ ‖*)
Lordship exists in all the deities; therefore Agni too gives strength and wealth, and because he gives strength and wealth Agni may also be called द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*). Moreover, the word द्रविण (*draviṇa*) means strength. When Agni is produced, it is by using strength (by forceful) churning, so there is a tradition that Agni is born of strength. Therefore he is praised with the words सहसस्पुत्रं (*sahasasputraṃ*) (सहसः (*sahasaḥ*) of strength, पुत्रं (*putraṃ*) Agni the son), सहसः सूनुं (*sahasaḥ sūnuṃ*), सहसो यहुं (*sahaso yahuṃ*)—
द्रव्यः सर्पिरासुतिः प्रत्नो होता वरेण्यः । सहसस्पुत्रो अद्भुतः ॥ (*dravyaḥ sarpirāsutiḥ pratno hotā vareṇyaḥ | sahasasputro adbhutaḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 2 7-6)

### Printed p. 223
त्वं ह यद्यविष्ठ्य सहसः सूनवाहुत । ऋता वा यज्ञियो भुवः ॥ (*tvaṃ ha yadyaviṣṭhya sahasaḥ sūnavāhuta | ṛtā vā yajñiyo bhuvaḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-75-3)
अग्ने वाजस्य गोमत ईशानः सहसो यहो । अस्मे धेहि जातवेदो महि श्रवः ॥ (*agne vājasya gomata īśānaḥ sahaso yaho | asme dhehi jātavedo mahi śravaḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-79-4)
In these Ṛks the words सहसस्पुत्र (*sahasasputra*), सहसः सूनु (*sahasaḥ sūnu*) and सहसो यहु (*sahaso yahu*) respectively are used in praising Agni. Therefore, although other deities too give strength, it is Agni in particular who is expressed by the word द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*).
Here let us cite the statements of the Bṛhaddevatā and see to which deity this epithet chiefly belongs and what qualities of that deity are understood from it. The Bṛhaddevatā first gives the explanation of the word अग्नि (*agni*) and then examines why the epithet द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*) is used of Agni.
जातो यदग्रे भूतानां अग्रणीरध्वरे च यत् । नाम्ना संनयते वांगं स्तुतोग्निरिति सूरिभिः ॥ (*jāto yadagre bhūtānāṃ agraṇīradhvare ca yat | nāmnā saṃnayate vāṃgaṃ stutogniriti sūribhiḥ ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 2-24)
Because he was born first of all beings, because he is the leader in sacrifices and the like, and because whatever thing he enters he causes to merge in himself, he is praised by the sages as Agni. This explanation agrees with the explanations stated in the Nirukta, अग्रणीर्भवति अग्रं यज्ञेषु प्रणीयते, अंगं नयति सन्नममानः । (*agraṇīrbhavati agraṃ yajñeṣu praṇīyate, aṃgaṃ nayati sannamamānaḥ |*) (Ni. 7-14). After explaining the word अग्नि (*agni*), it gives the explanation of the word द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*)—
द्रविणं धनं बलं वापि प्रायच्छद्येन कर्मणा । तत्कर्म दृष्ट्वा कुत्सस्तु प्राहैनं द्रविणोदसं ॥ (*draviṇaṃ dhanaṃ balaṃ vāpi prāyacchadyena karmaṇā | tatkarma dṛṣṭvā kutsastu prāhainaṃ draviṇodasaṃ ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 2-25)
Having observed the deeds of Agni, who gives wealth and strength so generously, the seer Kutsa praised Agni as द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*), the giver of wealth (**NOTE**) or of strength.
द्रविणोदा द्रविणसस्तुरस्य द्रविणोदाः सनरस्य प्र यंसत् । द्रविणोदा वीरवतीमिषं नो द्रविणोदा रासते दीर्घमायुः ॥ (*draviṇodā draviṇasasturasya draviṇodāḥ sanarasya pra yaṃsat | draviṇodā vīravatīmiṣaṃ no draviṇodā rāsate dīrghamāyuḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-96-8)

### Printed p. 224
Having indicated Kutsa's praise of Agni in this Ṛk, it then confirms that this statement concerns Agni as deity and is not directed to other deities.
अयं हि द्रविणोदोऽग्निरयं दाता बलस्य हि । जायते च बलेनायं मध्यकृत्यृषिभिरध्वरे ॥ द्रविणोदोऽग्निरेवायं द्रविणोदास्तदोच्यते । आग्नेयेष्टेव दृश्यंते प्रवादा द्रविणोदसः ॥ (*ayaṃ hi draviṇodo 'gnirayaṃ dātā balasya hi | jāyate ca balenāyaṃ madhyakṛtyṛṣibhiradhvare ‖ draviṇodo 'gnirevāyaṃ draviṇodāstadocyate | āgneyeṣṭeva dṛśyaṃte pravādā draviṇodasaḥ ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. 3-61; 3-65 **NOTE**)
Because he is the giver of wealth, the giver of strength, and born of strength, the praise द्रविणोदाः (*draviṇodāḥ*) is fitting for Agni. Therefore the authors of the Bṛhaddevatā have established that this epithet belongs to Agni as deity.
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ दे॒वाना॒मपि॒ पंथा॑मगन्म॒ यच्छ॒क्तवा॑म॒ तदनु॒ प्रवो॑ळ्हुं।  
> अ॒ग्निर्वि॒दान्तन्य य॑जा॒त्सेदु॒ होता॒ सो अ॑ध्व॒रान्तन्य  
> ऋ॒तून्क॑ल्पयाति ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । देवानां । अपि । पंथां । अगन्म । यत् । शक्नवाम । तत् । अनु । प्रवोळ्हुं ।  
> अग्निः । विद्वान् । सः । यजात् । सः । इत् । ऊं इति । होता । सः । अध्वरान् । सः ।  
> ऋतून् । कल्पयाति ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दर्शपूर्णमासातिपत्याद्य निमित्ते पाथिकृतीष्टिः कर्तव्या । तत्रा देवानामित्येषा याज्या । सूत्रितं च । वेत्था हि वेधो अध्वन आ देवानामपि पंथामगन्म ।
> आ. ३.१०! इति ॥ प्रायणीयायामप्येषैव याज्योदयनीयायां सैवानुवाक्या!
> सूत्रितं च । अग्ने नय सुपथा राये अस्माना देवानामपि पंथामगन्म । आ. ४-३।
> इति । विपरीताश्च याज्यानुवाक्या इति ॥
> देवानां देवलोकादिगमनसाधनं देवानां स्वभूतं पंथां पंथानं
> तमपि वैदिकमार्गं वयमागन्न । आगतवंतो भवेम । येन देवान्प्राप्नुमः '

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

When there is a lapse such as the overstepping of the Darśapūrṇamāsa, the Pathikṛt offering (iṣṭi) is to be performed. In it the Ṛk beginning देवानामित्येषा (*devānāmityeṣā*) is the yājyā. It is stated in the sūtra: वेत्था हि वेधो अध्वन (*vetthā hi vedho adhvana*) ... आ देवानामपि पंथामगन्म (*ā devānāmapi paṃthāmaganma*) (Ā. 3.10). In the Prāyaṇīyā too this same Ṛk is the yājyā; in the Udayanīyā it is the anuvākyā. It is stated in the sūtra: अग्ने नय सुपथा राये अस्मान (*agne naya supathā rāye asmāna*) ... आ देवानामपि पंथामगन्म (*ā devānāmapi paṃthāmaganma*) (Ā. 4-3); and the yājyā and anuvākyā are reversed (विपरीताश्च याज्यानुवाक्या इति (*viparītāśca yājyānuvākyā iti*)). The path of the gods is the means of going to the world of the gods and the like, the path that belongs to the gods; may we come to that Vedic way, by which we reach the gods.

> तथा वयं यत्कर्मानुष्ठातुं शक्नवाम शक्नुमः समर्था भवामः तत्कर्मानुक्रमेण प्रवोळ्हुं प्रकर्षेण वोढुं समाप्तिं प्रापयितुं समर्था भवेम! अनंतरं
> विद्वांस्तं पंथानं जानानः सोऽग्निर्यजात् । देवान्यजतु । सेदु स एव खलु
> होता मनुष्याणां होमनिष्पादकः । ततः स एवाग्निरध्वरान्यज्ञान् किंचर्तून्
> कालान्कल्पयाति । कल्पयतु । करोतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the same way, may we be able to carry through to completion, in due sequence, whatever rite we are able to perform. Thereafter let that Agni, knowing the path, worship; let him worship the gods. He indeed is the Hotṛ of men, the accomplisher of the homa. Therefore that same Agni arranges the sacrifices and also the seasons, the times; let him arrange them, let him do so.

### Printed p. 225

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवानां (*devānāṃ*)—of the gods
- पंथां अपि (*paṃthāṃ api*)—the excellent paths
- आ अगन्म (*ā aganma*)—let us follow
- यत् (*yat*)—whatever rite
- शक्नवामु (*śaknavāmu*)—we are able to perform
- तत्अनु (*tatanu*)—that rite
- प्र वोळ्हुं (*pra voḷhuṃ*)—let us be able to carry out well
- विद्वान् (*vidvān*)—knowing the good path
- सः अग्निः (*saḥ agniḥ*)—that same Agni
- यजात् (*yajāt*)—let him worship the gods
- सः इत् (*saḥ it*)—that same Agni
- होता (*hotā*)—the accomplisher of men's sacrifice
- सः (*saḥ*)—that same Agni
- अध्वरान् (*adhvarān*)—the sacrifices
- ऋतून् (*ṛtūn*)—the times of sacrifice
- कल्पयाति (*kalpayāti*)—let him regulate

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Let us follow the excellent paths of the gods. Whatever rite we are able to perform, let us be able to carry that rite out well. That same Agni, who knows the good path, is the accomplisher of men's sacrifice. Let that same Agni regulate the sacrifices and the times of sacrifice.

##### English translation
May we pursue the path that leads to the gods to convey that (worship) which we may be able (to offer). May that wise Agni minister ; for He verily is the ministrant priest, and let him prescribe the ceremonies and the seasons.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In the Darśapūrṇamāsa sacrifices the Pathikṛt iṣṭi is to be performed. Then this Ṛk beginning आ देवानां (*ā devānāṃ*) is to be recited as the yājyā mantra; this is made clear by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, वेत्था हि वेधो अध्वन आ देवानामपि पंथामगन्म (*vetthā hi vedho adhvana ā devānāmapi paṃthāmaganma*) (Ā. 3 10). And in the Prāyaṇīyeṣṭi, in the homa made to Agni, this same Ṛk is to be recited as the yājyā mantra, and in the Udayanīyeṣṭi as the anuvākyā mantra; this is made clear by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, अग्ने नय सुपथा राये अस्माना देवानामपि पंथामगन्म (*agne naya supathā rāye asmānā devānāmapi paṃthāmaganma*) (Ā. 4-3).

### Printed p. 226
…is explained. That is, in the Prāyaṇīya and Udayanīya sacrifices this mantra is to be transposed: in one it is recited as the Yājyā mantra, in the other as the Anuvākyā mantra.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यद्वो॑ व॒यं प्र॑मि॒नाम॑ व्र॒तानि॑ वि॒दुषां॑ देवा॒ अवि॑दुष्टरासः ।  
> अ॒ग्निष्टद्विश्व॒मा पृ॑णाति वि॒द्वान्येभि॑र्दे॒वा ऋ॒तुभिः॑ क॒ल्पया॑ति ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । वः । वयं । प्रमिनाम । व्रतानि । विदुषां । देवाः । अविदुःतरासः ।  
> अग्निः । तत् । विश्वं । आ । पृणाति । विद्वान् । येभिः । देवान् । ऋतुभिः ।  
> कल्पयाति ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> व्रतातिपत्तौ व्रातपतीष्टिः कर्तव्या । तत्र यद्वो वयमित्येषा याज्या ।
> सूत्रितं च । त्वमग्ने व्रतपा असि यद्वो वयं प्रमिनाम व्रतानि । आ. ३-१३ । इति ॥
> हे देवाः अविदुष्टरासोऽविद्वत्तरा अत्यंतमजानंतो वयं युष्माकं
> यत् यत्किंचिद्वतानि कर्माणि विदुषां भवतां जानतामेव प्रमिनाम प्रहिंसितवंतः । भवत्सु जानत्तु नित्यनैमित्तिककर्माणि विलोपितवंत इत्यर्थः । विद्वानेतत्सर्वं
> जानानोऽग्निः स्विष्टकृत्तद्विश्वं सर्वं कर्मजातमा पृणाति । आपूरयतु । फलसहितं
> करोत्वित्यर्थः । केनापूरयतु तत्राह । येभिर्ऋतुभिर्यागयोगैः कालैर्देवान्कल्पयाति कल्पयति कर्मांगभावाय समर्थयति तैरापूरयतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

When a vow has been transgressed, the Vrātapatī offering is to be performed. In it this Ṛk, यद्वो वयं (*yadvo vayaṃ*), is the Yājyā. It is laid down in the Sūtra: त्वमग्ने व्रतपा असि यद्वो वयं प्रमिनाम व्रतानि (*tvamagne vratapā asi yadvo vayaṃ pramināma vratāni*) (Ā. 3-13).
O gods, we who are exceedingly ignorant (अविदुष्टरासः (*aviduṣṭarāsaḥ*) = very unlearned, knowing nothing at all) have violated (प्रमिनाम (*pramināma*) = injured) whatever rites of yours we have violated, while you were knowing them. That is, in your presence, and with you knowing, we have caused the obligatory and occasional rites to lapse. Agni Sviṣṭakṛt, knowing all this, is to fill up (आपूरयतु (*āpūrayatu*)) the whole body of rites, that is, to make it fruitful. By what means? Sāyaṇa answers: by those Ṛtus, the seasons suited to sacrifice, by which he makes the gods fit to take part in the rite (कल्पयाति (*kalpayāti*) = कल्पयति (*kalpayati*), he qualifies them as members of the ritual); by these let him fill up.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवाः (*devāḥ*)—O gods
- अविदुष्टरासः (*aviduṣṭarāsaḥ*)—we, endowed with ignorance
- वः (*vaḥ*)—in regard to you
- यत् व्रतानि (*yat vratāni*)—whatever rites
- विदुषां (*viduṣāṃ*)—as coming within your cognizance
- प्रमिनाम (*pramināma*)—we injure (do not perform)
- विद्वान् (*vidvān*)—knowing these
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni
- येभिः (*yebhiḥ*)—by which
- ऋतुभिः (*ṛtubhiḥ*)—seasons fit for sacrifice
- देवान् (*devān*)—the gods
- कल्पयाति (*kalpayāti*)—makes sharers in the rite
### Printed p. 227
- तत् विश्वं (*tat viśvaṃ*)—all those seasonal rites
- आ पृणाति (*ā pṛṇāti*)—let him carry out and make them fit for fruit

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods, whatever rites we, being ignorant, leave unperformed in your presence in regard to you, let Agni, knowing our ignorance and knowing the whole secret of the rites, make the gods sharers in the rite at the proper seasons and so make all the rites fruitful.

##### English translation
O gods, when we who are most ignorant neglect the offices adderssed to you who are wise, may the wise Agni complete the whole by (appointing) those seasons by which he regulates the worship of the gods.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

That this Ṛk is to be recited as the Yājyā mantra in the Vrātapatī offering is explained by the Sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra त्वमग्ने व्रतसा असि यद्वो वयं प्रमिनाम व्रतानि (*tvamagne vratasā asi yadvo vayaṃ pramināma vratāni*) (Ā. 3-13).**NOTE**
In this Ṛk and the next, Agni is prayed to remedy faults that may arise in a sacrifice through ignorance, such as omission of mantras or of acts, and to bring the sacrifice to a complete conclusion.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यत्पा॑क॒त्रा मन॑सा दी॒नद॑क्षा॒ न य॒ज्ञस्य॑ मन्व॒ते मर्त्या॑सः ।  
> अ॒ग्निष्टद्धोता॑ क्रतु॒विद्वि॑जा॒नन्यजि॑ष्ठो दे॒वा ऋ॑तु॒शो  ॑
> यजाति ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । पाकत्रा । मनसा । दीनदक्षाः । न । यज्ञस्य । मन्वते । मर्त्यासः ।  
> अग्निः । तत् । होता । क्रतुवित् । विजानन् । यजिष्ठः । देवान् । ऋतुशः ।  
> यजाति ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 228

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दीनदक्षा दीनबला दिनोत्साहा वा मर्त्यासो मर्त्या मनुष्या ऋत्विजः पाकत्रा
> पाकेन ॥ बहुलग्रहणादस्मादपि तृतीयार्थे त्राप्रत्ययः ॥ पाकेन पक्तव्येन विशिष्टज्ञानरहितेनाल्वेन वा मनसा युक्ताः संतो यज्ञसंबंधि यत्कर्म न मन्वते न
> जानंति तत्कर्म विजानन् होता देवानामाह्वाता होमनिष्पादको वा क्रतुवित्
> कर्मविद्यजिष्टो देवानामतिशयेन यष्टा सोग्निः स्विष्टकृदृतुशः ऋतावृतौ स्वे
> स्वै यागयोग्ये काले देवान्यजाति । यजतु । हविर्भिः पूजयतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

दीनदक्षा (*dīnadakṣā*) means those of feeble strength or feeble zeal. मर्त्यासः (*martyāsaḥ*) are mortals, men, the priests. पाकत्रा (*pākatrā*) is पाकेन (*pākena*); by the rule of "manifold usage" the affix त्रा (*trā*) is used also after this word in the sense of the instrumental. पाक (*pāka*) here is immature, lacking special knowledge, or small. Being endowed with such a mind, they do not know (न मन्वते (*na manvate*)) whatever act belongs to the sacrifice. Knowing that act is Agni Sviṣṭakṛt: होता (*hotā*), the invoker of the gods or the one who accomplishes the oblation; क्रतुवित् (*kratuvit*), knower of the rite; यजिष्ठः (*yajiṣṭhaḥ*), the greatest sacrificer to the gods. ऋतुशः (*ṛtuśaḥ*) means: in each proper season suited to sacrifice, let him sacrifice to the gods (यजाति (*yajāti*) = यजतु (*yajatu*)), worship them with oblations.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दीनदक्षाः (*dīnadakṣāḥ*)—those of feeble strength
- मर्त्यासः (*martyāsaḥ*)—the human priests
- पाकत्रा (*pākatrā*)—with an immature
- मनसा (*manasā*)—mind, being possessed of it
- यज्ञस्य यत् (*yajñasya yat*)—whatever sacrificial act is to be done
- न मन्वते (*na manvate*)—they do not know
- तत् (*tat*)—that act
- विजानन् (*vijānan*)—knowing
- होता (*hotā*)—in the form of the Hotṛ
- क्रतुवित् (*kratuvit*)—knower of the rite
- यजिष्ठः (*yajiṣṭhaḥ*)—exceedingly worthy of worship
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni
- ऋतुशः (*ṛtuśaḥ*)—according to the respective seasons
- देवान् (*devān*)—the gods
- यजाति (*yajāti*)—let him worship

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The priests, men of feeble strength, being of immature mind, do not know whatever sacrificial act is to be done; knowing each such act, let Agni, in the form of the Hotṛ, knower of the rite and most worthy of worship, worship the gods according to the respective seasons.

##### English translation
Since men of feeble energies, with mind yet immature, are unaquainted with (the ritual of) the sacrifice, may Agni, the invoker (of the gods), the skilled in rites, well knowing (what is to be done), the best offerer, offer worship according to the fit season to the gods.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> विश्वे॑षां॒ ह्य॑ध्व॒राणा॒मनी॑कं चि॒त्रं के॒तुं जनि॑ता त्वा ज॒जान॑।  
> स आ य॑जस्व नृ॒वती॒रनु॒ क्षाः स्पा॒र्हा इषः॑ क्षु॒मती॑र्वि॒श्वज॑न्याः ॥ ६ ॥

### Printed p. 229

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> विश्वेषां । हि । अध्वराणां । अनेकं । चित्रं केतुं । जनिता । त्वा । जजान ।  
> शः । आ । यजस्व । नृवतीः । अनु । क्षाः । स्वाहर्हाः । इषः । क्षुमतीः । विश्वजन्याः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने विश्वेषां सर्वॆषामध्वराणां यज्ञानामनीकं मुखं प्रधानं
> चित्रं चायनीयं नानारूपं केतुं प्रज्ञापकं ता त्वां जनिता जनयिता प्रजापतिर्यजमानो वा जनान । जनयामास । उत्पादितः स त्वं नृवतीर्दासादियुक्ताः
> क्षाः । पृथिवीनामैतत् । क्षयंत्यत्रेति क्षा भूमिः । अन्वा यजस्व । अनुप्रयच्छ ।
> यद्वा । नृवतीर्नृभिः संस्कृतानि क्षा अनु पृथिवीं वेदिलक्षणमनुगतानि वेद्यामासादितानि स्पार्हाः स्पृहणीयानि क्षुमतीः स्तुतिमंत्रलक्षणशब्दवंति विश्वजन्याः सर्वजनहितानि वृष्ट्यन्नहेतुत्वात् नानीषोऽन्नानि हविर्लक्षणानि यजस्व । देवेभ्यः प्रयच्छ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, Prajāpati the begetter, or the sacrificer, has produced you, *anīkaṃ*, the face, the chief, of all sacrifices (अध्वराणां (*adhvarāṇāṃ*) = *yajñānāṃ*); चित्रं (*citraṃ*), to be gathered, of manifold form; केतुं (*ketuṃ*), the one who makes known. Being so produced, do you bestow (अन्वा यजस्व (*anvā yajasva*) = अनुप्रयच्छ (*anuprayaccha*)) नृवतीः क्षाः (*nṛvatīḥ kṣāḥ*), lands with servants and the like. क्षाः (*kṣāḥ*) is a name of the earth: क्षयंत्यत्रेति क्षा भूमिः (*kṣayaṃtyatreti kṣā bhūmiḥ*), "they dwell here, hence क्षा (*kṣā*) is the earth".
Or else: नृवतीः (*nṛvatīḥ*) means things prepared by men; क्षा अनु (*kṣā anu*) means following the earth in the form of the altar, that is, placed upon the altar; स्पार्हाः (*spārhāḥ*), desirable; क्षुमतीः (*kṣumatīḥ*), having sound in the form of praise-mantras; विश्वजन्याः (*viśvajanyāḥ*), beneficial to all people, being the cause of rain and food. Offer इषः (*iṣaḥ*), foods in the form of oblations, to the gods.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Agni) विश्वेषां हि (*viśveṣāṃ hi*)—of all
- अध्वराणां (*adhvarāṇāṃ*)—the sacrifices
- अनेकं (*anekaṃ*)—the chief**NOTE**
- चित्रं (*citraṃ*)—the attractive
- केतुं (*ketuṃ*)—the one who makes known
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- जनिता (*janitā*)—Prajāpati the creator
- जजान (*jajāna*)—produced
- सः (*saḥ*)—such a you
- नृवतीः (*nṛvatīḥ*)—endowed with sons and the like
- क्षाः (*kṣāḥ*)—the earth
- अनु आ यजस्व (*anu ā yajasva*)—bestow
- स्पार्हाः (*spārhāḥ*)—the attractive
- क्षुमतीः (*kṣumatīḥ*)—having sound in the form of praise
- विश्वजन्याः (*viśvajanyāḥ*)—beneficial to all people
- इषः (*iṣaḥ*)—foods; bestow these too

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, Prajāpati the creator has produced you, the chief of all sacrifices, the attractive, the one who makes known. Such as you are, give us the earth endowed with sons and the like. Provide to the gods the oblations that are attractive, possessed of sound in the form of praise, and beneficial to all people.

##### English translation
The progenitor has engendered you the chief of all sacrifices, variegated, illuminating (all); offer (to the gods) the desirable all-sustaining praise-accompanied viands growing upon man-occupied earth.
### Printed p. 230

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

जनिता त्वा जजान (*janitā tvā jajāna*)—प्रजापतिर्यजमानो वा जनयामास (*prajāpatiryajamāno vā janayāmāsa*). Prajāpati (Brahmā) created you, who are Agni; or, you who are in the form of the earthly fire (the sacrificial fire) were brought into being by the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice, who with the help of the priests churned you out of the kindling sticks. This is the meaning.
सः नृवतीः क्षाः अनु यजस्व (*saḥ nṛvatīḥ kṣāḥ anu yajasva*)—स त्वं दासादियुक्ताः भूमिः अनुप्रयच्छ (*sa tvaṃ dāsādiyuktāḥ bhūmiḥ anuprayaccha*). O Agni, provide us with servants and the like and with land; or provide land well cultivated by servants and the like, that is, ploughed, sown and made ready, and rich in plants and the like (fields and paddy-lands).
स्वर्हा इषः क्षुमतीर्विश्वजन्याः (*svarhā iṣaḥ kṣumatīrviśvajanyāḥ*)—provide foods and other nourishment that are beneficial and desirable to all people and prayed for with praises and the like. This is the meaning.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यं त्वा॒ द्यावा॑पृथि॒वी यं त्वाप॒स्वष्टा॒ यं त्वा॑ सु॒जनि॑मा  ॒॑
> जजान ।  
> पंथा॒मनु॑ प्रवि॒द्वान्पि॑तृ॒याणं॑ द्यु॒मद॑ग्ने समिधा॒नो  ॑
> वि भाहि ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यं । त्वा । द्यावापृथिवी इति । यं । त्वा । आपः । त्वष्टा । यं । त्वा । सुजनिमा ।  
> जजान ।  
> पंथां । अनु । प्रविद्वान् । पितृयानं । द्युमत् । अग्ने । संइधानः । वि ।  
> भाहि ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने यं त्वा त्वां द्यावापृथिवी जनितवत्या आदित्यात्मनाग्न्यात्मना ना ।
> तथा यं त्वामापो मेघोदरगता विद्युदात्मना स्थितं जनितवत्यः । यद्वा ! आप

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, the heaven and earth have begotten you, you who are in the form of the sun and in the form of fire. Likewise the waters within the clouds have begotten you who abide in the form of lightning. Or else: आप (*āpa*)
### Printed p. 231

> इत्यंतरिक्षनाम । द्यावापृथिवी अंतरिक्षं चेमे त्रयो लोका यं त्वामजीजनन् ।
> किंच सुजनिमा शोभनजननस्त्वष्टा प्रजापतिर्यं त्वां जजान जनयामास हे अग्ने
> पितृयाणं पितरो येन मार्गेण गच्छंति तं पंथां हविर्वहनमार्गमनु प्रविद्वान् प्रजानंस्त्वं समिधानः समिध्यमानः सन् द्युमद्दीप्तियुक्तं यथा भवति
> तथा वि भाहि । विशेषेण दीप्यसे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

…is a name for the atmosphere; heaven, earth and atmosphere, these three worlds, have begotten you. Moreover, सुजनिमा (*sujanimā*), of good birth, Tvaṣṭṛ, that is Prajāpati, has begotten you. O Agni, knowing well (प्रविद्वान् (*pravidvān*) = *prajānan*) the path (पंथां (*paṃthāṃ*)) of the Pitṛs, the way by which the fathers go, the way that carries the oblation, being kindled (समिधानः (*samidhānaḥ*) = समिध्यमानः (*samidhyamānaḥ*)), shine forth (वि भाहि (*vi bhāhi*)) so that there is brilliance (द्युमत् (*dyumat*)), that is, blaze especially.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- यं त्वा (*yaṃ tvā*)—you whom
- द्यानापृथिवी (*dyānāpṛthivī*)—heaven and earth produced
- यं त्वा (*yaṃ tvā*)—you whom
- अपः (*apaḥ*)—the waters made
- यं त्वा (*yaṃ tvā*)—you whom
- सुजनिमा (*sujanimā*)—the excellent creator
- त्वष्टा (*tvaṣṭā*)—Prajāpati
- जजान (*jajāna*)—created; such as you are
- पितृयाणं (*pitṛyāṇaṃ*)—on which the Pitṛs travel
- पंथां (*paṃthāṃ*)—the excellent path
- अनु प्रविद्वान् (*anu pravidvān*)—knowing it fully
- समिधानः (*samidhānaḥ*)—kindled
- द्युमत् (*dyumat*)—shedding radiance
- वि भाहि (*vi bhāhi*)—shine exceedingly

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, you whom heaven and earth, the waters, and Prajāpati the excellent creator have produced: such as you are, knowing fully the excellent path on which the Pitṛs travel, kindled and shedding radiance, shine exceedingly.

##### English translation
Do you, Agni, whom the earth and heaven, whom the waters, whom Twashtri, the glorious creator, engendered, who are cognizant of the path, the road of the Pitris, shine brilliantly, being kindled.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The commentator has shown that the first half of this Ṛk can be explained in two or three ways. द्यावापृथिवी यं त्वा जजान आपः यं त्वा जजान (*dyāvāpṛthivī yaṃ tvā jajāna āpaḥ yaṃ tvā jajāna*)—द्यावापृथिवी आदित्यात्मनाग्न्यात्मना यं त्वा जनितवत्यौ तथा मेघोदरगता आपः विद्युदात्मना स्थितं त्वां जनितवत्यः । (*dyāvāpṛthivī ādityātmanāgnyātmanā yaṃ tvā janitavatyau tathā meghodaragatā āpaḥ vidyudātmanā sthitaṃ tvāṃ janitavatyaḥ |*). Heaven and earth: that is, heaven produced you, Agni, in heaven in the form of the sun; the earth produced you in the form of the earthly fire; and the waters in the midst of the clouds produced you in the form of lightning (the fire of the lightning-flash). This is one kind of meaning.

### Printed p. 232

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

not applicable here (no bhāṣya block on this page).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

(Continuing.) द्यावापृथिवी अपः यं त्वां (*dyāvāpṛthivī apaḥ yaṃ tvāṃ*) (who have generated): here the word āpaḥ denotes the atmosphere. The three worlds, earth, atmosphere and heaven, each produced Agni in its own world in various forms. This is another way of understanding it.

त्वष्टा यं त्वा सुजनिमा जजान (*tvaṣṭā yaṃ tvā sujanimā jajāna*): although it was said above that heaven and earth produced Agni, the seer speaks this sentence to show that it was Prajāpati who first created Agni. The intention of the sentence is: Prajāpati, the creator of excellent birth, produced you (Agni). Here Tvaṣṭā must be understood as Prajāpati, because the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa says plainly: प्रजापतिरग्निमसृजत (*prajāpatiragnimasṛjata*) (Tai. Brā. 2-1-2-1), "Prajāpati created Agni."

पितृयाणं (*pitṛyāṇaṃ*): the path on which the Pitṛs travel. This word occurs in the Ṛgveda only in the present Ṛk. The word देवयानं (*devayānaṃ*), the path on which the gods travel, occurs in many places. It is necessary to know the nature of these two paths and the difference between them.

In the Upaniṣads the words devayāna and pitṛyāna have a technical meaning. But in the Ṛksaṃhitā, in many places devayāna means simply the path on which the gods travel. Only in a few places is the spiritual sense described in the Upaniṣads also suggested.

In the Upaniṣads the two words denote the two well-known paths on which the eternal Self travels after a man's death, when his body has turned to ashes. Of the paths one goes to enjoy the fruit of good works, one is devayāna and the other pitṛyāna: one is the path of the gods, the other the path of the Pitṛs. Those who know the Pañcāgni-vidyā and have set their faith in truth set out on the path of the gods and reach the Supreme Brahman. After their death the Self enters the flame, and through it the day, the bright fortnight, the northern course of the sun, the year, the sun and lightning; through these, with the help of a non-human person, he attains the Supreme Brahman. This is described in the Upaniṣads.

Those who satisfy the world merely by sacrifice and gifts, that is, who without knowing the Pañcāgni-vidyā are devoted only to good works, travel after death on another path. After the body is burnt the Self joins the smoke, then enters the night, through it the dark fortnight, through that the southern course of the sun, then the world of the Pitṛs, and finally the world of the moon.

### Printed p. 233
They reach it; that is their final place. There, having fully enjoyed the fruits proportionate to their good works in company with the Pitṛs, as soon as the impression of their work is exhausted they return to earth by the same smoke path. Such is the nature of devayāna and pitṛyāna as described in the Upaniṣads.

What we must note here is whether all that is described in the Upaniṣads as above is fully indicated by the word devayāna used in the Ṛksaṃhitā, or whether it means merely the material path on which the gods travel. Likewise, what the word pitṛyāna means must be examined here.

In the Ṛksaṃhitā the word devayāna is used thirteen times in all [1-72-3; 1-162-4; 1-183-6; 1-184-6; 4-37-1; 5-43-6; 7-38-8; 7-16-2; 10-18-1; 10-51-2; 10-51-5; 10-98-11; 10-181-3]. In all these places Sāyaṇa gives mainly two meanings: first, the path by which human sacrificers go with their oblations to approach the gods; second, the path by which the gods come to earth to receive their oblations.

The word pitṛyāna is used only once in the Ṛksaṃhitā, in the present Ṛk:

**Saṃhitā-pāṭha** (as quoted)
यं त्वा द्यावापृथिवी यं त्वापस्त्वष्टा यं त्वा सुजनिमा जजान । पंथामनु प्रविद्वान् पितृयाणं द्युमदग्ने समिधानो वि भाहि ॥ (*yaṃ tvā dyāvāpṛthivī yaṃ tvāpastvaṣṭā yaṃ tvā sujanimā jajāna | paṃthāmanu pravidvān pitṛyāṇaṃ dyumadagne samidhāno vi bhāhi ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-2-7)

In this Ṛk Agni is prayed to: born of heaven and earth, of the waters, of the creator and of Tvaṣṭā, kindled, and fully knowing the nature of the pitṛyāṇa, may he shine as soon as he is kindled. For this word in the third pāda Sāyaṇa explains पितृयाणं पितरो येन मार्गेण गच्छंति तं (*pitṛyāṇaṃ pitaro yena mārgeṇa gacchaṃti taṃ*): the path by which the Pitṛs go.

## Sūkta 3 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इनो राजन्निति सप्तर्चं तृतीयं सूक्तं । ऋष्याद्याः पूर्ववत् । इन
> इत्यनुक्रांतं ॥ प्रातरनुवाकाश्विनशस्त्रयोरुक्तो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"इनो राजन् (*ino rājan*)" is the third sūkta, of seven Ṛks. Seer and the rest as before. The Anukramaṇī says इन (*ina*). Its use in the Prātaranuvāka and the Āśvina-śastra has been stated.

### Printed p. 234
Anuvāda: The sūkta "इनो राजन् (*ino rājan*)" is the third sūkta in the first anuvāka. It has seven Ṛks. Seer, deity and metre are as in the previous sūkta. In the Anukramaṇī it is said इनः (*inaḥ*). Its use for the Prātaranuvāka mantras and the Āśvina-śastra mantras has been stated before.

Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 1; Sūkta 3. Aṣṭaka 7; Adhyāya 5; Varga 31. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 7. Seer: Trita Āptya. Deity: Agni. Metre: Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒नो रा॑जन्नर॒तिः समि॑द्धो॒ रौद्रो॒ दक्षा॑य सुषु॒मां अ॑दर्शि।  
> चि॒किद्वि भा॑ति भा॒सा बृ॑ह॒तासि॑क्मीति॒ रुश॑तीम॒पाज॑न् ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इनः । राजन् । अरति: । संइद्धः । रौद्रः । दक्षाय । सुसुमान् । आदर्शि ।  
> चिकित् । वि । भाति । भासा । बृहता । असिक्नीं . एति । रुशतीं : अप्‌अजन् ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे राजन्वीप्यमानाग्ने त्वमिन ईश्वरः सर्वस्य भवसि, अथ परोक्षः । अरतिर्हविरादाय देवान्प्रति गंता समिद्धः संदीप्तो रौद्रः शत्रूणां भयंकरः सुषुमान् ।
> ओषध्यात्मना स्थितोऽंशुः सुष्टु सूयत इति सुषुः सोमः । तेन तद्वान् शोभनप्रसवो वा सोग्निर्दक्षाय यजमानानां धनानिवृद्ध्यर्थमदर्शि । सर्वैदृश्यते ।
> किंच चिकित् सर्वं जानानोग्निर्वि भाति । विशेषेण दीप्यते । तथा बृहता महता
> भासा तेजसा ज्वालालक्षणेनासिक्कीं रात्रिमेति । सायंहोमसिद्ध्यर्थमेव गच्छति ।
> कं कुर्वन् । रुशतीं श्वेतवर्णां दीप्तिमपाजन्नपगमयन्नात्रिं गच्छति । सामर्थ्यद्रात्रिं परित्यजन्नुषसं प्रातर्होमसिद्धर्थं गच्छतीत्यर्थो लक्ष्यते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O shining Agni, you are lord (ina) of all; the rest is indirect address. Conveyer of the oblation to the gods (arati), kindled, terrible (raudra) to enemies, possessed of Soma (suṣumān): the Soma in the plants, pressed well, is suṣu; or he is of fair birth. That Agni is seen by all for the prosperity of the sacrificers. Knowing all, he shines forth; with great brilliance, of the nature of flame, he goes to the night (asiknī), to accomplish the evening homa. Driving away the white (rushatī) light, he goes into night; by implication, leaving the night he goes to the dawn for the morning homa.

### Printed p. 235

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- राजन् (*rājan*)—O lord Agni
- इनः (*inaḥ*)—you are lord of all
- अरतिः (*aratiḥ*)—who carries the oblation to the gods
- समिद्धः (*samiddhaḥ*)—kindled
- रौद्रः (*raudraḥ*)—terrible to enemies
- सुषुमान् (*suṣumān*)—possessed of Soma; such Agni
- दक्षाय (*dakṣāya*)—for the prosperity of the sacrificers
- अदर्शि (*adarśi*)—is seen by all
- चिकित् (*cikit*)—Agni, knowing all
- वि भाति (*vi bhāti*)—shines exceedingly
- बृहता (*bṛhatā*)—very great
- भासा (*bhāsā*)—with brilliance
- रुशतीं (*ruśatīṃ*)—the white-coloured radiance
- अपाजन् (*apājan*)—driving away
- असिक्नीं (*asiknīṃ*)—the night
- एति (*eti*)—enters

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O lord Agni, you are master of all. Agni, who goes to the gods, is kindled, terrible to enemies and possessed of Soma, is seen by all for the prosperity of the sacrificers. Knowing all, Agni shines exceedingly. With very great brilliance, driving away the white-coloured radiance, he enters the night.

##### English translation
