Royal Agni, (you are) the lord (of all). He who is the conveyer of the oblation, the radiant, the formidable, the recipient of the Soma, is manifested for the benefaction (of the worshipper) : all-knowing, he shines forth with great lustre ; he proceeds, scattering the glimmering darkness.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

सुषुमान् दक्षाय अदर्शि (*suṣumān dakṣāya adarśi*): the commentator gives two meanings of suṣumān: ओषध्यात्मना स्थितोऽंशुः सुष्टु सूयत इति सुषुः सोमः तेन तद्वान् शोभनप्रसवो वा सोग्निः (*oṣadhyātmanā sthito 'ṃśuḥ suṣṭu sūyata iti suṣuḥ somaḥ tena tadvān śobhanaprasavo vā sogniḥ*). The Soma juice obtained by well crushing and squeezing the Soma plant is called suṣu; suṣumān means possessing such Soma. Since Soma is offered into Agni as an oblation to please the gods who love the indirect, Agni is addressed here as suṣumān, possessing Soma. Such Agni, well kindled by the priests and blazing with fuel, shines: this is one meaning. Since in the sacrifice the priests produce Agni by rubbing the aranis according to rule and with mantras, Agni is described as suṣumān, śobhanaprasavaḥ, of excellent birth: another meaning.

बृहता भासा असिक्कीं एति (*bṛhatā bhāsā asikkīṃ eti*)—महता ज्वालालक्षणेन तेजसा रात्रिं गच्छति सायंहोमसिद्ध्यर्थं (*mahatā jvālālakṣaṇena tejasā rātriṃ gacchati sāyaṃhomasiddhyarthaṃ*). Agni, shining with his great splendour, enters, that is approaches, the night to receive the oblations of the evening homas.

### Printed p. 236
Since in the night there is darkness without sunlight, Agni's light is seen all the more. And Agni is the chief deity who receives oblations at night; to the sun the oblation is offered only by day. On this there is an account in the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa of the Yajurveda:

यदग्निहोत्रं । तस्मादग्निहोत्रमुच्यते । तद्दूयमानमादित्योऽब्रवीत् । मा हौषीः । उभयोर्वैनावेतदिति । सोऽग्निरब्रवीत् । कथं नौ होष्यंतीति । सायमेव तुभ्यं जुहवन् । प्रातर्मह्यमित्यब्रवीत् । तस्मादग्नये सायग्ं हूयते। सूर्याय प्रातः । अग्निया वै रात्रिः । ऐंद्रमहः। यदनुदिते सूर्ये प्रातर्जुहुयात् । उभयमेवाग्नेयग्गं स्यात् । उदिते सूर्ये प्रातर्जुहोति । तथाग्नये सायग्ं हूयते.... .... .... अग्निं नावादित्यस्सायं प्रविशति । तस्मादग्निर्दूरान्नक्तं ददृशे । उभे हि तेजसी संपद्येते । उद्यंतं नावादित्यमग्निरनु समारोहति । तस्माद्दूम एवाग्निर्दिवा ददृशे.... ........ .... अग्निर्ज्योतिर्ज्योतिस्सूर्यस्साहेत्येव सायग्ं होतव्यं । सोर्यो ज्योतिर्ज्योतिरग्निस्साहेति प्रातः । तथोभाभ्याग्ं सायग्ं हूयते । उभाभ्यां प्रातः । (*yadagnihotraṃ | tasmādagnihotramucyate | taddūyamānamādityo 'bravīt | mā hauṣīḥ | ubhayorvaināvetaditi | so 'gnirabravīt | kathaṃ nau hoṣyaṃtīti | sāyameva tubhyaṃ juhavan | prātarmahyamityabravīt | tasmādagnaye sāyagṃ hūyate| sūryāya prātaḥ | agniyā vai rātriḥ | aiṃdramahaḥ| yadanudite sūrye prātarjuhuyāt | ubhayamevāgneyaggaṃ syāt | udite sūrye prātarjuhoti | tathāgnaye sāyagṃ hūyate.... .... .... agniṃ nāvādityassāyaṃ praviśati | tasmādagnirdūrānnaktaṃ dadṛśe | ubhe hi tejasī saṃpadyete | udyaṃtaṃ nāvādityamagniranu samārohati | tasmāddūma evāgnirdivā dadṛśe.... ........ .... agnirjyotirjyotissūryassāhetyeva sāyagṃ hotavyaṃ | soryo jyotirjyotiragnissāheti prātaḥ | tathobhābhyāgṃ sāyagṃ hūyate | ubhābhyāṃ prātaḥ |*) (Śai. Brā. 2-1-2-9 to 13)

In former times in the rite called Agnihotra the sacrificer made the homa in Agni; because he made the homa in Agni the rite got the name Agnihotra. To the sacrificer thus offering into Agni with Agni as the object, the Sun said: "You are offering to Agni alone in Agni. The oblation you offer should reach both Agni and me, not Agni alone." Agni asked how it should reach both. The Sun replied: "The homa made in the evening should reach Agni (you); the homa made in the morning should reach me (the Sun)." Since Agnihotra is done in the evening and in the morning, the oblations must reach both Agni and Sun. Therefore at night they offer to Agni; to the Sun they offer in the morning. Thus Agni became the presiding deity of night; all homa made at night must go to Agni. The period between sunset and sunrise is night. Hence a homa made before sunrise (that time being counted as night) goes to Agni. In that case both evening and morning oblations would have gone to Agni.

### Printed p. 237
This did not suit either. Therefore in the evening the Sun enters Agni and is hidden within him; likewise Agni enters the rising Sun and is hidden within him by day. For this reason the homas made in the evening and morning, though the times differ, go to the deities intended. Because each entered the other, the qualities of both became visible in both. At night Agni, very bright, is seen from a great distance; by day, though Agni burns, only the smoke is seen from afar. Since each has entered the other, the order of reciting the mantras addressed to them is also reversed. In both evening and morning the homa must be made addressed to both. But in the evening one must first utter Agni's name, अग्निर्ज्योतिः (*agnirjyotiḥ*), and then recite ज्योतिः सूर्यः (*jyotiḥ sūryaḥ*). In the morning, conversely, first the Sun's name, सूर्यो ज्योतिः (*sūryo jyotiḥ*), and then Agni's, ज्योतिरग्निः (*jyotiragniḥ*), and the homa is made. Thus primacy is given to both at both times, and it is as if Agni were separately addressed in the evening and the Sun in the morning.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> कृ॒ष्णां यदेनी॑म॒भि वर्प॑सा॒ भूज्ज॒नय॒न्नोषां॑ बृह॒तः  ॒
> पितुर्जां ।  
> ऊ॒र्ध्वं भा॒नुं सूर्य॑स्य स्तभा॒यन् दि॒वो वसु॑भिरर॒तिर्वि  ॑
> भाति ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कृष्णां । यत् । एनीं । अभि । वर्पसा । भूत् । जनयन् । योषां । बृहतः ।  
> पितुः । जां ।  
> ऊर्ध्वं । भानुं । सूर्यस्य । स्तभायन् । दिवः । वसुभिः । अरतिः । वि । भाति ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सोग्निर्यद्यदा कृष्णां कृष्णवर्णामेनीं गच्छंतीं रात्रिं वर्पसात्मीयेन
> ज्वालालक्षणेन रूपेणाभि भूत् अभिभवति । किं कुर्वन् । बृहतो महतः पितुः

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

That Agni, when he overcomes (abhi bhūt) with his own form of flame (varpas) the dark (kṛṣṇā) night as she goes (enī); doing what? ….

### Printed p. 238

> सर्वस्य जगतः पालयितुः पितृभूताद्वादित्याज्ञां जायमानां योषामुषसं
> जनयन्नभिव्यंजयन् । तदानीमरतिर्गमनशीलोऽग्निर्दिवो द्युलोकस्य वसुभिर्वासयितृभिराच्छादकैः संधुक्षणसमर्थैरात्मीयैस्तेजोभिः सूर्यस्य भानुं दीप्तिमूर्ध्वमुपरिष्टात् स्तभायन् स्तंभयन् वि भाति । विशेषेण दीप्यते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

(continuing the explanation of the second Ṛk) Agni, as he overcomes the dark night, begets, that is, brings to light, the maiden Uṣas, who is born of the great father, the protector of the whole world. The father is Āditya. At that time Agni, the goer, shines forth variously. With his own radiances, which cover heaven and are able to kindle, he holds the light of the sun back above, in the upper region.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- बृहतः (*bṛhataḥ*)—of the great one
- पितुः (*pituḥ*)—from the protector of the world
- जां (*jāṃ*)—the one born
- योषां (*yoṣāṃ*)—Uṣas
- जनयन् (*janayan*)—making manifest
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- कृष्णां (*kṛṣṇāṃ*)—the black
- एनीं (*enīṃ*)—night
- वर्पसा (*varpasā*)—with his own flame
- अभि भूत् (*abhi bhūt*)—he overcomes (then)
- अरतिः (*aratiḥ*)—Agni, the ranging one
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—of the world of heaven
- वसुभिः (*vasubhiḥ*)—with his own covering radiances
- सूर्यस्य (*sūryasya*)—of the sun
- भानुं (*bhānuṃ*)—the light
- ऊर्ध्वं (*ūrdhvaṃ*)—in the upper region itself
- स्तभायन् (*stabhāyan*)—stopping and holding
- विभाति (*vibhāti*)—shines exceedingly

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Making manifest Uṣas, who was born of the great protector of the world, Agni, the ranging one, overcomes with his flame the black night. Then, with his own radiances that cover the world of heaven, he stops and holds the sun's light in the upper region itself, and shines exceedingly.

##### English translation
When he has overcome the dark departing (night) by his radiance, then, begetting the damsel, the daughter of the great father, he shines in his course with the treasures of heaven, establishing above the light of the sun.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

कृष्णां एनीं वर्षसा अभि भूत् (*kṛṣṇāṃ enīṃ varṣasā abhi bhūt*)—This Agni defeats or overpowers the night, which is full of dense darkness and is passing away, with his radiance in the form of flame. "Black one" here means the night, which begins after sunset. As time passes the darkness spreads thicker and thicker and the deep gloom sets in. At such a time there is no sun to give light, so Agni himself destroys the darkness with his brilliantly shining flames and makes light everywhere. Because he destroys the deep darkness of the night by his light, the seer describes, figuratively, that Agni has defeated the night.

### Printed p. 239
बृहतः पितुः जां योषां जनयन् (*bṛhataḥ pituḥ jāṃ yoṣāṃ janayan*)—"Making manifest the dawn-time created by Prajāpati, or by Āditya, the protector of this great world of moving and unmoving things." The meaning intended is this: while Agni is shining with his radiance in the night, the night gradually passes away and the dawn-time arrives. The seer says that Agni does this work.
अरतिः दिवः वसुभिः सूर्यस्य भानुं ऊर्ध्वं स्तभायन् वि भाति (*aratiḥ divaḥ vasubhiḥ sūryasya bhānuṃ ūrdhvaṃ stabhāyan vi bhāti*)—In the night Agni, with his radiance (flames or rays) spread everywhere, goes on giving light to a great distance in heaven or in the atmosphere. At that time dense darkness has settled and the time of sunrise has not arrived. So the seer describes, figuratively, that Agni stops the sun from rising and himself shines in a special way. In truth the shining of Agni has no connection with the rising of the sun; in due course the sun rises without fail. Until then, that is until that time, Agni shines especially in the all-pervading deep darkness. That the sunrise is delayed in this situation is due to the power of Agni. The seer has described it in this way in order to describe Agni's qualities.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> भ॒द्रो भ॒द्रया॒ सच॑मान॒ आग॒त्स्वसा॑रं जा॒रो अ॒भ्ये॑ति प॒श्चात् ।  
> सु॒प्र॒के॒तैर्द्युभि॑र॒ग्निर्वि॒तिष्ठ॒नुश॑द्भि॒र्वर्णरै॑र॒भि रा॒मम॑स्थात् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> भद्रः । भद्रया । सचमानः । आ । अगात् । स्वसारु । जारः । अभि । एति । पश्चात् ।  
> सुप्रकेतैः । द्युबिः । अग्निः । वितिष्ठन् । रुशत्भिः । वर्णैः । अभि । रामं ।  
> अस्थात् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> भद्रो भजनीयः कल्याणो भद्रया भजनीयया दीप्त्योषसा वा सचमानः
> सेव्यमानः संगच्छमानो वाग्निरागात् । आजगाम।गार्हपत्यादाहवनीयं ततः पश्चाज्जारो जरयिता शत्रूणां सोऽग्निः स्वसारं स्वयंसारिणीमागच्छति भगिनीं वागतामुषसमभ्येति । अभिगच्छति । तथा सुप्रकेतैः सुप्रज्ञानैर्दुर्भिर्दिप्तैस्तेजोभिः सह

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Bhadra is the auspicious, the one to be resorted to. Agni has come, either served by the resortable radiance (bhadrā) or accompanied by Uṣas. He has come from the Gārhapatya to the Āhavanīya. Afterwards that Agni, the jāra, the one who wears away his enemies, goes to his svasāra: either his self-moving (svayaṃsāriṇī) sister, or the Uṣas who has arrived; abhyeti means "goes toward". Likewise he is accompanied by suprakētas, well-known, shining lights.

### Printed p. 240

> विशिष्ठन् सर्वतो वर्तमानः सोग्नी रुशद्भिः श्वेतैर्वण्यार्पारक्षैरात्मीयैस्तेजोभी
> रामं कृष्णं शार्वरं तमोऽभ्यस्थात् । सायंहोमकालेऽभिभूय तिष्ठति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Agni stands everywhere around, with his own white radiances **NOTE**. Rāma, the black one, is the darkness of the night; he overpowers it and stands, at the time of the evening homa.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- भद्रः (*bhadraḥ*)—Agni, the auspicious one
- भद्रया (*bhadrayā*)—with auspicious radiance
- सचमानः (*sacamānaḥ*)—served
- आगात् (*āgāt*)—has come
- पश्चात् (*paścāt*)—afterwards
- चारः (*cāraḥ*)—the destroyer of enemies
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni
- स्वसारं (*svasāraṃ*)—his sister Uṣas
- अभ्येति (*abhyeti*)—approaches
- सुप्रकेतैः (*supraketaiḥ*)—with excellent, informing
- द्युभिः (*dyubhiḥ*)—lights
- वितिष्ठन् (*vitiṣṭhan*)—spreading everywhere
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni
- रुशद्भिः (*ruśadbhiḥ*)—with white
- वर्णैः (*varṇaiḥ*)—radiances
- रामुं (*rāmuṃ*)—the darkness of the night
- अभि अस्थात् (*abhi asthāt*)—has stood overpowering it

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Agni, the auspicious one, has come, served by auspicious radiance. Afterwards Agni, the destroyer of enemies, approaches his sister Uṣas. Agni, who spreads everywhere with excellent, informing lights, stands overpowering the darkness of the night with white radiances.

##### English translation
He has come auspicious, waited on by the auspicious (light); he afterwards approaches his sister (dawn) like a gallant : Agni, spreading everywhere, with omniscient rays overpowers the dark (night) with brilliant beams.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The auspicious Agni, who is served, comes with his auspicious radiance and approaches his sister Uṣas. That is, while Agni shines with his own radiance throughout the night, the night gradually passes and the dawn-time draws near. To indicate this it is said that Agni approaches Uṣas. Agni and the night are related; the Vedic sentence आग्नेया वैरात्रि (*āgneyā vairātri*) supports this. Because Agni shines especially at night, there is a close connection between Agni and night. At the end of the night the dawn-time appears, so the dawn-time is, as it were, born of the night. Thus both Agni and the dawn-time are connected with the night, since both are born in the night-time, and it is proper to call the dawn-time Agni's sister (the younger one). Agni, who shines in the night in this way, shines brilliantly with his radiances, which destroy the deep darkness of the night and cast light. That is the main idea.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒स्य यामा॑सो बृह॒तो न व॒ग्नूनिंधा॑ना अ॒ग्नेः सख्युः॑  ॒॑
> शिवस्य ।  
> ईड्य॑स्य॒ वृष्णो॑ बृह॒तः स्वासो॒ भामा॑सो॒ याम॑न्न॒क्तव॑श्चिकिते॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अस्य । यामासः । बृहते । न । वग्नून् । इंधानाः । अग्नेः । सख्युः । शिवस्य ।  
> ईड्यस्य । वृष्णः । बृहतः । सुआसः । भामासः । यामन् । अक्तवः । चिकित्से ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> बृहतो महतोऽस्याग्नेः संबंधिन इंधाना दीप्यमाना यामासः । यांति
> गच्छंतीति यामा रश्मयः । नवग्नून् स्तुतिकारिणो जनान्न बाधंते । किंतु सख्यु:
> स्तुत्यस्तोतृत्वयष्टृयष्टव्यव्यत्वलक्षणेन सख्येन सखिभूतस्य शिवस्य कल्याणस्य भक्तानां
> सुखकरस्येड्यस्य स्तोतव्यस्य वृष्णः कामानां वर्षितुर्बृहतो महतः स्नासः शोभनास्यस्यास्याग्नेः स्वभूता अक्तवस्तमांस्यंजंतोऽपगमयंत आहुतिभिः संगता वा
> भामासः ॥ भाम क्रोधे ॥ तीक्ष्णा अप्रसह्या रश्मयो यामन् . याति देवान्प्रति
> तर्पणाय यांति देवा एतं प्रत्यंगभावायेति यामा यज्ञः . तस्मिंश्चिकित्रॆ ।
> सर्वतो जज्जिरे । प्रथिता अभूवन् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The kindled, shining yāmāsas of this great Agni are his rays: "they go (yānti)". They do not harm the vagnus, the praisers. They are rather those of Śiva, the auspicious one, who is a friend through the friendship characterised by the relation of praised and praiser, worshipped and worshipper, the pleasant one to his devotees, the one to be praised, the showerer of desires, the great one, the well-mouthed (svāsaḥ: of beautiful mouth) Agni. These rays, his own, are akta: they dispel the darkness (aṅj), or else they are joined with the oblations. They are bhāmāsaḥ. The root bhām is used in the sense of anger: the rays are sharp and irresistible. In the yāman, the sacrifice, they are chikitre, that is, they have become famous, spread, everywhere. Yāman is explained in two ways: the sacrifice goes toward the gods for their satisfaction, or the gods go to it in order to become its limbs (प्रत्यंगभावाय (*pratyaṃgabhāvāya*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- बृहतः (*bṛhataḥ*)—of the great one
- अस्य अग्ने: (*asya agne:*)—of this Agni
- इंधानाः (*iṃdhānāḥ*)—the shining
- यामासः (*yāmāsaḥ*)—rays
- वग्नून् (*vagnūn*)—the praisers
- न (बाधंते) (*na (bādhaṃte)*)—do not hurt
- सख्युः (*sakhyuḥ*)—of the friend
- शिवस्य (*śivasya*)—the auspicious
- ईड्यस्य (*īḍyasya*)—the worthy of praise
- वृष्णः (*vṛṣṇaḥ*)—the showerer of desires
- बृहतः (*bṛhataḥ*)—the great
- स्वासः (*svāsaḥ*)—the one with an auspicious mouth, Agni's
- अक्तवः (*aktavaḥ*)—joined with the oblations
- भामासः (*bhāmāsaḥ*)—rays
- यामन् (*yāman*)—in the sacrifice
- चिकित्से (*cikitse*)—are known by all

### Printed p. 243

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The shining rays of this great Agni do not hurt the praisers. The rays of Agni, who is a friend, auspicious, worthy of praise, the showerer of desires, great, and of auspicious mouth, joined with the oblations, are known by all in the sacrifice.

##### English translation
The blazing flames of that mighty Agni do not (deter) his adorers ; the fierce flames of the friendly auspicious adorable (Agni), the showerer of (benefits), the vast, the strong-mouthed, are visible in the sacrifice.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

यामासः (*yāmāsaḥ*)—यांति गच्छंतीति यामा रश्मयः (*yāṃti gacchaṃtīti yāmā raśmayaḥ*). Because they go out from Agni and spread around, the yāmāḥ are the rays.
वग्नून् न (*vagnūn na*)—स्तुतिकारिणो जनान् न बाधंते (*stutikāriṇo janān na bādhaṃte*). The fierce rays of Agni, or his burning power, are capable of doing harm to enemy people, but they cause no hurt to the people who praise Agni. The seer says this because there is a kind of friendship between Agni and the people who praise him.
सख्युः शिवस्य (*sakhyuḥ śivasya*)—What is the friendship between Agni and the people? It is स्तुत्यस्तोतृत्वयष्टृयष्ट. व्यत्वलक्षणेन सख्येन! (*stutyastotṛtvayaṣṭṛyaṣṭa. vyatvalakṣaṇena sakhyena!*). There is a kind of relation between Agni and the people. Agni is the one who is praised; the people praise Agni. This relation is called that of praised and praiser. In the same way, since the people worship Agni with oblations and the like, there is also the relation of worshipped and worshipper. The seer describes this relation as the friendship between Agni and those who praise him.
भामासः (*bhāmāsaḥ*)—भाम क्रोधे । तीक्ष्णा अप्रसह्या रश्मयः (*bhāma krodhe | tīkṣṇā aprasahyā raśmayaḥ*). Rays of the nature of anger, that is, sharp rays that cannot be borne. When Agni burns with great flames, no one can go near him. The meaning is: such fierce rays of Agni.
यामन् (*yāman*)—याति देवान्प्रति तर्पणाय यांति देवा एतं प्रत्यंगभावायेति यामा यज्ञः . (*yāti devānprati tarpaṇāya yāṃti devā etaṃ pratyaṃgabhāvāyeti yāmā yajñaḥ .*). Two explanations are given. Yāman is the sacrifice because it satisfies the gods with the offering of oblations; or yāman is the sacrifice because the gods themselves, invited, go to this place of sacrifice for their own pleasure.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स्व॒ना न यस्य॒ भामा॑सः॒ पवं॑ते॒ रोच॑मानस्य बृह॒तः सु॒दिवः॑।  
> ज्येष्ठे॑भि॒र्यस्तेजि॑ष्टैः क्रीळु॒मद्भि॒र्वर्षि॑ष्टेभिर्भा॒नुभि॒र्नक्ष॑ति॒  
> द्यां ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> स्वनाः । न । यस्य । भामासः । पवंते । रोचमानस्य ! बृहतः । सुदिवः ।  
> ज्येष्ठेभिः । यः । तेजिस्थैः । क्रीळुमत् 5भिः । वर्षिष्ठेभिः । भानुभिः । नक्षति ! द्यां"

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> रोचमानस्य दीप्यमानस्य बृहतो महतः सुदिवः शोभनदीप्तेर्वा
> यस्याग्नेः स्वभूता भामासः ॥ भा दीप्ता ॥ दीप्ता रश्मयः स्वना न स्वनंतो मरुत
> इव पवंते सर्वतः शब्दं कुर्वंतो गच्छंति। किंच योऽग्निर्जैष्टेभिरत्यंतं प्रशस्त्यस्तेजिष्ठैस्तेजिस्वितमैः क्रीळुमद्भिः क्रीडनवद्धिर्वर्षिष्टेभिर्वृद्धतमैर्भानुभिरात्मीय्यैस्तेजोभिदार्क्यं द्युलोकं नक्षति व्याप्नोतीति । सत्वं देवानावहेत्युत्तरेण
> संबंधः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Of Agni, who is shining, great, and su-div (of fair radiance), the own rays are bhāmāsaḥ: "भा (*bhā*)" is shining. The shining rays pavante, that is, go everywhere making a noise, "like the roaring Maruts" (स्वना न स्वनंतो मरुत इव (*svanā na svanaṃto maruta iva*)). Moreover, that Agni pervades the world of heaven with his own rays, which are the most excellent, most lustrous, sportive, and most grown. The sentence is connected with the later words सत्वं देवानावह (*satvaṃ devānāvaha*) ("may he bring the gods").

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- रोचमानस्य (*rocamānasya*)—of him who is radiant
- बृहतः (*bṛhataḥ*)—of great form
- सुदिवः (*sudivaḥ*)—of excellent splendour
- यस्य (*yasya*)—whose Agni's
- भामासः (*bhāmāsaḥ*)—rays
- स्वना न (*svanā na*)—like the Maruts
- पवंते (*pavaṃte*)—move about making a noise everywhere
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Agni
- ज्येष्ठेभिः (*jyeṣṭhebhiḥ*)—with the most praiseworthy
- तेजिस्थैः (*tejisthaiḥ*)—radiant
- क्रीळुमद्भिः (*krīḷumadbhiḥ*)—sportive
- वर्षिष्ठेभिः (*varṣiṣṭhebhiḥ*)—most excellent
- भानुभिः (*bhānubhiḥ*)—with rays
- द्यां (*dyāṃ*)—the world of heaven
- नक्षति (*nakṣati*)—pervades (may that Agni bring the gods)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Whose rays, of the radiant, great, and fair-splendoured Agni, move about like the Maruts, making a noise everywhere, and who pervades the world of heaven with his most praiseworthy, radiant, sportive, and most increasing rays, may that Agni bring the gods.

### Printed p. 244

##### English translation
The flames of whom, radiant, vast, bright-shining, go roaring like the winds; who covers the sky with most excellent, most lustrous, most sportive, and most mighty rays ;—

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk and the next, only the qualities of Agni are described and there is no sentence to complete the sense. So they must be connected with the sentence in the seventh Ṛk of this sūkta, स आ वक्षि महि नः (*sa ā vakṣi mahi naḥ*): "bring, such as you are, the greatly glorious gods to our sacrifice".
स्वनाः न (*svanāḥ na*)—स्वनंतो मरुत इव (*svanaṃto maruta iva*). As the blowing wind makes a noise, so the description here is of Agni making a crackling noise of a kind when he consumes wood and the like.
ज्येष्ठभिः यः तेजिस्थैः क्रीळुमद्भिः वर्षिष्ठेभिः भानुभिः द्यां नक्षति (*jyeṣṭhabhiḥ yaḥ tejisthaiḥ krīḷumadbhiḥ varṣiṣṭhebhiḥ bhānubhiḥ dyāṃ nakṣati*)—It is well known that heaven and the sky are at a very great height. The very radiant rays or flames of Agni rise very high, appear to be playing, and touch heaven, which is at that great height. That is, they spread to a very great height. That is the meaning.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒स्य शुष्मा॑सो ददृशा॒नप॑वे॒र्जॆह॑मानस्य स्वनयन्नि॒युद्धिः॑ ।  
> प्र॒त्नेभि॒र्यो रुश॑द्भीर्दे॒वत॑मो॒ वि रेभ॑द्धिरर॒तिर्भाति॒ विभ्वा॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अस्य । शुष्मासः । ददृशानपवेः । जेहमानस्य । स्वनयन् । नियुत् 5भिः ।  
> प्रत्येभिः । यः । रुशत् 5भिः । देवतमः । वि । रेभत् 5भिः । अरतिः । भाति । विभ्वा ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ददृशानपवेर्दर्शनीयज्वालाग्नेः । यद्वा । पनिर्वज्र आयुधं । दृश्यमानायुधस्य । जेहमानस्य । जेहतिर्गतिकर्मा । हविरादाय देवान्प्रति गच्छतोऽस्याग्नेः
> स्वभूताः शुष्मासः शोषका नियुद्भिः । नियुतो वायोरिति वायोरश्वा नियुतः ।
> तमुक्कैर्वायुभिः संयुक्ता रश्मयः स्वनयन् । शब्दायंते । किंच देवतमो

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

ददृशानपवेः (*dadṛśānapaveḥ*) means: of the Agni whose flames are worth beholding. Or else: pavi is the vajra, a weapon, so "of him whose weapon is seen". जेहमानस्य (*jehamānasya*): the root jehati is a verb of motion, so "of this Agni, who goes with the oblation toward the gods". His own śuṣmāsaḥ, the drying rays, with the niyuts: "niyuts" are the horses of Vāyu (नियुतो वायोरिति (*niyuto vāyoriti*)). The rays, united with the winds **NOTE**, svanayan, that is, make a noise. Moreover, devatama:

### Printed p. 245

> देवानां मुख्योऽरतिर्गंता विभ्वा विभवनशीलो महान् योऽग्निः प्रत्नेभिः
> पुराणै रुशद्भिः श्वेतवर्णॆ रेभद्धिः शब्दायमानैस्तेजोभिर्वि भाति विविधं दीप्यते ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

(continuing) Devatama is the chief of the gods; arati is the goer; vibhvā is the mighty one who prevails. The Agni who, with ancient (pratnebhiḥ: of old), white (ruśadbhiḥ), and noisy (rebhadbhiḥ) radiances, vi bhāti, shines variously.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ददृशानपवे? (*dadṛśānapave?*)—of him who has flames visible to all
- जेहमानस्य (*jehamānasya*)—of him who goes to the gods taking the oblation
- अस्य (*asya*)—Agni's
- शुष्मासः (*śuṣmāsaḥ*)—drying rays
- नियुद्भिः (*niyudbhiḥ*)—with the winds that are joined with horses
- स्वनयन् (*svanayan*)—make a noise
- देवतमः (*devatamaḥ*)—the chief of all the gods
- अरतिः (*aratiḥ*)—the ranging one
- विभ्वा (*vibhvā*)—the great one
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which same Agni
- प्रत्नेभिः (*pratnebhiḥ*)—ancient
- रुशद्भिः (*ruśadbhiḥ*)—white
- रेभद्धिः (*rebhaddhiḥ*)—noisy
- वि भाति (*vi bhāti*)—shines exceedingly (with radiances)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The drying rays of Agni, who has flames visible to all and goes to the gods taking the oblation, make a noise with the winds joined with horses. That same Agni, the chief of all the gods and the great one, shines exceedingly with ancient, white, and noisy radiances.

##### English translation
Of him, bright blazing and hastening (toward the gods), the fierce-burning flames roar with the horses (of the Maruts) : who shines mighty in his course, most divine. with ancient, brilliant, and loud-sounding (flames).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

ददृशानपवेः (*dadṛśānapaveḥ*)—दर्शनीयज्वालाग्नेः (*darśanīyajvālāgneḥ*). The flames of Agni are bright so as to be seen by all. The meaning is: Agni, who is possessed of such flames. Or else पनिर्वज्र आयुधं दृश्यमानायुधस्य (*panirvajra āyudhaṃ dṛśyamānāyudhasya*) **NOTE**. Pavi means the thunderbolt weapon, or a weapon. Here Agni's weapon is his flames, which are seen by all. In sum, the intent of the two meanings is the same.
नियुद्भिः स्वनयन् (*niyudbhiḥ svanayan*)—Niyut means the horse of Vāyu. The meaning is: making a noise together with the wind accompanied by horses, that is, the wind blowing swiftly. The intention is that the more the wind blows, the more Agni burns.

### Printed p. 246
#### Ṛk 3 (continuing)

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स आ व॑क्षि॒ महि॑ न॒ आ च॑ सत्सि दि॒वस्पृ॑थि॒व्योर॑र॒तियु॑र्व॒त्योः ।  
> अ॒ग्निः सु॒तुकः॑ सु॒तुके॑भि॒रश्चै॒ रभ॑स्वद्भि॒ रभ॑स्वा॒ एह ग॑म्याः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । आ । वक्षि । महि । नः । आ । च । सत्सि । दिवःपृथिव्योः । अरतिः । युवत्योः ॥  
> अग्निः । सुतुकः । सुतुकेभिः । अश्वैः । रभस्वत् 5भिः । रभस्वान् । आ । इह । गम्याः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने स तादृशस्त्वं नोऽस्मदीये यज्ञे महि महान् देवाना वक्षि ।
> आवह । प्रापय । किंच युवत्योः परस्परं मिश्रितयोस्तरुण्योर्वा दिवस्पृथिव्योर्दावापृथिव्योर्मध्यॆऽरतिरग्निः सूर्यात्मना गंता त्वमा सप्सि! अस्माकं यज्ञमासीद । तथा सुतुकः । तुकिगर्त्यर्थः । सुगमः स्तोतृभिर्यष्टृभिश्च सुखेन
> प्राप्तव्यू रभस्वान्वेगवानग्निरंगनादिगुणयुक्तस्त्वं सुतुकेभिः सुगमै रभस्वद्धिर्वेगवद्भिरश्वै रोहिदाख्यैः सहेहास्मदीये यज्ञ आ गम्याः । आगच्छ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, being such, bring the great gods to our sacrifice, make them reach it. Further, you who move as the sun between the two young, mutually mingled heaven and earth (or, "the two youthful ones"), come and sit at our sacrifice. Likewise सुतुकः (*sutukaḥ*) (the root तुकि (*tuki*) has the sense of going) means "easy to approach", easily reached by singers and sacrificers. You, Agni, the swift one, endowed with qualities such as limbs, come to our sacrifice with your easy-going, swift horses called Rohits.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सः (*saḥ*)—you, the famous one (O Agni)
- नः (*naḥ*)—to our sacrifice
- महि (*mahi*)—the great gods
- आ वक्षि (*ā vakṣi*)—bring
- युवत्योः (*yuvatyoḥ*)—of the two that are mingled with each other
- दिवस्पृथिव्योः (*divaspṛthivyoḥ*)—in heaven and earth everywhere
- अरतिः (*aratiḥ*)—you who move about in the form of the sun
- आ सत्सि (*ā satsi*)—come to our sacrifice
- सुतुकः (*sutukaḥ*)—worthy of easy approach
- रभस्वान् (*rabhasvān*)—swift
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni
- सुतुकेभी (*sutukebhī*)—having excellent gait
- रभस्वद्भिः (*rabhasvadbhiḥ*)—swift
- अश्वैः (*aśvaiḥ*)—together with horses
- इह (*iha*)—to our sacrifice
- आ गम्याः (*ā gamyāḥ*)—may he come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, you, the famous one, bring the great gods to our sacrifice. You who move as the sun in all the mutually joined heaven and earth, come to our sacrifice. May Agni, who is easy of approach and swift, come to our sacrifice with horses of excellent gait and swiftness.

### Printed p. 247

##### English translation
Great Agni, bring the gods to our ( sacrifice ). The messenger of the young heaven and earth, sit down ( at our sacrifice ). Agni, who are easily found, who are rapid in movement come with your docile and rapid steeds (to our rite).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

युवत्योः दिवस्पृथिव्योः (*yuvatyoḥ divaspṛthivyoḥ*)—"in the middle of the mutually mingled, or the two young, heaven and earth": between heaven and earth, which seem joined to one another. Since the earth is below and the heaven-world above, they are as if related to each other. Between them Agni, in the form of the sun, is described as moving. The explanation of युवत्योः (*yuvatyoḥ*) as "of the two young ones" (तरुण्योर्वा (*taruṇyorvā*)) does not seem very suitable.

सुतुकेभिः रभस्वद्भिः अश्वैः (*sutukebhiḥ rabhasvadbhiḥ aśvaiḥ*)—the meaning is: accompanied by the horses called Rohit, which have excellent gait or stride and are swift. Yāska, describing the vehicles of various deities, says:
हरी इंद्रस्य, रोहितोग्नेः! हरित आदित्यस्य । रासभावश्विनोः अजा पूष्णः । पृषत्यो मरुतां । आरुण्यो गाव उषसः । श्यावा सवितुः । विश्वरूपा बृहस्पतेः । नियुतो वायोरिति दशादिष्टोपयोजनानि ॥ (*harī iṃdrasya, rohitogneḥ! harita ādityasya | rāsabhāvaśvinoḥ ajā pūṣṇaḥ | pṛṣatyo marutāṃ | āruṇyo gāva uṣasaḥ | śyāvā savituḥ | viśvarūpā bṛhaspateḥ | niyuto vāyoriti daśādiṣṭopayojanāni ‖*) (6-24 **NOTE**)
That is: the two horses named Hari are Indra's vehicle; the red horse Rohita is Agni's; green horses are the Āditya's; two asses are the Aśvins'; a she-goat is Pūṣan's; spotted deer are the Maruts'; red cows are Uṣas's; reddish horses are Savitṛ's; horses of various colours are Bṛhaspati's; the swift horses called Niyut are Vāyu's. Thus he has described the vehicles of the several deities. In the present Ṛk, it should be understood, the Rohit horses, Agni's vehicles, are meant.
For the words सुतुकः (*sutukaḥ*) and सुतुकेभिः (*sutukebhiḥ*) in this Ṛk Yāska gives the etymology:
सुतुकः । अग्निः सुतुकः सुतुकनः सुतुकनैरिति वासुप्रजाः सुप्रजोभिरिति वा ॥ (*sutukaḥ | agniḥ sutukaḥ sutukanaḥ sutukanairiti vāsuprajāḥ suprajobhiriti vā ‖*) (Ni. 4-18)

### Printed p. 248
That is: सुतुकः (*sutukaḥ*) is the swift Agni. If Agni begins to burn, he very quickly spreads over everything and burns it; hence he has very rapid movement. His horses too are swift; or they are of good breed, that is, good thoroughbreds, famed for running fast. This is the intention of the etymology.

## Sūkta 4 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्र त इति सप्तर्चं चतुर्थं सूक्तं । ऋष्याद्याः पूर्ववत् । प्र त इत्यनुक्रांतं ॥ प्रातरनुवाकाश्विनशस्त्रयोरुक्तो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

प्र ते (*pra te*) is a hymn of seven Ṛks, the fourth sūkta. Seer and the rest as before. The Anukramaṇī says प्र ते (*pra te*). The use (viniyoga) in the Prātaranuvāka and the Āśvina-śastra has been stated.

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta प्र ते (*pra te*) is the fourth sūkta in the first anuvāka. It has seven Ṛks. The seer, deity and metre are the same as in the previous sūkta. The Anukramaṇī says प्र ते (*pra te*). It was said earlier that the sūkta is used for the Prātaranuvāka and the Āśvina-śastra mantras.

Sūkta 4; Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 1; Aṣṭaka 7; Adhyāya 5; Varga 32. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 7. Seer: Trita Āptya. Deity: Agni. Metre: Triṣṭubh.
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र ते॑ यक्षि॒ प्र त॑ इयर्मि॒ मन्म॒ भुवो॒ यथा॒ वंद्यो॑ नो॒  ॑
> हवेषु ।  
> धन्व॑न्निन प्र॒पा अ॑सि॒ त्वम॑ग्न इय॒क्षवे॑ पू॒रवे॑ प्रश्न राजन् ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । ते । यक्षि । प्र । ते । इयर्मि । मन्म । भुवः । यथा । वंद्यः । नः । हवेषु ॥  
> धन्वन् इव । प्रपा । असि । त्वं । आग्ने । इयक्षवे । पूरवे । प्रत्य । राजन् ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 249

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने ते तुभ्यं हविः प्र यक्षि । प्रयच्छामि । यजिर्वानार्थः । तथा मन
> मननीयां स्तुतिं ते त्वदर्थं प्रेयर्मि । प्रेरयामि । प्रोच्चारयामि : वंद्यः
> सर्वैर्वंदनीयस्त्व नोऽस्मदीयेषु हवेषु देवाह्वानेषु यथा त्वं संनिहितो
> भुवः भवसि तथा हविः प्रयच्छामि स्वामि च । हे प्रश्नॆ पुराण राजन् सर्वस्य जगतः
> स्वामिन् हे अग्ने स त्वमियक्षवे यष्टु मिच्छते पूरवे पूरुरिति मनुष्यनाम ।
> मनुष्याय यजमानाय हविर्भिर्देवान्पूरयित्रे जनाय धन्वन्निव प्रपासि ।
> यथा धन्वन् धन्वनि मरा निरुदकप्रदेशे प्रपा । प्रपिबंत्यत्रेति प्रपा । सा यथोदकप्रदानेन जनेभ्यः सुखदा भवति एवं त्वं धनदानेन तस्मै सुखदाता भवसि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, I offer the oblation to you (the root यजि (*yaji*) is here in the sense of giving), and I send forth, utter, a thoughtful praise for you. You who are worthy of homage by all, as you are present at our invocations of the gods, so I offer the oblation. O ancient king, lord of the whole world, you are to the man पूरवे (*pūrave*) (*pūruḥ* is a word for man), the sacrificer who wishes to sacrifice and fills the gods with oblations, like a प्रपा (*prapā*) in a waterless desert place (प्रपा (*prapā*): where people drink). As it gives happiness to people by giving water, so you give happiness to him by giving wealth.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यथा (*yathā*)—in what way
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- हवेषु (*haveṣu*)—at the time of invocation
- वंद्य (*vaṃdya*)—you, the venerable
- भुवः (*bhuvaḥ*)—you are near us; in that manner
- ते (*te*)—to you
- प्रयक्षि (*prayakṣi*)—I offer the oblation
- मन्म (*manma*)—meditative praise
- ते (*te*)—to you
- प्र इयर्मि (*pra iyarmi*)—I offer
- प्रश्नॆ (*praśnè*)—ancient
- राजन् (*rājan*)—lord
- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- इयक्षवे (*iyakṣave*)—to one wishing to sacrifice
- पूरवे (*pūrave*)—to the sacrificer
- धन्वन् (प्रप्ना) इव (*dhanvan (prapnā) iva*)—like a spring supplying water in a sandy desert
- प्रपा असि (*prapā asi*)—you are the fulfiller of desired ends

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, as you remain near us at the time when we invite the gods to the sacrifice, so I offer you the oblation and also offer meditative praise. O ancient lord Agni, to the sacrificer who wishes to sacrifice, you are the fulfiller of desired ends, like a spring supplying water in a sandy desert.

##### English translation
To you, Agni, I present the oblation : to you I repeat praise ; that
you, who are deserving of laudation, may be present at our invocations :
ancient sovereign, Agni, you are like a well in a desert, to the man desirous
of offering sacrifice.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

धन्वन्निव प्रपा असि—यथा मर्ौ निरुदकप्रदेशे प्रपा प्रपिबंत्यत्रेति प्रपा । सा यथोदकप्रदानेन जनेभ्यः सुखदा भवति एवं त्वं धनदानेन तस्मै सुखदाता (*dhanvanniva prapā asi—yathā marau nirudakapradeśe prapā prapibaṃtyatreti prapā | sā yathodakapradānena janebhyaḥ sukhadā bhavati evaṃ tvaṃ dhanadānena tasmai sukhadātā*)

### Printed p. 250
भवसि (*bhavasi*). प्रपा (*prapā*) means a reservoir such as a well that is convenient for drinking water. When a man travelling in a waterless desert, exhausted by thirst, finds a reservoir such as a well convenient for drinking, he is satisfied and happy; likewise, O Agni, be to the sacrificing yajamāna one who gives happiness, like a reservoir in the desert. Thus the quality of Agni is described by the illustration of the word प्रपा (*prapā*).
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यं त्वा॒ जना॑सो अ॒भि सं॒चरं॑ति॒ गाव॑ उ॒ष्णमि॑व व्र॒जं य॑विष्य ॥  
> दू॒तो दे॒वाना॑मसि॒ मर्त्या॑नामं॒तर्म॒हाश्च॑रसि रोच॒नेन॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यं । त्वा । जनासः । अभि । संचरंति । गावः । उष्णं इव । व्रजं । यविष्ठ ॥  
> दूतः । देवानां । असि । मर्त्यानां । अंतः । महान् । चरसि । रोचनेन ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे यविष्ठ युवतमाग्ने यं त्वा त्वां जनासो जना यजमानाः फलप्राप्त्यर्थमभि संचरंति अभितः परिचरंति सेवंते । तत्र दृष्टांतः । गाव उष्णमिव यथा गानः शीतार्ता उष्णं शीतजनितदुःखापनोदनार्थमुष्णं व्रजं गोष्ठमभि गच्छंति तद्वत् । स त्वं देवानामिंद्रादीनां मर्त्यानां मनुष्याणां च
> दूतोऽसि हविष्टापणद्वारा । ततो महांस्त्वमंतर्दावापृथिव्योर्मध्यॆ हवींष्यादाय रोचनेनांतरिक्षेण लोकेन चरसि । गच्छसि । तं त्वां स्तुम इति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Yaviṣṭha, youngest Agni, the people, the sacrificing yajamānas, go about you on all sides, serve you, for the attainment of fruit. The illustration: as cattle afflicted by cold go to the warm व्रजं (*vrajaṃ*), the cattle-pen, to remove the pain caused by cold. You are the messenger of the gods, Indra and others, and of mortals, by conveying the oblations. Therefore you, the great one, go between heaven and earth, taking the oblations, by the luminous region, the atmosphere. "We praise you" is to be supplied.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यविष्ठ (*yaviṣṭha*)—O Agni, the exceedingly young
- गावः (*gāvaḥ*)—cattle (afflicted by cold)
- उष्णं (*uṣṇaṃ*)—warm
- व्रजं इव (*vrajaṃ iva*)—like taking shelter in a cattle-pen
- यं त्वा (*yaṃ tvā*)—you, the famous
- जनासः (*janāsaḥ*)—the yajamānas
- अभि संचरंति (*abhi saṃcaraṃti*)—serve all around
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- देवानां (*devānāṃ*)—of the gods, Indra and others
- मर्त्यानां (*martyānāṃ*)—of men
- दूतः असि (*dūtaḥ asi*)—you are the messenger
- महान् (*mahān*)—you, the great
- अंतः (*aṃtaḥ*)—between heaven and earth
- रोचनेन (*rocanena*)—by the luminous world
- चरसि (*carasi*)—you move

### Printed p. 251

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O exceedingly young Agni, as cattle afflicted by cold take shelter in a warm cattle-pen, so the famous yajamānas serve you on all sides and take refuge in you alone. You are the messenger for gods, men and all. You, the great one, move between heaven and earth by the luminous world.

##### English translation
Men have recourse to you, Agni, youngest (of the gods), as cattle hasten to the warm stall. You are the messenger of the gods and of the mortals ; mighty, you proceed by the firmament between (heaven and earth).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Yāska, explaining the word वाहिष्ठः (*vāhiṣṭhaḥ*), gives as example the third pāda of this Ṛk, दूतो देवानामसि मर्त्यानां (*dūto devānāmasi martyānāṃ*). He explains वाहिष्ठः (*vāhiṣṭhaḥ*) as वोदृतमः दूतः (*vodṛtamaḥ dūtaḥ*) (Ni. 5-1). In the sacrifices performed by yajamānas, Agni carries the oblation offered in the fire for the gods to the gods in heaven and delivers it to them; hence he is called the messenger between the yajamānas and the gods. A man who carries a word or other thing from one person to another is called a messenger. Since Agni does that work here, he is described in this Ṛk as messenger. The meaning of the words दूतो देवानामसि मर्त्यानां (*dūto devānāmasi martyānāṃ*) is: the messenger who carries men's oblations and the like to the gods. Further, for the word दूत (*dūta*) Yāska gives the etymology दूतो जवतेर्वा द्रवतेर्वा वारयतेर्वा (*dūto javatervā dravatervā vārayatervā*): the word दूत (*dūta*) may be derived from the root जु (*ju*) "to hasten", or the root द्रु (*dru*) "to run", or the root वृ (*vṛ*) "to return". A messenger goes quickly, running, to the place he is sent to; so the word is derived from the roots mentioned.

गावः उष्णमिव व्रजं (संचरंति) (*gāvaḥ uṣṇamiva vrajaṃ (saṃcaraṃti)*)—In the previous Ṛk Agni was compared to a reservoir in the desert; in this Ṛk to the cattle-pen where cows are tied. As in winter cattle grazing outside, bitten by frost, enter in the evening with joy the warm pens, covered and free from the touch of cold wind, so people serve you, Agni, for happiness. This is the purport of the illustration.

### Printed p. 252
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> शिशुं॒ न त्वा॒ जेन्यं॑ व॒र्धयं॑ती मा॒ता बि॑भर्ति सचन॒स्यमा॑ना ॥  
> धनो॒रधि॑ प्र॒वता॑ यासि॒ हर्य॒ञ्ङ्गी॑षसे प॒शुरि॒वाव॑सृष्टः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शिशुं । न । त्वा । जेन्यं । वर्धयंती । माता । बिभर्ति । सचनस्यमाना ।  
> धनोः । अधि । प्रवता । यासि । हर्यन् । जिगीषसे । पशुः इव । अवसृष्टः ॥ ३ .

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने जेन्यं जयशीलं त्वा त्वां शिशुं न पुत्रमिव वर्धयंती पोषयंती
> माता पृथिवी सचनस्यमाना संपर्कमिच्छंती सती बिभर्ति । धारयति । सत्वं
> हर्यन् कामयमानस्तस्मैल्लोके धनोरधि । अधिः सप्तम्यर्थद्योतकः । अंतरिक्षात्पवता प्रवणेन मार्गेण यासि । यज्ञं प्रत्यागच्छसि । किंच त्वमवसृष्टो
> यष्टृभिर्हविरादाय जिगीषसे । देवास्पृति गंतुमिच्छसि । कथमिव । पशुरिव यथा
> विमुक्तः पशुर्गॊष्ठं प्रत्यागच्छति तद्वत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, you are victorious. The Earth is your mother. She nourishes you as a mother nourishes a child, wishes to be in contact with you, and so bears you. Desiring this world, you come to the sacrifice from the sky by a sloping path; अधि (*adhi*) here only marks the locative sense. Further, when the sacrificers have taken the oblation and released you, you wish to go to the gods. The comparison is with an animal that, once set free, returns to its stall.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- जेन्यं (*jenyaṃ*)—the victorious one
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- शिशुं न वर्धयंती (*śiśuṃ na vardhayaṃtī*)—nourishing like a son (as one nourishes a son)
- माता (*mātā*)—the Earth
- सचनस्यमाना (*sacanasyamānā*)—desiring contact with you
- बिभर्ति (*bibharti*)—bears you
- हरन् (*haran*)—you, desirous of the sacrifice
- धनोरधि (*dhanoradhi*)—from the sky
- प्रवता (*pravatā*)—by a path that comes downward
- यासि (*yāsi*)—you come toward the sacrifice
- अवसृष्टः (*avasṛṣṭaḥ*)—after receiving your oblation
- पशुः इव (*paśuḥ iva*)—as an animal that has grazed on grass goes back to its stall
- जिगीषसे (*jigīṣase*)—you again desire to join the gods

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, as a mother nourishes her son, the Earth, desiring contact with you, the victorious, bears you. You, desirous of the sacrifice, come from the sky by a downward path toward the sacrifice. After receiving your oblation, as an animal that has grazed on grass goes back to its stall, you again desire to join the gods.

### Printed p. 253

##### English translation
Cherishing you, (Agni), the victorious, like a child, your mother (earth) desirous of your contact sustains you. You come, eager from above the firmament, by a downward path, and when released,(after the oblation is offered), you desire to go back like cattle (to their stall).
#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मू॒रा अ॑मूर॒ न व॒यं चि॑कित्सो महि॒त्वम॑ग्ने॒ त्वमं॒ग  ॑
> वित्से ॥  
> शये॑ व॒व्रिश्चर॑ति जि॒ह्वया॒दनरे॑रि॒ह्यते॑ युव॒तिं वि॒श्वतिः॒ सन् ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मूराः । अमूर । न । वयं । चिकित्सः । महित्वं । अग्ने । त्वं । अंग । वित्से ॥  
> शये । वव्रिः । चरति । जिह्वया । अदन् । रेरिह्यते । युवतिं । विश्वतिः । सन् ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अमूरामूढ अत एव चिकित्सश्चेतनवन् हे अग्ने मूरा मूढा वयं
> तव महात्म्यं न जानीमः । किंतु हे अग्ने त्वमंग । अंगेति निपात निवार्थे ।
> त्वमेव तव महित्वं माहात्म्यं वित्से । जानासि ! त्वत्तः कोऽन्नो वेदितुमर्हति ।
> किंच वन्रिर्जीर्ण ओषधीसंगः शये । शेते । तिष्ठति । यद्वा । वन्रिरिति रूपनाम
> सामर्थ्याच्चांतर्णीतमत्र्थं द्रष्टव्यं । आहवनीयाख्येन रूपेण रूपवाख्याये ।
> चरति! अथ जॆह्वया ज्वालाख्ययादन्नवींषि भक्षयंश्चरति ॥ वाक्यभेदादनिघातः ॥ किंच
> विश्वतिर्विशां स्वामी सन्नग्नियुवतिमात्मनो मिश्रयित्रिमाहुतिं दीर्घप्रसृतया
> ज्वालया जिह्वया रेरिह्यते । अत्यर्थं लेढि । आस्वादयति ॥ लिह आस्वादने । यजि
> रूपं ॥ यद्वा सोऽग्नियुवतिं तरुणीं जीर्णौषधिकां पृथिवीमास्वादयति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, you are unperplexed and therefore intelligent. We, perplexed, do not know your greatness; you alone know it, for who else could know it? अंग (*aṃga*) is a particle used in the sense of निवार्थे (*nivārthe*). Further, Agni, the old herbs' ... वव्रिः (*vavriḥ*) lies, that is, stays: either it means the aged union with the herbs, or वव्रि (*vavri*) is a word for form, and by implication the sense "having a form" is to be understood, so that he is established in the form called Āhavanīya. And he moves about devouring the oblations with his tongue, the flame. Being lord of the people, Agni licks the युवति (*yuvati*), the oblation that mixes with him, with his far-extending flame-tongue. रेरिह्यते (*rerihyate*) means "licks intensely", "tastes", from the root लिह (*liha*) "to taste". Or else he tastes the Earth, the "young woman" whose herbs are old.**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अमूर (*amūra*)—O Agni, free from folly
- चिकित्सः (*cikitsaḥ*)—possessed of excellent knowledge
- मूराः (*mūrāḥ*)—foolish
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- न (*na*)—do not know your greatness
- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni

### Printed p. 254
- त्वं अंग (*tvaṃ aṃga*)—you alone
- महित्वं (*mahitvaṃ*)—your greatness
- वित्से (*vitse*)—you know
- वव्रिः (*vavriḥ*)—wearing the form of the Āhavanīya fire
- शये (*śaye*)—you are established
- जिह्वया (*jihvayā*)—with the tongue
- अदन् (*adan*)—devouring the oblation
- चरति (*carati*)—Agni moves about
- विश्वतिः सन् (*viśvatiḥ san*)—being protector of the people
- युवतिं (*yuvatiṃ*)—the oblation that mixes with him
- रेरिह्यते (*rerihyate*)—licks and tastes

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, free from folly and possessed of excellent knowledge, we, being foolish, cannot know your greatness. You alone know it. Agni moves about devouring the oblation with his tongue. Being protector of the people, he licks and tastes the oblation that mixes with him.

##### English translation
We, who are perplexed, know not, O unperplexed, intelligent one, your greatness : but you, Agni, verily know it. Wearing a form he reposes ; he moves about devouring (the offering) with his tongue (of flame) ; being lord of men, he licks up the mixed oblation.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Explaining the meaning of the word अमूर (*amūra*), Yāska explains the sentence of the verse (we the foolish do not know, O Agni, your greatness; you alone know it) thus: अमूरः! मूढा वयं स्मोऽमूढस्त्वमसि न वयं विद्मो महत्त्वमग्ने त्वं तु वेत्थ । (*amūraḥ! mūḍhā vayaṃ smo 'mūḍhastvamasi na vayaṃ vidmo mahattvamagne tvaṃ tu vettha |*) (Ni. 6-8). मूर (*mūra*) means foolish; अमूर (*amūra*) means one who is not foolish, discerning, intelligent. In the present sentence the sense is: O Agni, discerning one, we who are foolish, that is, without the power of discernment, cannot understand your greatness, but you alone know it. This is the intention of the explanation.
जिह्वया अदन् रेरिह्यते युवतिं विश्वतिः सन् (*jihvayā adan rerihyate yuvatiṃ viśvatiḥ san*)—In this sentence the tongue of Agni is the flame. In the word रेरिह्यते (*rerihyate*) the letter ra comes as a substitute for la, that is, it becomes लेलिह्यते (*lelihyate*); the meaning is "licks". In the Ṛgveda there is a custom in many places of using ra in place of la; for example the word रघु (*raghu*) is used in place of लघु (*laghu*). As for the word युवति (*yuvati*): the root यु (*yu*) means "to mix"; hence the word युवति (*yuvati*) formed from that root means an oblation mixed with ghee and similar things. The sentence therefore suggests: O Agni, you, lord of all, devour, licking, with the flame that is like your tongue, the oblation mixed with ghee.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> कूचि॑ज्जायते॒ सन॑यासु॒ नव्यो॒ वने॑ तस्थौ पलि॒तो धू॒मके॑तुः ॥  
> अ॒स्ना॒तापो॑ वृष॒भो न प्र वे॑ति॒ सचे॑तसो॒ यं प्र॒णयं॑त॒  ॑
> मर्ताः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आचित् । जायते । सनयासु । नव्यः । वने । तस्थौ । पलितः । धूमकेतुः ॥  
> अस्नाता । अपः । वृषभः । न । प्र । वेति । सचेतसः । यं । प्रनयंत । मर्ताः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> नव्यो नवतरः स्तुत्यो वाग्निः कूचित् क्वचित्पदेशे जायते । कुत्र ।
> सनयासु पुराणीषु जीर्णास्वोषधीष्वरण्योः । तथा पलितः पालयिता श्वेतवर्णो
> धूमकेतुर्धूमप्रज्ञानोग्निर्वने सर्वस्मिन्नरण्ये तस्थौ । तिष्ठति । यद्वा । वन
> उदके मेघगते विद्युदात्मना तिष्ठति च । अस्नाता स्नानमकुर्वन्ननपेक्षितस्नानः सर्व
> दाशुद्धः सोग्निरापः ॥ सुब्बत्ययः ॥ अप उदकानि शांत्यर्थं प्र वेति । प्रगच्छति ।
> तत्र दृष्टांतः । वृषभो ! न यथा वृषभस्तृ ष्टोपशमनार्थमरण्यमध्यस्थान्युदकानि प्रगच्छति तद्वत् । मर्ता मनुष्या ऋत्विजः सचेतसः समानमनस्काः समानप्रज्ञानाः संतो यमग्निं प्रणयंत हविर्भिः प्रीणयंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Agni, who is new (or praiseworthy), is born somewhere. Where? In the old, aged herbs, the two kindling-woods. Likewise, grey (the protector, white in colour) and smoke-bannered (known by his smoke), Agni stands in the whole forest. Or else वन (*vana*) means water: he stands in the water in the cloud, in the form of lightning. Not bathing, needing no bath, always pure, that Agni goes to the waters, that is, to the water, for cooling. The comparison: as a bull goes to waters standing in the forest to quench his thirst. The mortals, the priests, being of one mind and one knowledge, propitiate that Agni with oblations.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नव्यः (*navyaḥ*)—the new Agni
- कूचित् (*kūcit*)—in what place
- जायते (*jāyate*)—is he born?
- वने (*vane*)—in the forest
- सनयासु (*sanayāsu*)—between the kindling-woods (araṇis)
- पलितः (*palitaḥ*)—the one of white colour
- धूमकेतुः (*dhūmaketuḥ*)—the one whose banner is smoke, Agni
- तस्थौ (*tasthau*)—stands
- अस्नाता (*asnātā*)—not needing a bath (being pure)
- वृषभो आपः न (*vṛṣabho āpaḥ na*)—as a bull resorts to water
- प्र वेति (*pra veti*)—Agni also resorts to water
- मर्ताः (*martāḥ*)—priests
- सचेतसः (*sacetasaḥ*)—being of one mind
- यं (*yaṃ*)—that same Agni
- प्रणयंत (*praṇayaṃta*)—they please

### Printed p. 256

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In what place is the new Agni born? In the forest, between the kindling-woods, Agni, white in colour and having smoke as his banner, stands. Though perfectly pure, as a bull resorts to water, Agni also resorts to water. The priests, being of one mind, please Agni.

##### English translation
Where is the new (Agni) born ? he is present in the old.(plants) and in the wood, grey-haired, smoke-bannered : though not needing ablution,he hastens to the water like a (thirsty) bull, he whom men with one consent propitiate.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk the seer asks, "In what place is Agni born?" and himself gives the answer. How? Agni is born in the forest, from old, dried trees, that is, by churning in the kindling-woods (araṇis) made of wood. Likewise in the forest also he is born, from the friction of trees, in the form of forest fire. Besides, since the word वन (*vana*) also means water, he is born also in the waters in the middle of the clouds, in the form of lightning. Thus the seer describes the three kinds of origin of Agni.
अस्नाता आपः वृषभो न (*asnātā āpaḥ vṛṣabho na*)—Since this Agni is always pure, he has no need of a bath for the removal of impurity. Not only that, this Agni purifies other things, such as metals, by his touch. Yet it is commonly held that Agni dwells in the water. The seer describes it thus: as a rutting bull in the forest, even though it has no need of a bath, enters water to relieve the heat of the sun, so Agni, though he needs no bath and is pure by nature, enters the water when he likes and stays there.
#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त॒नू॒त्यजी॑व॒ तस्क॑रा वन॒गू र॑श॒नाभि॑र्द॒शभि॑र॒भ्य॑धीतां ॥  
> इ॒यं ते॑ अग्ने॒ नव्य॑सी मनी॒षा यु॒क्षा रथं॒ न शु॒चय॑द्भि॒रंग्यः॑ ॥ ६ ॥

### Printed p. 257

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तसूत्यजा इव । तस्करा । वनर्गू इति । रशनाभि । दशभि अभि । अधीतां ॥  
> इयं । ते । अग्ने । नव्यसी । मनीषा । युक्त्व । रथं । न । शुचयत्भिः । अंगैः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने वनर्गू वनगामिनौ तनूत्यजा चौर्ये मर्तुं कृतनिश्चयौ दृष्ट्वा
> तस्करा तस्करौ यथा पथिकं मोषणार्थं रज्ञा बद्धाकृष्य च क्वचित्प्रदेशे स्थापयतः
> तद्वत् । तथा यास्कः । तनूत्यक् तनूत्यक्ता वनर्गू वनगामिनावग्निमंथनौ बाहू
> तस्कराभ्यामुपमिमिते । नि. ३-१४ । इत्यादि । अधीतां । दधातेर्लुण सिचो लुकि
> छंदस्युभयथेति तस अर्थधातुकत्वेन जित्वाद्दुमास्थेतीकारः ॥ ततो हे अग्ने ते
> त्वदर्थं नव्यसी नवतरेयं मनीषा स्तुतिर्मया क्रियते । एतज्जाण्वा शुचयद्दि
> सर्वं प्रकाशयद्भिरात्मीयैरंगैस्तेजोभिरात्मानं मदीयं यज्ञं प्रति युक्त्व ।
> योजये ! कथमिव ! रथं न यथा रथमश्वैः संयोजयंति तद्वत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, as two thieves who go in the forest and are resolved to die in the robbery seize a traveller in order to rob him, bind him with cords and drag him to some place, so वनर्गू (*vanargū*) and तनूत्यजा (*tanūtyajā*) ... the two arms (do likewise to the kindling-wood). Yāska says: the two arms, going to the forest (that is, the two that churn the fire), abandoning the body, are compared to two thieves (Ni. 3-14), and so on. अधीतां (*adhītāṃ*) is explained from the root *dadhāti*. Then, O Agni, for you this newer praise is made by me. Yoke yourself to my sacrifice with your own limbs, your radiances, that illuminate everything. How? As men yoke a chariot with horses.**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वनर्गू (*vanargū*)—those who roam in the forest
- तनूत्यजा (*tanūtyajā*)—those with the daring to give up their own body
- तस्करा (*taskarā*)—two robbers
- रशनाभिः (*raśanābhiḥ*)—as they seize people with cords
- दशभिः (*daśabhiḥ*)—with the ten fingers
- अभ्यधीतां (*abhyadhītāṃ*)—the arms seize you (your kindling-wood)
- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- ते (*te*)—addressed to you
- *navyasī iyaṃ manīṣā*—this new praise is composed
- रथं न (*rathaṃ na*)—as a chariot joins with horses
- शुचयद्भिः (*śucayadbhiḥ*)—illuminating
- अंगैः (*aṃgaiḥ*)—with your radiances
- यक्ष्व (*yakṣva*)—join

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, as robbers who roam in the forest and are ready to give up their very life seize people with cords and bind them, so the arms with the ten fingers seize your kindling-wood (the araṇi). O Agni, this new praise is composed addressed to you. As a chariot joins with horses, join with your radiances that illuminate the world.

##### English translation
As two daring robbers lurking in a forest seize (upon a traveller) with cords, (so the two hands seize upon the touchwood, with the ten

### Printed p. 258
fingers). This new praise, Agni, is addressed to you: attach yourself (to my sacrifice) with your radiant members, as they harness horses to a car.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Where he explains a simile, and in particular compares a lesser thing with a greater one, Yāska gives the first half of this Ṛk as an example and states its etymological explanation:
अथापि कनीयसा ज्यायांसं । तनूत्यजीव तस्करा वनर्गूरशनाभिर्दशभिरभ्यधीतां । तनूत्यक् तनूत्यत्ता । वनर्गू वनगामिनौ । अग्निमंथनौ बाहू तस्कराभ्यामुपमिमाते । तस्करस्तत्करो भवति । करोति यत्पापकमिति नैरुक्ताः i तनोतीर्वा स्यात् संततकर्मा भवति । अहोरात्रकर्मा वा । रशनाभिर्दशभिरभ्यधीतां इति अभ्यधातां । ज्यायांस्तत्र गुणोऽभिप्रेतः ॥ (*athāpi kanīyasā jyāyāṃsaṃ | tanūtyajīva taskarā vanargūraśanābhirdaśabhirabhyadhītāṃ | tanūtyak tanūtyattā | vanargū vanagāminau | agnimaṃthanau bāhū taskarābhyāmupamimāte | taskarastatkaro bhavati | karoti yatpāpakamiti nairuktāḥ i tanotīrvā syāt saṃtatakarmā bhavati | ahorātrakarmā vā | raśanābhirdaśabhirabhyadhītāṃ iti abhyadhātāṃ | jyāyāṃstatra guṇo 'bhipretaḥ ‖*) (Ni. 3-14)
In this passage the two hands of the officiating priests, at the time of churning the fire, are compared to two robbers who commit theft. The robbers who steal here should not be understood as the usual thieves who enter a house unseen at night and carry off wealth and the like. Some thieves of that kind exist. But since the word वनर्गू (*vanargū*) is used here, these are thieves of another kind. Such thieves live in the forest and trouble travellers going from village to village, that is, they bind them with ropes and rob them of all they possess. Such thieves, when they face a band of travellers stronger than themselves, often risk their lives. Therefore तनूत्यजा (*tanūtyajā*) is used here, to indicate that while stealing they fight with no regard even for their lives. And the word वनर्गू (*vanargū*) denotes those who lie in wait in the midst of the road in the forest, wayfarers. Since the words तनूत्यजा (*tanūtyajā*) and वनर्गू (*vanargū*) here are dual forms, two robbers are meant. The word रशनाभिः (*raśanābhiḥ*) has two meanings, "with ropes" and "with fingers". For the robbers it must be taken as "with ropes", and for the officiating priests as "fingers". So the main purport of the explanation is this: as two thieves who, careless of their lives, lie in wait in the forest to rob travellers of their wealth bind the travellers with ropes and with their fingers to seize the wealth, so in the sacrificial rite the two arms of the Adhvaryu, binding the upper churning-stick with a rope, churn with the ten fingers of his hands and produce Agni.

### Printed p. 259
Here, as to तस्कर (*taskara*): तत्करः करोति यत्पापकमिति नैरुक्ताः । (*tatkaraḥ karoti yatpāpakamiti nairuktāḥ |*) The meaning of the word तस्कर (*taskara*) for the etymologists is "one who does just that which is a sinful act". Or the word तस्कर (*taskara*) may be derived from the root तनु विस्तारे (*tanu vistāre*). In that case the word can be explained as one who steals always, that is, at night and by day. Yāska explains that in this illustration the noble act of churning fire is compared with the base act of the thief, and the meaning is thereby made clear.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ब्रह्म॑ च ते जातवेदो॒ नम॑श्चे॒यं च॒ गीर् सद॒मिद्वर्ध॑निः  
> भूत् ॥  
> रक्षा॑ णो अग्ने॒ तन॑यानि तो॒का रक्षो॒त न॑ स्त॒न्वो॒३॒॑ ३ अप्र॑युच्छन् ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ब्रह्म । च । ते । जातवेदः । नमः । च । इयं । च । गीर् । सदं । इत् । वर्धनी । भूत् ।  
> रक्ष । नः । अग्नेः । तनयानि तोका । रक्ष । उत । नः । तन्नः । अप्रयुच्छन् ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ॥ श्रीरामचंद्राय नमः ॥
> उखासंभरणीयेष्टावग्नेर्ब्रह्मण्वतो याज्या ब्रह्म च त इत्येषा । सूत्रितं
> च । ब्रह्म च ते जातवेदो नमश्च पुरूण्यग्ने पुरुधा त्वाया : आ. ४.१ । इति ॥
> हे जातवेदो जातप्रज्ञाग्ने ते त्वदर्थं ब्रह्मास्माभिर्दीयमानं हवीरूपमन्नं वर्धनी वर्धनं भूत् । भवतु । तथा नमो नमस्कारश्च किंचेयमिदानींक्रियमाणैषा स्तुतिः सदमित् सद्येव वर्धनी वर्धयित्रि भूत् । भवतु । तस्माद्दे
> अग्ने नोऽस्माकं तनयानि पुत्रांस्तोका पौत्रांश्च रक्ष । पालय । उतापि चाप्रयुच्छन्नप्रमाद्यंत्वं नोऽस्माकं तन्नोऽंगानि रक्ष ।
> , प्रपदार्थ .
> जातवेदः - जातवेदोरूपनाद ऎलै अग्निये ; ते-निन्नन्नुद्देशिसिद । ब्रह्म चहविस्सु । वर्धनी भूत्-पुष्टिसाधकवागलि । नमः-नमस्कारवू । इयं च-ई
> स्तुतियू सदमित्-सर्वदा ! वर्धनी भूत्-पुष्टिसाधकवागलि । अग्ने-ऎलै अग्निये ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the Ukhāsambharaṇī offering, this is the yājyā verse for Agni Brahmaṇvat; he cites the sūtra ब्रह्म च ते जातवेदो नमश्च पुरूण्यग्ने पुरुधा त्वाया (*brahma ca te jātavedo namaśca purūṇyagne purudhā tvāyā*) (Ā. 4.1). O Jātavedas, Agni of born wisdom, may the "brahma", the food in the form of oblation given by us for you, be a means of strengthening. So also reverence; and this praise now being recited, may it at once be a strengthener. Therefore, Agni, protect our sons and grandsons; and unremiss, protect our bodies, our limbs.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- जातवेदः (*jātavedaḥ*)—O Agni, who are Jātavedas
- ते (*te*)—addressed to you
- ब्रह्म च (*brahma ca*)—the oblation
- वर्धनी भूत् (*vardhanī bhūt*)—may it be a means of nourishment
- नमः (*namaḥ*)—the reverence
- इयं च (*iyaṃ ca*)—and this praise
- सदमित् (*sadamit*)—always
- वर्धनी भूत् (*vardhanī bhūt*)—may it be a means of nourishment
- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni

### Printed p. 260
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- तनयानि (*tanayāni*)—sons
- तोका (*tokā*)—grandsons
- रक्षिसु (*rakṣisu*)—protect
- अंत (*aṃta*)—and
- अप्रयुच्छन् (*aprayucchan*)—you who are ever watchful
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- तन्न (*tanna*)—bodies
- रक्षिसु (*rakṣisu*)—protect

> नः—नम्म । तनयानि—पुत्ररन्नू । तोका—पौत्ररन्नू । रक्षिसु—रक्षिसु । अंत—मत्तु ।
> अप्रयुच्छन्—सर्वदा जागरूकनाद नीनु । नः—नम्म । तन्न—शरीरगळन्नू । रक्षिसु.

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, who are Jātavedas, may the oblation offered to you be a means of nourishment. May our reverence and this praise always be a means of nourishment. O Agni, protect our sons and grandsons. Ever watchful, protect our bodies.

##### English translation
May this oblation, Jatavedas, this reverence, this praise, ever magnify you. Protect, Agni, our sons, our grandsons, and diligently defend our persons.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In the Ukhāsambharaṇa offering, in the homa made to the deity Agni Brahmaṇvat, this Ṛk ब्रह्म च ते (*brahma ca te*) is to be recited as the yājyā mantra; this is explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra: ब्रह्म च ते जातवेदो नमश्च पुरुण्याग्ने पुरुधा त्वाया (*brahma ca te jātavedo namaśca puruṇyāgne purudhā tvāyā*) (Ā. 4-1).
In this last Ṛk, which is the conclusion of the sūkta, praise is offered to Agni and he is prayed to cause our nourishment and to protect our sons, grandsons and ourselves.

## Sūkta 5 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> एकः समुद्र इति सप्तर्चं पंचमं सूक्तं । ऋष्याद्याः पूर्ववत् । विनियोगश्च । एक इत्यनुक्रांतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The fifth sūkta, एकः समुद्र (*ekaḥ samudra*), has seven Ṛks. Seer and the rest as before; the viniyoga too. It is cited in the Anukramaṇī as एक (*eka*).

Anuvāda: this sūkta एकः समुद्रः (*ekaḥ samudraḥ*) is the fifth sūkta in the first anuvāka. It has seven Ṛks. Seer, deity and metre are the same as in the previous sūkta. The viniyoga is also as before. In the Anukramaṇī it is given as beginning with एकः (*ekaḥ*).

### Printed p. 261
Sūkta 5.

Ṛṣi: Trita Āptya.
Deity: Agni.
Metre: Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> एकः॑ समु॒द्रो ध॒रुणो॑ रयी॒णाम॒स्मद्धृ॒दो भूरि॑जन्मा॒ वि  ॑
> चष्टे ॥  
> सिष॒क्तूध॑र्नि॒ण्योरु॒पस्थ॒ उत्स॑स्य॒ मध्ये॒ निहि॑तं प॒दं वेः ॥ १ ॥

> एकः । समुद्रः । धरुणः । रयाणां । अस्मत् । हृदः । भूरिजन्मा । वि । चष्टे ॥  
> सिसक्ति । ऊधः । निण्योः । उपस्थे । उत्सस्य । मध्ये । निहितं । पदं । वेरिति वेः ॥ १॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> समुद्रः यस्माद्दनास्युदकानि ना समुद्धवंतीति तादृशो रयाणां
> धनानां धरुणो धारयितैकोऽसहायो भूरिजन्मा बहुविधजननो नानायज्ञॆष्वाहवनीयादिभावेन बहुजननः सोग्निरस्कत् ॥ सुपां सुलुगिति षष्ठ्या
> लुक् ॥ अस्माकं हृदो हृदयान्यभिलषितानि वि चष्टे । विपश्यति । जानाति । उक्तं च ।
> मनो वै देवा मनुष्यस्य जानंतीति । किंच निण्योः । अंतर्हितनामैतत् । अंतर्हितयोज्योतिस्तमोभ्यामाच्छादितयोः प्रातःसायंकालयोरुपस्थ उपस्थाने
> समीपे वर्तमानमूधो रात्रिं सिषक्ति । सेवते उपजीवति । यद्वा । निण्योरंत.
> र्हितस्यांतरिक्षस्य समीपे वर्तमानमूधो मेघं सेवते । अथ प्रत्यक्षः । हे अग्ने
> उत्सस्योदकधारकस्य लोकस्य मध्यॆ पदमपां स्थानं निहितं । तन्नाध्यमिकां वाचं
> वेः । गच्छ । यद्वा । उत्सस्य मेघस्य मध्यॆ निहितं पदं विद्युदाख्यं तत् स्वात्मतया
> गच्छ ॥ वेः । वी गत्यादिसु । लजि सिपि रूपं । वाक्यभेदादनिघातः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The ocean (samudra) is so called because waters flow together to it; such is Agni, the sole supporter of riches, unaided, of many births, since he has many births in the various sacrifices as Āhavanīya and so on. He sees our hearts, our desires, knows them; as it is said, मनो वै देवा मनुष्यस्य जानंतीति (*mano vai devā manuṣyasya jānaṃtīti*). Further, निण्योः (*niṇyoḥ*) is a name for "hidden": of the morning and evening, hidden by light and darkness, he enjoys the night (ऊधः (*ūdhaḥ*)) lying near. Or, he enjoys the cloud lying near the hidden atmosphere. Now, in the direct address: O Agni, to the place of the waters, set in the midst of the उत्स (*utsa*), the water-holding world, go, reaching the middle-region speech. Or: go, as your own self, to the place called lightning, set in the midst of the cloud.

### Printed p. 262

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- समुद्रः (*samudraḥ*)—like the ocean
- रयीणां (*rayīṇāṃ*)—for our riches
- एकः धरुणः (*ekaḥ dharuṇaḥ*)—the unique supporter
- भूरिजन्मा (*bhūrijanmā*)—Agni, who has many kinds of birth
- अस्मत् (*asmat*)—our
- हृदः (*hṛdaḥ*)—the desires in the heart
- वि चष्टे (*vi caṣṭe*)—knows fully
- निश्योः (*niśyoḥ*)—of morning and evening, covered by darkness
- उपस्थे (*upasthe*)—lying near
- ऊधः (*ūdhaḥ*)—the night
- सिषक्ति (*siṣakti*)—enjoys
- उत्सस्य (*utsasya*)—of the world that holds water
- मध्यॆ (*madhyè*)—in the middle
- निहितं (*nihitaṃ*)—established
- पदं (*padaṃ*)—to the place
- वेः (*veḥ*)—go

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Like the ocean, the support of wealth, Agni, the unique supporter of our riches, of many kinds of birth, fully knows the desires in our hearts. He enjoys the night, which lies near the morning and evening that are covered by darkness. O Agni, go to the place established in the midst of the water-holding world.

##### English translation
Agni, the sole capacious ocean of riches, who is of many births,, beholds our hearts; he waits on the cloud in the neighbourhood of the hidden (firmament); go, Agni, to your appointed place in the midst of the waters.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

समुद्रः—यस्माद्दनान्युदकानि ना समुद्रवंतीति तादृशः । (*samudraḥ—yasmāddanānyudakāni nā samudravaṃtīti tādṛśaḥ |*) Here it is explained that it is called "samudra" because it flows (is poured forth) with wealth or with water. As to how the ocean is related to wealth: according to the meaning of the lexicon रत्नाकरो जलनिधिः (*ratnākaro jalanidhiḥ*), the ocean is famed as the abode of many kinds of jewels.
Since the word samudra is used in the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā in different senses, the Nirukta-kāras have included the word among the names of the atmosphere (Ni. 2-10). Afterwards they showed the difference between the terrestrial ocean and the ocean that is a synonym of the atmosphere. Concerning those who explain without knowing the difference between these two and so give room for doubt, he says:
तत्र समुद्र इत्येत्पार्थिवेन समुद्रेण संदिह्यते । (*tatra samudra ityetpārthivena samudreṇa saṃdihyate |*) Many mistake the samudra that is a synonym of the atmosphere for the terrestrial ocean and so explain it (that is not right). Thus he shows the etymology of the word samudra.

### Printed p. 263
समुद्रः कस्मात्समुद्ध्रवंत्यस्मादापः समभिद्रवंत्येनमापः संमोदंतेs स्मिन् भूतानि समुदको भवति समुनत्तीति वा । (*samudraḥ kasmātsamuddhravaṃtyasmādāpaḥ samabhidravaṃtyenamāpaḥ saṃmodaṃtes smin bhūtāni samudako bhavati samunattīti vā |*) (Ni. 2-10)
First, the root द्रु (*dru*), meaning "to move", together with the two prefixes सम् (*sam*) and उद् (*ud*), gives the sense "it flows welling upward"; or second, the same root द्रु (*dru*) with the prefixes सम् (*sam*) and अभि (*abhi*) gives the sense that the waters (of rivers) flow towards it; or the root मुद् (*mud*), meaning "to rejoice", with the prefix सम् (*sam*) gives the sense that aquatic creatures rejoice in it; or because it is a store of water; or the root उद् (*ud*), meaning "to wet", with the prefix सम् (*sam*) gives the sense "it makes wet". In these ways the word samudra is formed, and the etymology of the word is shown (Ni. 2-10). In this connection, to show the difference between the word samudra as a synonym of the atmosphere and the terrestrial ocean, he cites also a story.
(Ni. 2.11)
तयोर्विभागस्तत्रेतिहासमाचक्षते । देवापिश्चार्ष्टषेणः शंतनुश्च कौरव्या भ्रातरा बभूवतुः । स शंतनुः कनीयानभिषेजचयांचक्रे । देवापिस्तपः प्रतिपेदे । ततः शंतनो राज्ये द्वादश वर्षाणि देवो न ववर्ष । तमूचुब्राह्मणा अधर्मस्त्वया चरितो जेष्ठंभ्रातरमंतरित्याभिषॆचितं तस्मा त्ते देवो न वर्षतीति । स शंतनुदेवापिं शिशिक्ष राज्येन । तमुनाच देवापिः पुरोहितस्तेऽसानि याजयानि च त्वेति तस्यैतद्वर्षकामसूक्तंतस्येषा भवति ॥ (*tayorvibhāgastatretihāsamācakṣate | devāpiścārṣṭaṣeṇaḥ śaṃtanuśca kauravyā bhrātarā babhūvatuḥ | sa śaṃtanuḥ kanīyānabhiṣejacayāṃcakre | devāpistapaḥ pratipede | tataḥ śaṃtano rājye dvādaśa varṣāṇi devo na vavarṣa | tamūcubrāhmaṇā adharmastvayā carito jeṣṭhaṃbhrātaramaṃtarityābhiṣècitaṃ tasmā tte devo na varṣatīti | sa śaṃtanudevāpiṃ śiśikṣa rājyena | tamunāca devāpiḥ purohitaste 'sāni yājayāni ca tveti tasyaitadvarṣakāmasūktaṃtasyeṣā bhavati ‖*)
Of Devāpi and Śantanu, the two Kuru-line sons of Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa, the younger, Śantanu, had himself consecrated and became king of that realm. Devāpi went to the forest to practise austerities. Thereafter, for twelve years no rain fell in Śantanu's kingdom. The Brāhmaṇas then said to Śantanu: "Having sent your elder brother to the forest and become king yourself, you have committed a sinful act. Therefore the rain-god does not rain in your kingdom." Then, when Śantanu tried to have Devāpi consecrated king, Devāpi said, "I shall be your priest and perform the sacrifice for you," and he had the sacrifice performed for rain. Afterwards rain fell from the ocean in the upper region into the ocean in the lower region; thus showing the difference between the two oceans,

### Printed p. 264
Quoting this Ṛk, अर्ष्पिषेणो होत्रमृषिर्निषीदन् देवापिर्देवसुमतिं चिकित्वान् । स उत्तरस्मादधरं समुद्रमपो दिव्या असृजद्वर्ष्या अभि । (*arṣpiṣeṇo hotramṛṣirniṣīdan devāpirdevasumatiṃ cikitvān | sa uttarasmādadharaṃ samudramapo divyā asṛjadvarṣyā abhi |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-98-5), they have shown the difference between the upper sea and the lower sea. Here "upper sea" means the atmosphere, and "lower sea" means the terrestrial sea.

In the Ṛksaṃhitā the word *samudra* ("sea") can in some places be said definitely to mean the terrestrial sea. But in Ṛks such as यथा वातो यथा वनं यथा समुद्र विजति (*yathā vāto yathā vanaṃ yathā samudra vijati*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-78-8) it is hard to say whether it means the atmosphere or the terrestrial sea.

In अग्निं विश्वा अभि पृक्षः सचंते समुद्रं न स्रवतः सप्त यह्वीः (*agniṃ viśvā abhi pṛkṣaḥ sacaṃte samudraṃ na sravataḥ sapta yahvīḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-71-7), in सं यं स्तुभोऽवनयो न यंति समुद्रं न स्रवतो रोधचक्राः (*saṃ yaṃ stubho 'vanayo na yaṃti samudraṃ na sravato rodhacakrāḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-190 7), and in या आपो दिव्या उत वा स्रवंति खनित्रिमा उत वा याः स्वयंजाः । समुद्रार्था याः शुचयः पावकास्ता आपो देवीरिह मामवंतु । (*yā āpo divyā uta vā sravaṃti khanitrimā uta vā yāḥ svayaṃjāḥ | samudrārthā yāḥ śucayaḥ pāvakāstā āpo devīriha māmavaṃtu |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-49-2) — in these and many other Ṛks it can be said with certainty that the terrestrial sea is meant. In some places the word is used as an adjective and gives the sense "flowing".

In the Ṛk अद्भर्याति वरुणः समुद्रैयुष्मा इच्छंतः शवसो नपातः (*adbharyāti varuṇaḥ samudraiyuṣmā icchaṃtaḥ śavaso napātaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-160-4), समुद्रैः (*samudraiḥ*) means "with waters whose nature is to flow". In this way the meaning must be made out according to the context.

भूरिजन्मा (*bhūrijanmā*)—Agni who has many births. Many sacrificers, each in his own many sacrifices, kindle Agni separately in the fire-sticks (araṇi) and install him in the Āhavanīya and other places. Since Agni is produced afresh in many sacrifices, it is natural to call him *bhūrijanmā*, "of many births".

एकः (*ekaḥ*)—without a helper: one who has the power to accomplish any task by himself, without needing another's help.

### Printed p. 265
अस्मत् हृदः वि चष्टे (*asmat hṛdaḥ vi caṣṭe*)—He knows the desires in our hearts. This Agni knows the wishes in the heart, that is the mind, of us who sacrifice or praise. No one can know what is in another's mind; but Agni has the power to know what is in another's mind.

निण्योः उपस्थे ऊधः सिषक्ति (*niṇyoḥ upasthe ūdhaḥ siṣakti*)—Two kinds of meaning have been explained for this sentence. (1) He serves, that is approaches, the night near morning and evening, which are covered with light and with darkness. At morning there is light; at evening there is darkness. The night is related to both: at the beginning of the night is the evening with darkness, at the end of the night the morning with light. Therefore the intention is that Agni shines in the night, which is connected with both evening and morning. This is one meaning. (2) निण्योः (*niṇyoḥ*) means "of the hidden atmosphere". The other meaning is that Agni shines in the cloud in this atmosphere in the form of lightning-fire.

उत्सस्य मध्ये निहितं पदं वेकि (*utsasya madhye nihitaṃ padaṃ veki*)—Two kinds of meaning can be given for this sentence also. The intention is: go to the place called Mādhyamikā Vāk, situated in the middle of the world that holds the water, in the place of water or the atmosphere; or go to the place of lightning-fire, your own very form, in the middle of the cloud filled with water.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒मा॒नं नी॒ळं वृष॑णो॒ वसा॑नाः॒ सं ज॑ग्मिरे महि॒षा अर्व॑तीभिः ॥  
> ऋ॒तस्य॑ प॒दं क॒वयो॒ नि पां॑ति॒ गुहा॒ नामा॑नि दधिरे॒ परा॑णि ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> समानं । नीळं । वृषणः । वसानाः । सं । जग्मिरे । महिषाः । आर्वतीभिः ॥  
> ऋतस्य । पदं । कवयः । नि । पांति । गुहा । नामानि । दधिरे । पराणि ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वृषण आहुतीनां सेक्तारः समानमेकं नीळं स्थानमुग्निं वसाना मंत्रत
> आच्छादयंतो महिषा महांतो यजमाना अर्वतीभिर्वडनाभिः सु जग्मिरे !

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The showerers of oblations (वृषणः (*vṛṣaṇaḥ*)), covering with mantras the one common abode, Agni (समानं नीळं (*samānaṃ nīḷaṃ*)), the great ones, the sacrificers, have come together (सं जग्मिरे (*saṃ jagmire*)) with mares**NOTE**; that is, they became possessed of horses.

### Printed p. 266

> समगच्छंत । अश्ववंतोऽभूवन् । यद्वा । अग्निमेवात्मीययज्ञं प्रत्यागंतुं
> रोहिदाख्याभिर्नडनाभिः संयोजितवंतः । तथा कवयो मेधाविन ऋतस्योदकस्य
> पदमावासस्थानमग्निं नि पांति । नितरां रक्षंति । हविर्भिः स्तुतिभिश्चाराधयंतीत्यर्थः ।
> ततो गुहा गुहायामंतरिक्षे स्थितानि दिव्यानि नामान्न्युदकानि दधिरे । धारयंति ।
> यद्वा । गुहा गूढे संवृते हृदये पराणि प्रधानान्यग्निर्जातवेदा वैश्वानर इत्या
> दीनि नामानि दधिरे । कुर्वंति । स्तुवंतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

(Continuing.) Or else: they yoked Agni himself, so that he should come to their own sacrifice, with mares called Rohit. Likewise the sages (कवयः (*kavayaḥ*)), the wise, guard well the dwelling-place of the water (ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)), Agni; they worship him with oblations and praises. Then they hold the divine waters situated in the cave, that is in the atmosphere. Or else: in the hidden, covered heart they make the chief names, such as Agni, Jātavedas, Vaiśvānara; that is, they praise him.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वृषणः (*vṛṣaṇaḥ*)—those who offer oblations
- समानं (*samānaṃ*)—common to all
- नीळं (*nīḷaṃ*)—Agni, the place of worship
- वसानाः (*vasānāḥ*)—those who cover (him) with mantras
- महिषाः (*mahiṣāḥ*)—the great ones, the sacrificers
- अर्वतीभिः (*arvatībhiḥ*)—with horses
- सं जग्मिरे (*saṃ jagmire*)—have yoked
- कवयः (*kavayaḥ*)—the wise
- यतस्य (*yatasya*)—of the water
- पदं (*padaṃ*)—the place
- नि पांति (*ni pāṃti*)—protect
- गुहा (*guhā*)—meditated upon in their hidden heart
- पराणि नामानि (*parāṇi nāmāni*)—the excellent names of Agni
- दधिरे (*dadhire*)—have borne

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The sacrificers, who offer oblations, who cover with mantras Agni, the place of worship common to all, and who are great, have yoked Agni with horses, that he may come to their sacrifice. The wise protect the place of the water. They have borne the excellent names of Agni, who is meditated upon in their hidden heart.

##### English translation
Great (worshippers), showerers (of oblations) enveloping with their hymns the common dark (fire), have associated (him) with his horses (to the sacrifice). Sages, preserve (by oblations) the place of the water and support the divine rains in the vault (of heaven).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

वृषणः समानं नीळं वसानाः महिषाः अर्वतीभिः सं जग्मिरे (*vṛṣaṇaḥ samānaṃ nīḷaṃ vasānāḥ mahiṣāḥ arvatībhiḥ saṃ jagmire*)—The Bhāṣyakāra has explained this sentence in two ways. The excellent sacrificers, who offer oblations to Agni and who cover Agni with mantras, that is, praise him, became endowed with horses; that is, by Agni's grace they obtained horses. Or else, so that Agni might come to their place of sacrifice, they yoked Agni's chariot with Agni's horses called Rohita; this is also the meaning.

### Printed p. 267
ऋतस्य पदं कवयः नि पांति (*ṛtasya padaṃ kavayaḥ ni pāṃti*)—The wise priests guard well Agni, who is the dwelling-place of the water, that is, who had taken refuge in the water; that is, they worship Agni specially with offerings of oblations and recitations of hymns.

गुहा नामानि दधिरे पराणि (*guhā nāmāni dadhire parāṇi*)—They hold the divine, that is heavenly, water that is in the atmosphere. Or else it can be explained thus: in the inner depth of the heart, a hidden region, they meditate upon and praise the various names of Agni, such as Jātavedas, Vaiśvānara and the like.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऋ॒ता॒यिनी॑ मा॒यिनी॒ सं द॑धाते मि॒त्वा शिशुं॑ जज्ञतुर्व॒र्धयं॑ती ॥  
> विश्व॑स्य॒ नाभिं॒ चर॑तो ध्रु॒वस्य॑ क॒वेश्चि॒तंतुं॒ मन॑सा वि॒यिंतः॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ऋतयिनी इत्यृतयिनी । मायिनी इति । सं । दधाते इति । मित्वा : शिशुं ।  
> जज्ञतुः । वर्धयंती इति ॥  
> विश्वस्य । नाभिं । चरतः । ध्रुवस्य । कवेः । चित् । तंतुं । मनसा । वियंतः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऋतायिनी ऋतवत्यौ सत्यवत्यौ आत एव मायिनी । प्रज्ञावत्यौ कर्मयुक्ते
> ना द्यावापृथिव्यौ यज्ञसाधनभूते अरणिः वेममग्निं सं दधाते । सम्यग्धारयतः ।
> अग्निं सूर्यात्मना केवलाग्न्यात्मना ना सं दधाते । मित्वा कालपरिमाणं कृत्वा
> यद्वा संपरिच्छिद्य शिशुं शंसनीयं बालमग्निं जज्जतुः । जनयतः । वर्धयंती
> वर्धयंत्र्यौ द्यावापृथिव्यौ लोके यथा परिच्छेदाय पुत्रं पितरौ जनयतस्तद्वत् ।
> ततो विश्वस्य सर्वस्य चरतो जंगमस्य ध्रुवस्य स्थावरस्य च जगतो नाभिं नाभिभूतं
> मध्यं प्रधानभूतं वा कवेर्मेधाविनोऽग्नेस्तंतुं तनितारं वैश्वानराख्यमंशमेव मनसा वियंतो विविधं गच्छंतो मनुष्याः सुखेन युज्यंत इति शेषः ।
> तं वयं यजाम इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

ऋतायिनी (*ṛtāyinī*): the two possessing truth; hence मायिनी (*māyinī*): the two possessing wisdom, joined with action. Heaven and earth, the two means of the sacrifice**NOTE**, hold Agni well (सं दधाते (*saṃ dadhāte*)); they hold Agni in the form of the sun and in the form of simple fire. मित्वा (*mitvā*): having made a measure of time (or: having delimited). The two beget (जज्ञतुः (*jajñatuḥ*)) the child (शिशुं (*śiśuṃ*)), the praiseworthy young Agni, and make him grow (वर्धयंती (*vardhayaṃtī*): the two who cause to grow), as in the world parents beget a son for a defined purpose. Then the navel (नाभिं (*nābhiṃ*)) of all the world, the moving (चरतः (*carataḥ*)) and the fixed (ध्रुवस्य (*dhruvasya*)), that is, its centre or its chief part; the thread (तंतुं (*taṃtuṃ*)), the spreader, of the wise Agni, that part named Vaiśvānara — men, going variously (वियंतः (*viyaṃtaḥ*)) with the mind towards it, are joined with happiness (this is the word to be supplied). "We worship him."

### Printed p. 268

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऋतायिनी (*ṛtāyinī*)—possessing truth
- मायिनी (*māyinī*)—and endowed with wisdom; heaven and earth
- सं दधाते (*saṃ dadhāte*)—have borne Agni
- मित्वा (*mitvā*)—as those who measure time
- शिशुं (*śiśuṃ*)—Agni, in the form of a son
- जज्जतुः वर्धयंती (*jajjatuḥ vardhayaṃtī*)—have brought him forth and have made him grow (or: having made him grow, have brought him forth)
- विश्वस्य (*viśvasya*)—of the whole
- चरतः (*carataḥ*)—to the world of the moving
- ध्रुवस्य चित् (*dhruvasya cit*)—to the stable universe also
- नाभिं (*nābhiṃ*)—the navel
- कवेः (*kaveḥ*)—of the wise Agni
- तंतुं (*taṃtuṃ*)—the part named Vaiśvānara
- मनसा (*manasā*)—in the mind itself
- वियंतः (*viyaṃtaḥ*)—meditating (let us worship him)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Heaven and earth, possessing truth and endowed with wisdom, have borne Agni in their midst. They, who measure time, have brought forth Agni in the form of a son and raised him. Meditating in the mind itself upon that part named Vaiśvānara of the wise Agni, who is the navel of the whole world, moving and unmoving, let us worship him.

##### English translation
The two observers of the truth, the two possessors of wisdom, (heaven and earth) sustain (this Agni) ; measuring the time. they have brought forth and reared a son ; and (men), pondering in their minds on that navel of the universe, movable and immovable, the thread of the sage (Agni, are blessed with happiness).

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऋ॒तस्य॒ हि व॑र्त॒नयः॒ सुजा॑त॒मिषो॒ वाजा॑यि प्र॒दिवः॒ सचं॑ते ॥  
> अ॒धी॒वा॒सं रोद॑सी वावसा॒ने घृ॒तैरन्नै॑र्वावृधाते॒  ॑
> मधूनां ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ऋतस्य । हि । वर्तनयः । सुजातं । इषः । वाजाय । प्रदिवः । सचंते ॥  
> अधीवासं । रोदसी इति । ववसाने इति । पृथैः । अन्नैः । पवृधाते इति । मधूनां ।

### Printed p. 269
