##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऋतस्य यज्ञस्य वर्तनयः प्रवर्तका इषोऽभिलषितानीच्छंतः प्रदिवः पुरातना ऋत्विग्यजमानाः सुजातमाधानकाले सुषूत्पन्नमग्निं वाजाय बलार्थमन्नार्थं वा सचंते हि । सेवंते खलु । अपि च वावसाने सर्वस्य जगत अच्चादयित्र्यौ
> रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्यावधीनासमोषधिनक्षत्राद्यात्मना निवसंतं । यद्वा । अधिरुपर्यर्थे ' त्रयाणां लोकानामुपर्यग्निविद्युदादित्यात्मना निवसंतं । अग्निं
> मधूनामुवकानां जन्मत्तेन संबंधिभिर्घृतैरन्नॆः पुरोडाशादिभिश्च वावृधाते ।
> वर्धयां चक्रतुः । वर्धयतः । तस्माद्वयमपि तमग्निमभिष्टुम इत्यभिप्रायः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*) means of the sacrifice. The ancient priests and sacrificers (प्रदिवः (*pradivaḥ*)), who are the promoters (वर्तनयः (*vartanayaḥ*)) of the sacrifice and desire (इषः (*iṣaḥ*)) their wishes, serve (सचंते हि (*sacaṃte hi*)) Agni, well-born (सुजातं (*sujātaṃ*)), produced well at the time of installation, for strength or for food (वाजाय (*vājāya*)). Moreover heaven and earth (रोदसी (*rodasī*)), who enclose the whole world (वावसाने (*vāvasāne*)), cause him who dwells**NOTE** to grow. Or else, taking अधि (*adhi*) in the sense of "above": him who dwells above the three worlds as fire, lightning and sun, Agni. They cause him to grow (वावृधाते (*vāvṛdhāte*)) with ghee and with food such as the puroḍāśa, connected with the waters (मधूनां (*madhūnāṃ*)). Therefore we too praise that Agni; such is the intention.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)—to the sacrifice
- वर्तनयः (*vartanayaḥ*)—promoters
- इषः (*iṣaḥ*)—desirers of food
- प्रदिवः (*pradivaḥ*)—and ancient; the priests and sacrificers
- सुजातं (*sujātaṃ*)—Agni, who has an excellent birth
- वाजाय (*vājāya*)—for the gain of food
- सचंते हि (*sacaṃte hi*)—serve
- वावसाने (*vāvasāne*)—enclosing the whole world
- रोदसी (*rodasī*)—heaven and earth (printed द्याव्यापृथिविगळु (*dyāvyāpṛthivigaḷu*))
- अधीनासं (*adhīnāsaṃ*)—Agni, who has variety of forms in the three worlds
- मधूनां (*madhūnāṃ*)—which are causes of water
- धृतैः (*dhṛtaiḥ*)—with oblations of ghee
- अन्नैः (*annaiḥ*)—with puroḍāśa and the like
- वावृधाते (*vāvṛdhāte*)—make him grow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The ancient priests and sacrificers, who are promoters of the sacrifice and desirers of food, serve Agni, who has an excellent birth, for the gain of food. Heaven and earth, who enclose the whole world, make him grow, Agni who pervades the three worlds with variety of forms, with oblations of ghee, which are causes of water, and with foods such as the puroḍāśa.

##### English translation
The ancient celebrators of the rite desiring (boons) wait on (Agni) rightly born, for the sake of food : the all-comprising heaven and earth cherish him, who abides above the heavens, with the sacrificial butter and food (produced) from the water.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒प्त स्वस्व॒ररु॑षीर्वावशा॒नो वि॒द्वान्मध्व॒ उज्ज॑भारा दृ॒शे कं ॥  
> अं॒तर्ये॑मे अं॒तरि॑क्षे पुरा॒जा इ॒च्छन्व॒विम॑विदत्तूष॒णस्य॑ ॥

### Printed p. 270

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सप्त । स्वस्थाः । अरुषीः । वावशानः । विद्वान् । मध्वः । उत् । जभार । दृश्ये । कं ॥  
> अंतः । येमे । अंतरिक्षे । पुराजाः । इच्छन् । वव्रिं । अविदत् । पूषणस्य ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> नावशानः कामयमानो वाक्यमानः स्तोतृभिः स्तूयमानो वा विद्वान्
> सर्वं जानानः सोऽग्निररुषीरारोचमानाः सप्त सप्त संख्याकाः स्वस्थः स्वयंसारिणीः
> काली कराली चेत्यादिकाः सप्त जिह्वा मध्वो मदकराद्यज्ञादुज्जभार ! उज्जहार ।
> ऊर्ध्वं हृतवान् । यज्ञे स्वतेजसा प्रादुर्भूत इत्यर्थः । किमर्थं कं सुखेन
> वृशे सर्वर्षामेव पदार्थानां दर्शनार्थं ! किंच पुराजाः पुराजातः सोऽग्निर्दावापृथिव्योरंतर्मध्यॆऽंतरिक्षे स्थितस्ता जिह्वा येमे । नियमितवान् । सर्वत्र
> तेजोयुक्तो वर्तत इत्यर्थः । अपि चेच्छन्यजमानान्कामयमानोऽग्निः पूषणस्य । पूषेति पृथिवीनाम । पार्थिवस्य लोकस्य वन्रिं रूपं पृश्निवर्णमविदत् ।
> प्रायच्छत् । यद्वा मूर्धा भुवो भवति नक्तमग्निस्ततः सूर्यो जायते प्रातरुद्यन् ! ऋ. १-८-६ । इति मंत्रांतरे दर्शनादादित्यात्माग्निः स्तूयते । कामयमानः सूर्यात्माग्निः सप्तसंख्याकान् स्वस्व रश्मीन् मध्वः समुद्रोदकादुद्धृतवान् । प्राप्तः : अंतरिक्षे विद्युदात्मना स्थित्वा वृष्टिलक्षणं रूपं पृथिव्याः प्रयच्छति॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Agni is वावशानः (*vāvaśānaḥ*), either desiring (the sacrificers) or praised by the worshippers, and विद्वान् (*vidvān*), knowing everything. He has lifted up (उज्जभार (*ujjabhāra*), "carried upward") from the intoxicating sacrifice (मध्वः (*madhvaḥ*)) the seven radiant (अरुषीः (*aruṣīḥ*)) flames, which are स्वस्थाः (*svasthāḥ*), "self-moving"; these are the seven tongues, Kālī, Karālī and the rest. The meaning is that he has appeared in the sacrifice by his own brilliance. The purpose is कं (*kaṃ*), "happily", and दृशे (*dṛśe*), so that all things may be seen.

Further, Agni, born of old (पुराजाः (*purājāḥ*)), stands within, in the middle region between heaven and earth, and has restrained (येमे (*yeme*)) those tongues. That is, he is present everywhere endowed with brilliance. Moreover, desiring the sacrificers, Agni gave (अविदत् (*avidat*) is glossed प्रायच्छत् (*prāyacchat*)) to Pūṣan, that is, to the earth (*Pūṣā* being a name of the earth), the form of the earthly world, the dappled colour.

Alternatively, because another mantra shows it (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-8-6, मूर्धा भुवो भवति नक्तमग्निस्ततः सूर्यो जायते प्रातरुद्यन् (*mūrdhā bhuvo bhavati naktamagnistataḥ sūryo jāyate prātarudyan*)), the Agni who is the sun is praised here. Desiring, the sun-Agni drew up his own seven rays from the water of the sea. Standing in the middle region as lightning, he gives the earth the form that consists of rain.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नावशानः (*nāvaśānaḥ*)—praised by the worshippers
- विद्वान् (*vidvān*)—Agni, who knows everything
- अरुषीः (*aruṣīḥ*)—attractive ones
- सप्त (*sapta*)—seven in number
- स्वस्यः (*svasyaḥ*)—self-spreading; of these flames
- मध्वः (*madhvaḥ*)—from the sacrificial hall
- कं (*kaṃ*)—happily
- दृशे (*dṛśe*)—so as to be visible to all
- उज्जभार (*ujjabhāra*)—has raised aloft and spread
- पुराजाः (*purājāḥ*)—Agni, born before
- अंतः (*aṃtaḥ*)—the middle of heaven and earth
- अंतरिक्षे (*aṃtarikṣe*)—abiding in the atmosphere
- येमे (*yeme*)—has restrained his flames
- इच्चन् (*iccan*)—desiring to favour the sacrificers
- पूषणस्य (*pūṣaṇasya*)—to the earthly world
- वप्रिं (*vapriṃ*)—the form of dappled colour
- अविदत् (*avidat*)—has given

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Agni, who is praised by the worshippers and knows everything, has raised up and spread from the sacrificial hall the seven attractive, self-spreading flames, so that they may be seen happily by all.

### Printed p. 271
He, born before, abides in the atmosphere, the middle region of heaven and earth, and has restrained his flames. Desiring to favour the sacrificers, he has given the earthly world the form of dappled (many-coloured) colour.

##### English translation
The wise Agni glorified (by his worshippers) has from the sacrifice raised aloft, for the ready manifestation of (all things), the seven radiant sisters (flames) ; which when formealy engendered he restrained in thr firma ment, until desirous of (adorers) he bestowed form upon the region of the earth.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

नावशानः (*nāvaśānaḥ*)—For this word Yāska gives the etymology: नावशानो वष्टेर्वा वाश्यतेर्वा सप्त स्वस्था रारुषीर्वावशानः इत्यपि निगमो भवति (*nāvaśāno vaṣṭervā vāśyatervā sapta svasthā rāruṣīrvāvaśānaḥ ityapi nigamo bhavati*) (Ni. 5-1). The word वावशानः (*vāvaśānaḥ*) may be formed either from the root वर् (*var*), meaning "to desire", or from the root वाश् (*vāś*), meaning "to sound loudly". Therefore the word नावशानः (*nāvaśānaḥ*) means "desiring" or "sounding".

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒प्त म॒र्यादाः॑ क॒वय॑स्ततक्षु॒स्तासा॒मेका॒मिद॒भ्यं॑हु॒रोगा॑त् ॥  
> आ॒योर्ह॑ स्कं॒भ उ॑प॒मस्य॑ नी॒ळे प॒थां वि॑स॒र्गे ध॒रुणे॑षु  
> तस्थौ ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सप्त : मर्यादाः । कवयः । ततक्षुः । तासां । एकां ! इत् । अभि । अहुरःगात् ॥  
> अयोः । ह । स्कंभः । उपमस्य । नीळे । पथां । विसर्गे । धरुणेषु । तस्मा ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> कवयो मेधाविन ऋषयः सप्त मर्यादाः : कामजेभ्यः क्रोधजेभ्यश्लोद्धृताः पानमक्षाः स्त्रियो मृगया दंडः पारुष्यमन्यदूषणमिति सप्त
> मर्यादाः । यद्वा । स्तेयं गुरुतल्पारोहणं ब्रह्महत्यां सुरापानं दुष्कृत-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The कवयः (*kavayaḥ*) are the wise seers. They made seven limits (मर्यादाः (*maryādāḥ*)), which are held back from the sins born of desire and born of anger. These are: drinking, dice, women, hunting, punishment (दंडः (*daṃḍaḥ*)), harshness of speech, and slandering others. Alternatively, the seven limits are those set out in the Nirukta (Ni. 6-27): theft, ascending the teacher's bed, brahmin-killing, drinking of surā, and the repeated practice of an evil deed. The list continues on the next page.

### Printed p. 272

> कर्मणः पुनःपुनःसेवां पातकेऽनृतोद्यं । नि. ६-२७ । इति निरुक्ते निर्दिष्टाः
> सत्त मर्यादाः । अवस्थितास्ततः । तक्षतिः करोटिकर्मा । सुष्टु लंघनीयाश्चक्रुः ।
> वृत्तवंत इत्यर्थः । शासां मर्यादानामेकामिदेकामेवांहुरः पापवान्
> पुरुषोऽभि गात् अभिगच्छति । यस्त्वेवं करोति स पापवान्भवतीत्यर्थः । किंचायोस्तस्य मनुष्यस्य स्कंभः स्कंभयिता निरोधकोऽग्निरुपमस्य समीपभूतस्यायोर्नीळे स्थाने पथामादित्यरूपस्य स्वस्य रश्मीनां विसर्गे विसर्जनस्थानेऽंतरिक्षमध्ये
> वर्तमानेषु धरुणेषूदकेषु तस्थौ । विद्युदात्मना शिष्ठति । अनेन लोकत्रयवर्तित्वमिति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

(Continuing the Nirukta list, Ni. 6-27:) the seventh is speaking untruth in the matter of a sin (पातकेऽनृतोद्यं (*pātake 'nṛtodyaṃ*)). These are the seven limits that stand fixed. The verb तक्षति (*takṣati*) has the sense of "doing"; the seers made them well to be crossed (*suṣṭu laṃghanīyāḥ*), that is, they established them.**NOTE**. Of these limits, a sinful man comes to (transgresses) even one. The meaning is that whoever acts so becomes a sinner.

Moreover, Agni is the obstructor (स्कंभः (*skaṃbhaḥ*)) of that man. He stands in the place of the near man (उपमस्य (*upamasya*)), in the place of release of the rays of his own form as the sun, in the middle of the atmosphere, among the waters (धरुणेषु (*dharuṇeṣu*)) that are present there. He stands as lightning. By this his presence in the three worlds is shown.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- कवयः (*kavayaḥ*)—the wise seers
- सप्त मर्यादाः (*sapta maryādāḥ*)—seven kinds of rules of conduct
- वतक्षु: (*vatakṣu:*)—have established (having shown the seven kinds of sins, they have restrained them)
- तासां (*tāsāṃ*)—among them
- एकं इत् (*ekaṃ it*)—even if only one is practised
- अंहुरः (*aṃhuraḥ*)—the man, becoming a sinner
- अभि गात् (*abhi gāt*)—he becomes
- आयोः (*āyoḥ*)—for man
- स्कंभः (*skaṃbhaḥ*)—Agni, the obstructor of that sin
- उपमस्य (*upamasya*)—near
- नीळे (*nīḷe*)—in the place
- पथां (*pathāṃ*)—for the rays of the sun
- विसर्गे (*visarge*)—in the atmosphere, the place of spreading
- धरुणेषु (*dharuṇeṣu*)—in the waters
- तस्थौ (*tasthau*)—he is established

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The wise seers have pointed out the seven kinds of sins, drinking, dice and the like, and have established the rules of conduct that restrain them. If a man practises even one of them, he becomes a sinner. Agni, the restrainer of that sin, abides for man in the place near him and in the atmosphere, the place of spreading for the sun's rays.

##### English translation
The wise have established seven rules of conduct ; he is a sinner who deviates from any one of them Agni is the obstructor of (that) man, he abides in the dwelling of his neighbour, in the region of the scattering of the rays, and in the recipient (waters).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

सप्तमर्यादाः (*saptamaryādāḥ*)—In this mantra the nature of the sin that man may commit and its kinds are told, and there is praise of Agni who holds back such sin. It indicates the relation between spiritual principle and the moral life of man. It describes Agni, the basis of the whole universe of being and non-being, as prior to creation, and the creation of the world as from that basic principle, and all the variety of the world as only a manifestation of that principle. The present mantra praises Agni's quality of restraining sin, in that this basic principle, being in the form of Agni, prevents man from committing sin.

### Printed p. 273
By सप्त मर्यादाः (*sapta maryādāḥ*) the kinds of sin are told. मर्यादाः (*maryādāḥ*) is explained here as "sin". In ordinary usage the word मर्यादा (*maryādā*) commonly means a boundary (limit); its meaning is a rule of conduct or a limit. If it is explained here as "sin", the sense is "mar-ya + ada", that which eats man.

This sin is said to be sevenfold. They are:
1. 1) Pāna—the drinking of liquor and other forbidden things.
2) Akṣāḥ—gambling such as dice, and the cheating that belongs to it.
3) Striyaḥ—addiction to women and its consequences.
4) Mṛgayā—hunting and harming the wild animals of the forest.
5) Daṇḍaḥ—punishing others unjustly (not the king's punishment).
6) Pāruṣyaṁ—cruel speech and conduct that distress the minds of others.
7) Anyadūṣaṇaṁ—slandering others unjustly.

They are called the seven limits. In explaining these numbers Sāyaṇa has also cited the Nirukta author's statement made in this connection. The enumeration there is slightly different. According to the Nirukta passage the kinds of sin are as follows:
सप्तैव मर्यादाः कवयश्चक्रुस्तासामेकामप्यभिगच्छन्नंहस्वान् भवति । स्तेयंतल्पा रोहणं ब्रह्महत्यां भ्रूणहत्यां सुरापानं दुष्कृ तस्य कर्मणः पुनःपुनःसेवां पातकेऽनृतोद्यमिति ॥ (*saptaiva maryādāḥ kavayaścakrustāsāmekāmapyabhigacchannaṃhasvān bhavati | steyaṃtalpā rohaṇaṃ brahmahatyāṃ bhrūṇahatyāṃ surāpānaṃ duṣkṛ tasya karmaṇaḥ punaḥpunaḥsevāṃ pātake 'nṛtodyamiti ‖*) (Ni. 6-27).
1. 1) Steya—committing theft.
2) Talpārohaṇa—lusting after the teacher's wife and trespassing on her bed.
3) Brahmahatyā—killing a brahmin.
4) Bhrūṇahatyā—destroying the embryo.
5) Surāpāna—consuming liquor and other forbidden things.

### Printed p. 274
6) Duṣkṛtasya karmaṇaḥ punaḥ punaḥ sevā—resorting to an evil action and doing it again and again.
7) Anṛtodya—telling a lie to justify a sinful act.

According to the Nirukta authors, these seven are called the limits. If a man commits only one of these sins, he is considered a great sinner.

In the Chāndogya and other texts the grave sins are stated to be of five kinds. They are: स्तॆनो हिरण्यस्य सुरां पिबंश्च गुरोस्तल्पमावसन् ब्रह्महा (चैते पतंति चत्वारः) पंचमश्चाचरंस्तैः इति । (*stèno hiraṇyasya surāṃ pibaṃśca gurostalpamāvasan brahmahā (caite pataṃti catvāraḥ) paṃcamaścācaraṃstaiḥ iti |*) (Bhā. U. 5-10-9).**NOTE**
1) stealing gold, 2) drinking surā, 3) trespassing on the bed of the teacher's wife, 4) brahmin-killing: those who do these four deeds are fallen. Fifth, 5) constantly keeping company with the fallen who do these four deeds. It is stated that these five are sinners.

In general these five kinds are also the well-known ones in the Dharmaśāstras and the like. It cannot be improper in the present context to state the order of enumeration in the Dharmaśāstras and the like.

The grave sins are counted as sevenfold in some places and as fivefold in others. Sāyaṇa's statement indicates that among them there are two kinds, born of desire and born of anger. These two kinds are also clearly stated in the Dharmaśāstras and the like. Examining which is born of desire and which of anger, the Yājñavalkya Smṛti says as follows.**NOTE**

As said above, though the Nirukta and the Chāndogya and other Upaniṣads say that the principal sinful acts are seven, the Veda-puruṣa himself says in the Pariśiṣṭa mantras the following. There are many kinds of sins. If a man who has sinned regularly studies from the teacher's mouth the 610 ṛks praising the deity Pavamāna Soma (that is, sūktas 1-67 at the beginning of the 9th Maṇḍala of the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā, in Gāyatrī metre, containing 610 ṛks), the Veda-puruṣa says "I will remove the various sins of such a man and make him pure." They are set out here.
यषयस्तु तपस्तेपुः सर्वे स्वर्गजिगीषवः । तपंतस्तपसोग्रेण पावमानीर्यचोऽब्रुवन् (*yaṣayastu tapastepuḥ sarve svargajigīṣavaḥ | tapaṃtastapasogreṇa pāvamānīryaco 'bruvan*)
(Pariśiṣṭa 18-6)

### Printed p. 275
In former times all the seers, desiring to obtain heaven, performed fierce austerity, studied these Pāvamānī ṛks, and praised them thus as removers of sin.
यन्ने गर्भे वसतः पापमुग्रं यज्जायमानस्य च किंचिदन्यत् । जातस्य च यच्चापि च वर्धतो मे तत्पावमानीभिरहं पुनामि ॥ (*yanne garbhe vasataḥ pāpamugraṃ yajjāyamānasya ca kiṃcidanyat | jātasya ca yaccāpi ca vardhato me tatpāvamānībhirahaṃ punāmi ‖*)
(Sari. 18-7)**NOTE**
The Veda-puruṣa, the Lord of all, says thus: the man who has done extremely fierce sin while dwelling in the womb, then at birth, and after birth while growing, if he studies the Pāvamānī mantras, I will destroy all such sins and make him pure.
मातापित्रोर्यन्न कृतं वचो मे यत्स्थावरं जंगममाबभूव । विश्वस्य तत्पृहृषितं वचो मे तत्पावमानीभिरहं पुनामि ॥ (*mātāpitroryanna kṛtaṃ vaco me yatsthāvaraṃ jaṃgamamābabhūva | viśvasya tatpṛhṛṣitaṃ vaco me tatpāvamānībhirahaṃ punāmi ‖*)
(Sari. 18-8)**NOTE**
The sin of disobeying the word of father and mother, and the sin that arises from speech spoken wishing harm to any creature of this world, moving or unmoving, such sin I will remove and make him pure.
गोघ्नात् तस्करत्वात् स्त्रीवधाद्यच्च किल्बिषं । पापकं च चरणेभ्यस्तत्पावमानीभिरहं पुनामि ॥ (*goghnāt taskaratvāt strīvadhādyacca kilbiṣaṃ | pāpakaṃ ca caraṇebhyastatpāvamānībhirahaṃ punāmi ‖*)
(Pari. 18-9)**NOTE**
The sin that may arise from cow-killing, theft, killing of a woman, and the deed of trampling and harming creatures with the feet, I will remove and make him pure.
ब्रह्मवधात्सुरापानात्स्वर्णस्तेयाद्वृषलिगमनमैथुनसंगमात् । गुरोर्दाराधिगमनाच्च तत्पावमानीभिरहं पुनामि ॥ (*brahmavadhātsurāpānātsvarṇasteyādvṛṣaligamanamaithunasaṃgamāt | gurordārādhigamanācca tatpāvamānībhirahaṃ punāmi ‖*)
(Pari. 18-10)**NOTE**
Brahmin-killing, drinking liquor, stealing gold, the sin of having intercourse with a woman called vṛṣalī (one unmarried while her younger sister is married), and the sin of intercourse with the teacher's wife, all these I will remove and make him pure.

### Printed p. 276
The Kannada commentary continues the quotations from the Parishiṣṭa (the text is the Aghamarṣaṇa-style purification verses, each ending "I purify by the Pāvamānī verses"):

बालघ्नान्मातृपितृवधाध्भूमितस्करात्सर्ववर्णगमनमैथुनसंगमात् । पापेभ्यश्च प्रतिग्रहात्सद्यः प्रहरति सर्वदुष्कृतं तत्पावमानीभिरहं पुनामि ॥ (*bālaghnānmātṛpitṛvadhādhbhūmitaskarātsarvavarṇagamanamaithunasaṃgamāt | pāpebhyaśca pratigrahātsadyaḥ praharati sarvaduṣkṛtaṃ tatpāvamānībhirahaṃ punāmi ‖*) (Pari. 18-11) **NOTE**

From killing children, killing one's mother and father, fraudulently seizing land such as fields, union with women of all the low castes, accepting improper gifts, and doing other evil deeds: whatever sins arise from these, I remove all those sins and make him thoroughly pure.

क्रयविक्रयाद्योनिदोषाद्भक्षाद्योज्यात्पतिग्रहात् । असंभोजनाच्चापि नृशंसं तत्पावमानीभिरहं पुनामि ॥ (*krayavikrayādyonidoṣādbhakṣādyojyātpatigrahāt | asaṃbhojanāccāpi nṛśaṃsaṃ tatpāvamānībhirahaṃ punāmi ‖*) (Pari. 18-2) **NOTE**

Buying and selling by unjust means, that is, by fraud; union with women one happens upon; eating forbidden foods; accepting things not fit to be accepted; eating one's meal leaving aside a guest who has come to the house: I remove the sins that arise from these.

दुर्यष्टं दुरधीतं पापं यच्चाज्ञानतो कृतं । अयाजिताश्च संयाज्यास्तत्पानमानीभिरहं पुनामि ॥ (*duryaṣṭaṃ duradhītaṃ pāpaṃ yaccājñānato kṛtaṃ | ayājitāśca saṃyājyāstatpānamānībhirahaṃ punāmi ‖*) (Pari. 18-13) **NOTE**

Performing a sacrifice in another way, leaving aside the manner prescribed by rule; studying the Veda without discipline, a sin done through ignorance; making a sacrifice for those not qualified to sacrifice, and not making it for those qualified: I remove the sins arising from such evil deeds and make him pure.

आमंत्रमन्नं यत्किंचिद्दूयते च हुताशने । संवत्सरकृतं पापं तत्पावमानीभिरहं पुनामि ॥ (*āmaṃtramannaṃ yatkiṃciddūyate ca hutāśane | saṃvatsarakṛtaṃ pāpaṃ tatpāvamānībhirahaṃ punāmi ‖*) (Pari. 18-19) **NOTE**

The sin that arises from offering oblation into the fire without the prescribed recitation of mantras, or while reciting the mantra wrongly, and whatever sins have been committed during the year: I remove all of them and make him thoroughly pure.

ऋतस्य योनयोऽमृतस्य धाम विश्वा देवेभ्यः पुण्यगंधाः । ता न आपः प्र वहंतु पापं शुद्धा गच्छामि सुकृतामु लोकु तत्पावमानीभिरहं पुनामि ॥ (*ṛtasya yonayo 'mṛtasya dhāma viśvā devebhyaḥ puṇyagaṃdhāḥ | tā na āpaḥ pra vahaṃtu pāpaṃ śuddhā gacchāmi sukṛtāmu loku tatpāvamānībhirahaṃ punāmi ‖*) (Pari. 18-15) **NOTE**

### Printed p. 277
May the heavenly waters, which are the chief support of truth or of the sacrifice, which abide in the eternal heaven, and which bring pleasure to the gods, wash away the sins mentioned above. I shall make that man pure and cause him to obtain the highest world.

In the mantras mentioned above, many kinds of sin are described. No one should think that the number of sins ends here. There are many more kinds of sin. It is not possible to describe them all here, and describing them in that way is not relevant to the present context. It should be understood that, in the main, all kinds of sin are contained within the seven kinds of sin mentioned above.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अग॑च्च॒ सच्च॑ पर॒मे व्यो॑म॒न्नक्ष॑स्य॒ जन्म॒न्नदि॑तेरु॒पस्थॆ ॥  ॑
> अ॒ग्निर्ह॑ नः प्रथम॒जा ऋ॒तस्य॒ पूर्व॒ आयु॑निवृष॒भश्च॑ धे॒नुः॥  
> १ कदंबः.

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अगत् । च । सत् । च । परमे । विओमन् । वक्षस्य, । जन्मन् । अदितेः । उपस्थे ।  
> अग्निः । ह, सः । प्रथमजाः । ऋतस्य । पूर्वे । आयुनि । वृषभः । च । धेनुः । ॥७॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> असदव्याकृतं सच्च व्याकृतं । असदेवेदमग्र आसीत् तत्सदासीत् तत्समभवत् । छां. उ. ३-१९-१ इति श्रुतेः । सदसदात्मकं जगत्सर्वं परम उत्तमे
> ज्ञानसहिते व्योमन्वोमनि कारणात्मनि जातमभूत्सदा । आदितेः पृथिव्या उपस्थ
> उपस्थाने समापे दक्षस्य प्रजापतेः । यद्वा । दक्षस्य । स त्रेधात्मानं व्यभजतादित्यं
> तृतीयं वायुं तृतीयमिति श्रुतेः । शत. १०-६-५-३ । तृतीयस्य द्युलोकस्याधिष्ठातृत्वादादित्यस्य : जन्मज्जन्यनि । तत एवं सति मनुष्यस्य सृष्टिक्रमात्तूर्वमग्निर्हाग्निर्वतस्य कर्मफलस्य भोक्तृणां नोऽस्माकं प्रथमजाः प्रथमजातः समुत्पन्नः । तेजो रसो निरवर्तताग्निः । शत. १०-६-५-२ । बृ. उ. १-१-२-२ । इति श्रवणात् । पश्चात्तप्प करणे ततो मनुष्या अजायंतेति प्रथमत एव मनुष्यसृष्टेरभिहितत्वात् अनंतरं पूर्वे । उत्तर सृष्टिमपेक्ष्य पूर्वत्वं । आयुनि कालेय.
> मुग्निरेव वृषभश्चासीत् । धेनुश्काभवत् । स्त्रीपुंसवत् स्त्रीपुंसात्मकोऽभवदित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

असत् (*asat*) is the unmanifest and सत् (*sat*) the manifest; he cites the śruti असदेवेदमग्र आसीत् तत्सदासीत् तत्समभवत् (*asadevedamagra āsīt tatsadāsīt tatsamabhavat*) (Chā. U. 3-19-1). The whole world, made of sat and asat, was always born in the supreme (excellent), knowledge-endowed व्योमन् (*vyoman*), which is the causal self. It is born in the lap of Aditi, that is, the earth, near Dakṣa, that is, Prajāpati. Or else, in the जन्मन् (*janman*) of Dakṣa: he cites the śruti that Prajāpati divided himself threefold, the Āditya as one third and Vāyu as one third (Śata. 10-6-5-3) **NOTE**. Since Āditya presides over the third world, heaven, he is the जन्मन् (*janman*) (the birthplace). Therefore Agni, in the order of the creation of man, was the first-born, produced before us, the enjoyers of the fruit of the rite. He cites the śruti तेजो रसो निरवर्तताग्निः (*tejo raso niravartatāgniḥ*) (Śata. 10-6-5-2; Bṛ. U. 1-1-2-2). Then, since the creation of men is stated as having occurred first, … **NOTE** पूर्वे (*pūrve*) means "earlier" with reference to the later creation. आयुनि (*āyuni*) means in time. Agni himself was the bull and became the cow; that is, like a woman and a man, he was both female and male.

### Printed p. 278

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- असत् (*asat*)—he who has an unmanifest form
- सच्च (*sacca*)—and he who has a manifest form, Agni
- परमे (*parame*)—in the most excellent
- व्योमन् (*vyoman*)—in the atmosphere
- अदितेः (*aditeḥ*)—of the earth
- उपस्थे (*upasthe*)—in the middle part (he has existence)
- दक्षस्य (*dakṣasya*)—of Prajāpati
- जन्मन् (*janman*)—in the heaven-world which is the place of origin (he has existence)
- अग्निर्ह (*agnirha*)—Agni indeed
- ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)—of those who perform the rite
- नः (*naḥ*)—than us
- प्रथमजाः (*prathamajāḥ*)—has been born first
- पूर्वे (*pūrve*)—that existed before the creation of the world
- आयुनि (*āyuni*)—in the time
- वृषभश्च (*vṛṣabhaśca*)—Agni himself was the bull
- धेनुः च (*dhenuḥ ca*)—and also the cow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Agni, who has an unmanifest form and a manifest form, has existence in the most excellent atmosphere, in the middle part of the earth, and in the heaven-world which is the birthplace of Prajāpati. Agni was born first, before us who perform rites. Before the creation of the world, Agni himself was of male form and of female form.

##### English translation
Non-existent, and existent (is Agni) in the supreme heaven, in the birth-place of Daksha, and in the presence of Aditi : Agni is elder born to us (who enjoy the fruit) of the sacrifice ; in the olden time he was the bull and the cow.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk the seer, having declared the nature of Brahman as both sat and asat, describes the great nature of Agni in another manner.

असत् च सत् च परमे व्योमन् (*asat ca sat ca parame vyoman*)—The words परमे व्योमन् (*parame vyoman*) are words that indicate the unmanifest nature of Brahman, devoid of name and form. This unmanifest nature of the Supreme Brahman is described by Upaniṣad sentences such as असद्वा इदमग्र आसीत् । ततो वै सदजायत ! (*asadvā idamagra āsīt | tato vai sadajāyata !*) (Tai. U. 1-8) **NOTE** and असदेवेदमग्र आसीत् तत्सदासीत् तत्समभवत् (*asadevedamagra āsīt tatsadāsīt tatsamabhavat*) (Bhā. U. 3-19-1) **NOTE**. That is, before creation there was only Brahman without designation of name and form. It had neither shape nor form. It was purely of the nature of knowledge and infinite. This very nature is what the Upaniṣad sentence सत्यं ज्ञानमनंतं ब्रह्म (*satyaṃ jñānamanaṃtaṃ brahma*) (Tai. U. 2-1-1) declares. The unmanifest nature in this state is called असत् (*asat*) in Vedānta works and in the Upaniṣads. This word is a word indicating the negative sense of the word सत् (*sat*), which is formed from the root अस भुवि (*asa bhuvi*). सत् (*sat*) means that which is, that which is perceptible.

### Printed p. 279
असत् (*asat*) means the state without name and form; its state cannot be seen, cannot be rightly known. Even so, in such a Supreme Brahman of attributeless nature there are the qualities of truth, knowledge and infinity; and from the manifestation of those qualities, that is, of the guṇas sattva, rajas and tamas, that principle at the time of creation took a manifest form and acquired the nature of being perceptible to all. This is what is called by the word सत् (*sat*). This state called सत् (*sat*) exists only during the times of creation, and at the time of dissolution, step by step, it becomes one with the Brahman-substance and remains in an unmanifest form as before creation. Since this subject belongs to the Vedānta inquiry taught in the Upaniṣads, it is not possible to explain it at length in the explanation of the present Ṛk. The main point is this: the seer has said that from the Supreme Brahman, devoid of qualities, infinite like space without name and form, this whole world made of sat and asat came into being. This matter will be explained at length elsewhere later.

दक्षस्य जन्मन्नदितेः उपस्थे (*dakṣasya janmannaditeḥ upasthe*)—The seer has said that at the time of creation Prajāpati first created Agni on the earth; or else, according to the Śatapatha-Brāhmaṇa sentence स त्रेधात्मानं व्यभजतादित्यं तृतीयं वायुं तृतीयं (*sa tredhātmānaṃ vyabhajatādityaṃ tṛtīyaṃ vāyuṃ tṛtīyaṃ*), Prajāpati, desiring to create, divided himself into three parts. Among those parts one is Āditya. Since it is said that Agni himself exists in heaven in the form of Āditya, the statement that he first created Āditya means that he created Agni. The reason why it is said, when creation is made, त्रेधात्मानं व्यभजत (*tredhātmānaṃ vyabhajata*), that Prajāpati divided himself into three parts, is this: before creation only Brahman (Prajāpati), who was in unmanifest form, existed, and since there was no other substance with which to create, he divided himself; this is what is said here. From this statement it must be understood that all the things, moving and unmoving, now seen in the world are parts of Brahman, and that there is no substance other than Brahman. Moreover, in the Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad too it is said सह तेजोरसो निरवर्तताग्निः (*saha tejoraso niravartatāgniḥ*) (Bṛ. U. 1-1-2-2): at the time of creation, from the Supreme Brahman, splendour in the form of essence came into being, that is, came out. This śruti sentence also supports the point that Agni was created first.

अग्निः ह नः प्रथमजाः ऋतस्य पूर्वे आयुनि वृषभः च धेनुः (*agniḥ ha naḥ prathamajāḥ ṛtasya pūrve āyuni vṛṣabhaḥ ca dhenuḥ*)—As said above, after Prajāpati created Agni he created us, who experience the fruit of rites, that is, men. That is, Agni was produced before the creation of men. And at that very time, that is, when Agni was created, with the desire that men should be created afterwards, Agni himself became one with a form that is both male and female, "bull and cow". Here the word वृषभ (*vṛṣabha*) denotes the male and the word धेनु (*dhenu*) the female. The intention is that Prajāpati created the distinction of female and male in order to bring about increase of progeny among men.

### Printed p. 280
The colophon verse and closing lines of the Sāyaṇa text:

वेदार्थस्य प्रकाशेन तमो हार्दं निवारयन् । प्रमथांश्च तुरो देयाद्विद्यातीर्थमहेश्वरः ॥ (*vedārthasya prakāśena tamo hārdaṃ nivārayan | pramathāṃśca turo deyādvidyātīrthamaheśvaraḥ ‖*)

इति श्रीमद्राजाधिराजपरमेश्वरवैदिकमार्गप्रवर्तकश्रीवीरबुक्कभूपालसाम्राज्यधुरंधरेण सायणाचार्येण विरचिते माधवीये वेदार्थप्रकाश ऋक्संहिताभाष्येऽसप्तमाष्टके पंचमोऽध्यायः समाप्तः ॥ (*iti śrīmadrājādhirājaparameśvaravaidikamārgapravartakaśrīvīrabukkabhūpālasāmrājyadhuraṃdhareṇa sāyaṇācāryeṇa viracite mādhavīye vedārthaprakāśa ṛksaṃhitābhāṣye 'saptamāṣṭake paṃcamo 'dhyāyaḥ samāptaḥ ‖*)

Here ends the fifth chapter in the seventh aṣṭaka of the Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, with the bhāṣya of Śrī Sāyaṇa and with Kannada translation.

ओं तत्स्यत् (*oṃ tatsyat*)

यदक्षरपदभ्रष्टं मात्राहीनं तु यद्भवेत् । तत्सर्वं क्षम्यतां देव वागीश्वर नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ (*yadakṣarapadabhraṣṭaṃ mātrāhīnaṃ tu yadbhavet | tatsarvaṃ kṣamyatāṃ deva vāgīśvara namo 'stu te ‖*)

शुभं भूयात् (*śubhaṃ bhūyāt*)

मंगळं (*maṃgaḷaṃ*)

श्री महागणाधिसतये नमः (*śrī mahāgaṇādhisataye namaḥ*)

वागीशाद्याः सुमनसः सर्वार्थानामुकप्रमे । यं नत्वा कृतकृत्याः स्यास्तं नमूमि गजाननं ॥ (*vāgīśādyāḥ sumanasaḥ sarvārthānāmukaprame | yaṃ natvā kṛtakṛtyāḥ syāstaṃ namūmi gajānanaṃ ‖*)

यस्य निश्चसितं वेदा यो वेदेष्टोऽखिलं जगत् । निर्ममे तमहं वंदे विद्यातीर्थमहेश्वरं ॥ (*yasya niścasitaṃ vedā yo vedeṣṭo 'khilaṃ jagat | nirmame tamahaṃ vaṃde vidyātīrthamaheśvaraṃ ‖*)

वेदः शिवः शिवो वेदो वेबाध्याया सदाशिवः । तस्मात्सर्वप्रयत्नेन वेदमेव सदा जपेत् ॥ (*vedaḥ śivaḥ śivo vedo vebādhyāyā sadāśivaḥ | tasmātsarvaprayatnena vedameva sadā japet ‖*)

सप्तमस्टाष्टकस्येत्पं व्याकार्षित्पंचनं सुधीः । सायणाह्वस्ततः षष्ठव ध्यायं व्याचिकीर्षति ॥ (*saptamasṭāṣṭakasyetpaṃ vyākārṣitpaṃcanaṃ sudhīḥ | sāyaṇāhvastataḥ ṣaṣṭhava dhyāyaṃ vyācikīrṣati ‖*)

Ṛgveda-saṃhitā with the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya. अथ सप्तमाष्टके षष्ठोऽध्यायः (*atha saptamāṣṭake ṣaṣṭho 'dhyāyaḥ*) — the sixth chapter in the seventh aṣṭaka. The sixth sūkta in the tenth maṇḍala.

## Sūkta 6 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दशमे मंडले प्रथमेऽनुवाके षोडशसूक्तानि । तत्रायं स यस्येति
> सप्तर्चं षष्ठं सूक्तं । आप्त्यस्य त्रितस्यार्षं त्रैष्टुभवाग्नेयं । अयमित्यनुक्रांतं ॥ प्रातरनुवाकाश्विनशस्त्रयोरुक्तो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the tenth maṇḍala, in the first anuvāka, there are sixteen sūktas. Among them the sixth sūkta is अयं स यस्य (*ayaṃ sa yasya*), of seven ṛks. Its seer is Trita Āptya, its deity is Agni, its metre is Triṣṭubh; the Anukramaṇī says अयं (*ayaṃ*) as its beginning. The viniyoga was stated earlier for the Prātaranuvāka and the Āśvina-śastra.

Translation: In the first anuvāka of the tenth maṇḍala of the Ṛgveda-saṃhitā there are sixteen sūktas. Among them this sūkta beginning अयं स यस्य (*ayaṃ sa yasya*) is the sixth sūkta in the first anuvāka. It has seven ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is one named Trita Āptya; the deity is Agni; the metre is Triṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇī it is said to begin with अयं (*ayaṃ*).

### Printed p. 282
The viniyoga of this sūkta for the recitation of the Prātaranuvāka and the Āśvina-śastra mantras has already been stated earlier.

Sūkta 6. Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 1; Sūkta 6. Aṣṭaka 7; Adhyāya 6; Varga 1. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 7. Seer: Trita Āptya. Deity: Agni. Metre: Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒यं स यस्य॒ शर्म॒न्नवो॑भिर॒ग्नेरेध॑ते जरि॒ताभिष्टौ॑ ।  
> ज्येष्ठे॑भि॒र्यो भा॒नुभि॑र्युषू॒णां प॒र्येति॒ परि॑वीतो  ॒॑
> विभावा ॥१॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अयं । सः । यस्य । शर्मन् । अवःभिः । अग्नेः । विधते । जरिताभिष्टौ ।  
> जैष्टेभिः । यः । भानुभिः । ऋषूणां । परिएति । परिवीतः । विभावा ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> जरिता स्तोताभिष्टौ यागे कृते यस्याग्नेरवोभिः पालनैः शर्मञ्छर्मणि
> स्वगृह एधते वर्धते सोऽयमग्निर्यागार्थमस्माभिराहूत इति शेषः । विभावा
> दीप्तिमान् योऽग्निर्ऋषूणां सूर्यरश्मीनां ज्येष्ठेभिः प्रशस्तैर्भानुभिस्तेजोभिः परिवीतः परिवृतः पर्योति सर्वतो गच्छति । यत्र यत्र गंतव्यं तत्र तत्राप्रतिहतगतिर्गच्छतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

जरिता (*jaritā*) is the praiser. When the sacrifice (अभिष्टौ (*abhiṣṭau*)) has been performed, that Agni, by whose protections (अवोभिः (*avobhiḥ*)) he grows in his own house (शर्मन् (*śarman*))—this Agni, called by us for the sacrifice (this is the word to be supplied). विभावा (*vibhāvā*) means radiant. The Agni who is परिवीतः (*parivītaḥ*), surrounded, by the excellent (ज्येष्ठेभिः (*jyeṣṭhebhiḥ*)) splendours (भानुभिः (*bhānubhiḥ*)) of the sun's rays (ऋषूणां (*ṛṣūṇāṃ*)), goes about (पर्येति (*paryeti*)) everywhere; that is, wherever he must go, he goes there with unobstructed motion.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- जरिता (*jaritā*)—the praiser
- अभिष्टौ (*abhiṣṭau*)—at the time of his sacrifice
- यस्य अग्नेः (*yasya agneḥ*)—of which Agni
- अवोभिः (*avobhiḥ*)—by the protections
- शर्मन् (*śarman*)—in the sacrificial house
- विधते (*vidhate*)—(he who) attains increase
- सः आयं (*saḥ āyaṃ*)—he, this Agni
- विभावा (*vibhāvā*)—radiant
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which same Agni

### Printed p. 283

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऋषोणां (*ṛṣoṇāṃ*)—of the sun's rays
- ज्येष्ठेभिः (*jyeṣṭhebhiḥ*)—excellent
- भानुभिः (*bhānubhiḥ*)—with splendours
- परिवीतः (*parivītaḥ*)—being surrounded
- पर्येति (*paryeti*)—moves about everywhere

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

This is that Agni by whose protections the worshipper prospers in the sacrificial house at the time of his sacrifice. The radiant and famed Agni, surrounded by the excellent splendours of the sun's rays, moves about everywhere.

##### English translation
This is that Agni by whose protections the worshipper (diligent) in sacrifice prospers in his dwelling : who radiant, travels everywhere invested with the excellent splendours of his rays.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यो भा॒नुभि॑र्वि॒भावा॑ वि॒भात्य॒ग्निर्दे॒वेभि॑र्य॒तावाज॑स्रः ।  
> आ यो वि॒नाय॑ स॒ख्या सख॒भ्योऽप॑रिह्वतो॒ अत्यो॒ न सप्तिः॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यः । भानुभिः । विभावा । विभाति । अग्निः । देवेभिः । ऋतवा । अजस्रः ॥  
> आ । यः । विनाय । सख्या । सखभ्यः । अपरिह्वतः । अत्यः । न । सप्तिः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऋतवा सत्यवानुदकवान्यज्ञवान्वाजस्रोऽनुपक्षीणोऽहिंसितो वा । केनचिदनभिभूत इत्यर्थः । विभावा दीप्तिमान्योग्निर्देवेभिर्देवैर्दॊतमानैर्भानुभिस्तेजोभिर्विभाति विविधं दीप्यते किंच योऽग्निः सख्या सख्यानि सखिभिः कर्तव्यानि
> कर्माणि हविरादीनि । कर्तुम्मिति शेषः । सखिभ्यः सखिभूतानां यजमानानामर्थायापरिह्वतोऽपरिहिंसितः । अपरिश्रांत इत्यर्थः ! आ निवाय अभिमुख्येन गच्छति ।
> तत्र दृष्टांतः । सप्तिः । सदासर्पण इत्यर्थः । अत्यो न यथा सततगमनसमर्थोऽश्वोऽपरिश्रांतो गच्छति तद्वत् । सोऽयमग्निरस्माभिर्यागार्थमाहूत इति पूर्ववद्योज्यं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

ऋतवा (*ṛtavā*) means truthful, possessed of water, or possessed of sacrifice; अजस्रः (*ajasraḥ*) means undiminished or unharmed, that is, overpowered by no one. Agni, the radiant one, shines variously with the brilliant splendours that are the gods'. Further, he comes face to face with the friendly acts, the oblations and the like, that friends are to perform, for the sake of the sacrificers who are his friends, unharmed and untired. The example: like a swift horse, ever running, able to go continually, that goes untired. This Agni has been invoked by us for the sacrifice; it is to be construed as before.

### Printed p. 284

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यतवा (*yatavā*)—truthful
- अजस्रः (*ajasraḥ*)—undecaying
- विभावा (*vibhāvā*)—radiant
- यः अग्निः (*yaḥ agniḥ*)—which Agni
- देवेभिः (*devebhiḥ*)—shining
- भानुभिः (*bhānubhiḥ*)—with splendours
- विभाति (*vibhāti*)—shines exceedingly
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Agni
- अपरिह्वतः (*aparihvataḥ*)—without the least sloth
- सप्तिः (*saptiḥ*)—ever moving
- अत्यो न (*atyo na*)—like a horse
- आ विवाय (*ā vivāya*)—comes to the sacrificers
- सखिभ्यः (*sakhibhyaḥ*)—to his friends, the sacrificers and others
- सख्या (*sakhyā*)—he comes to accept the oblations and the like of those friends (he comes to favour them)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The truthful, undecaying, radiant Agni who shines exceedingly with brilliant splendours, and who comes to the sacrificers untiringly like a horse ever moving, comes to his friends, the sacrificers and others, to accept their oblations and to favour them.

##### English translation
Agni, the observer of truth, the eternal, who shines resplendent with celestial splendours, who comes untiring like a swift horse, (bringing) frendly gifts to his friends.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ईशे॒ यो विश्व॑स्या दे॒वपी॑ते॒रीशे॑ वि॒श्वायु॑रु॒षसो॒ व्यु॑ष्टौ ।  
> आ यस्मि॑न्म॒ना ह॒वींष्य॒ग्नावरि॑ष्टरथः स्क॒भ्नाति॑ शू॒षैः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ईशे । यः । विश्वस्याः । देवपीतेः । ईशे । विश्वआयुः । उषसः । विउष्टौ ।  
> आ । यस्मिन् : मना । हविरिषि । अग्नौ । अरिष्टरथः । स्वभ्नाति । शूष्टैः । ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> योग्निर्विश्वस्या देववीतेः सर्वस्य यज्ञस्थेशे ईष्टे । अपि च विश्वायु
> सर्वत्राप्रतिहतगमन उषसो व्युष्टौ विवासन उदयकाले होमार्थं यजमा-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Agni who rules over all the god-feast (देववीतेः (*devavīteḥ*)) of the sacrifice; moreover, going unimpeded everywhere, at the dawning of the dawn, at the time of rising, he rules over the sacrificers for the sake of the offering.

### Printed p. 285

> नानामीशे ईष्टे । शूषैः शत्रुबलैररिष्टरथोऽहिंसितयज्ञरथः सर्व एव यजमानो यस्मिन्नग्नौ मना मननीयानि हवींष्या स्कभ्नाति आदरेण प्रतिबध्नाति प्रक्षिपति।
> सोऽयमग्निरस्माभिर्यागार्थमाहूत इत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

He rules over the sacrificers. Every sacrificer, whose sacrificial chariot is unharmed by the hostile forces, in whatever Agni he lays down, with regard, the oblations worthy of thought (मना मननीयानि (*manā mananīyāni*)), he sets them up, restrains them, casts them in. This Agni has been invoked by us for the sacrifice.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Agni
- विश्वस्याः (*viśvasyāḥ*)—of all
- देववीतेः (*devavīteḥ*)—of the sacrifice too
- ईशे (*īśe*)—is lord
- विश्वायुः (*viśvāyuḥ*)—able to move about everywhere
- उषसः (*uṣasaḥ*)—of the dawn
- व्युष्पौ (*vyuṣpau*)—at the time of rising
- ईशे (*īśe*)—is the master
- यस्मिन् (*yasmin*)—in which
- अग्नौ (*agnau*)—Agni
- शून्यै (*śūnyai*)—by hostile forces
- अरिष्यरथः (*ariṣyarathaḥ*)—the sacrificer whose chariot is unharmed
- मनाः (*manāḥ*)—meditative
- हवींषि (*havīṃṣi*)—oblations
- आ स्सभाति (*ā ssabhāti*)—offers; that Agni we invoke

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Agni who is lord of every sacrifice, who, able to move about everywhere, assumes the mastership at the very rising of the dawn, and in whom the sacrificer, whose chariot is unharmed by hostile forces, offers oblations accompanied by meditation, that Agni we invoke.

##### English translation
Who rules over every oblation to the gods. who all- pervading rules at the opening of the dawn, and in whom (the sacrificer), whose sacrifice is undisturbed by his foes, throws his choice oblations,—

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> शू॒षेभि॑र्वृ॒धो जु॑षा॒णो अ॒र्कैर्दे॒वा अच्छ॑ रघु॒पत्वा॑  
> जिगाति ।  
> मं॒द्रो होता॒ स जु॒ह्वा॒३॒॑ ३ यजि॑ष्ठः॒ संमि॑श्लो अ॒ग्निरा ॑ ॒
> जिघर्ति देवान् ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शूषेभिः । वृधः । जुषाणः । अर्कैः । देवान् । अच्छ । रघुपत्वा । जिगाति ।  
> मंद्रः । होता । सः । जुह्वा । यजिष्ठः । संमिश्लो । अग्निः आ । जिघर्ति । देवान् ॥

### Printed p. 286

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वृधो वर्धितोऽग्निः शूषेभिर्हविलक्षणैर्बलैः । बलनिमित्तैर्हविर्भिरित्यर्थः ।
> अर्कैः स्तोत्रैश्च जुषाणः सेव्यमानः सन् देवानिंद्रादीनच्छापुं रघुपत्वा
> लघुपतनः शीघ्रगामि जिगाति । गच्छति । गत्वा च मंद्रः स्तुत्यो होता
> देवानामाह्वाता होमनिष्पादको ना यजिष्टो यष्टृतमः संमिश्लः स्वगुणैर्देवैर्ना संप्रयुक्तः सोऽग्निर्देवान्प्रति हविरा जिघर्ति । आहारयति । देवेभ्यो
> हविर्ददातीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Agni, strengthened by the powers (शूषेभिः (*śūṣebhiḥ*)) that have the nature of oblations, that is, by oblations that cause strength, and served by hymns (अर्कैः (*arkaiḥ*)), goes (जिगाति (*jigāti*)) swift-falling and swift-going towards the gods, Indra and the rest. Having gone, he, the praiseworthy invoker of the gods, the accomplisher of the offering, the most worthy of sacrifice, joined with the gods by his own qualities, brings the oblation (जिघर्ति (*jigharti*)) to the gods; that is, he gives the oblation to the gods.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वृधः (*vṛdhaḥ*)—the nourished Agni
- शूषेभिः (*śūṣebhiḥ*)—by strength-giving oblations
- अर्कैः (*arkaiḥ*)—and by hymns
- जुषाणः (*juṣāṇaḥ*)—being served
- देवान् (*devān*)—the gods, Indra and others
- अच्छ (*accha*)—to go and join
- रघुपत्वा (*raghupatvā*)—swift-going
- जिगाति (*jigāti*)—goes
- मंद्रः (*maṃdraḥ*)—worthy of praise
- होता (*hotā*)—in the form of the Hotṛ
- यजिष्ठः (*yajiṣṭhaḥ*)—most worshipful
- संमिश्लः (*saṃmiślaḥ*)—joined with the hosts of gods
- सः अग्निः (*saḥ agniḥ*)—that same Agni
- देवान् (*devān*)—to the gods
- जुह्वा (*juhvā*)—through his flame
- आ जिघर्ति (*ā jigharti*)—offers oblations and the like

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The nourished Agni, served by strength-giving oblations and hymns, goes swiftly to join Indra and the other gods. That same Agni, worthy of praise, in the form of the Hotṛ, most worshipful, joined with divine qualities, offers oblations and the like to the gods with his vessel of flame.

##### English translation
Magnified by our oblations, propitiated by our praises, swift-moving, he proceeds to the persence of the gods ; the aborable invoker of the deites, most entitled to worship, associated (with the gods), Agni presents to them (the oblation) with the sacrificial ladle.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तमु॒स्रामिंद्रं॒ न रेज॑मानम॒ग्निं गी॒र्भिर्नमो॑भि॒ राकृ॑णुद्ध्वं

### Printed p. 287

> आ यं विप्रासो मतिभिगृणंति जातवेदसं जुह्वं  
> सहानां ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तं । उस्रां । इंद्रं । न । रेजमानं । अग्निं । गीःभिः । नमःभिः । आ । कृणुध्वं ।  
> आ । यं । विप्रासः । मतिभिः । गृणंति । जातवेदसं । जुह्वं । सहानां ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ऋत्विजः यूयमुस्रां भोगानामुत्साविणं दातारं रेजमानं
> ज्वालात्मना कंपमानं तमग्निमिंद्रं नेंद्रमिव गीर्भिः स्तुतिभिर्नमोभिर्हविर्भिश्चा
> कुणुद्वं अस्मदभिमुखं कुरुत । विप्रासो मेधाविनः स्तोतारः सहानां शत्रुबलाद्यभिभवितृणां देवानां जुह्वमाह्वातारं जातवेदसं जातधनं जातप्रज्ञं वा
> यमग्निमा गृणंति आदरेण स्तुवंति । तमा कृणुद्वमिति पूर्वेण संबंधः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O priests, make that Agni face us with praises (गीर्भिः (*gīrbhiḥ*)) and with oblations, as one would Indra: Agni is उस्रां (*usrāṃ*), the fountain and giver of enjoyments, and रेजमानं (*rejamānaṃ*), trembling in the form of flame. The wise singers (विप्रासः (*viprāsaḥ*)) praise with reverence that Agni, the invoker (जुह्वं (*juhvaṃ*)) of the gods, the overpowerers of the forces of enemies and the like, जातवेदसं (*jātavedasaṃ*), that is, possessed of wealth or of knowledge. The connection is with the earlier "make him face us".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O priests) विप्रासः (*viprāsaḥ*)—wise praisers
- सहानां (*sahānāṃ*)—to the mighty gods
- जुह्वं (*juhvaṃ*)—the one who gives the invitation
- जातवेदसं (*jātavedasaṃ*)—in the form of Jātavedas
- यं (*yaṃ*)—which Agni
- मतिभिः (*matibhiḥ*)—with hymns
- आ गृणंति (*ā gṛṇaṃti*)—praise with reverence, such
- उस्रां (*usrāṃ*)—the giver of enjoyments
- रेजमानं (*rejamānaṃ*)—trembling with flame
- तं अग्निं (*taṃ agniṃ*)—that Agni
- इंद्रं न (*iṃdraṃ na*)—as one calls Indra
- गीर्भिः (*gīrbhiḥ*)—with praises
- नमोभिः (*namobhiḥ*)—with oblations
- आ कृणुध्वं (*ā kṛṇudhvaṃ*)—make him face us

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O priests, that Agni whom the wise praisers praise reverently with hymns, who gives the invitation to the mighty gods and is in the form of Jātavedas, the giver of enjoyments, trembling with flame: make him face us with praises and oblations, as one brings Indra to the sacrifice.

##### English translation
Bring before us with praises, and with adorations, the brilliant Agni, the bestower of enjoyments, as if he were Indra, him, whom wise wor-

### Printed p. 288
shippers praise with hymns as Jatavedas, the sacrificer to the victorious Gods.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सं यस्मि॒न्विश्वा॒ वसू॑नि ज॒ग्मुर्वाजे॒ नाश्वाः॒ सप्ती॑वंत॒ एवैः॑ ।  
> अ॒स्मे ऊ॒तीरिंद्र॑वाततमा अर्वाची॒न अ॑ग्न॒ आ कृ॑णुष्व ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सं । यस्मिन् । विश्वा । वसूनि । जग्मुः । वाजे । न । अश्वाः । सप्तिवंतः । एवैः ।  
> अस्मे । इति । ऊतीः । इंद्रवाततमाः । अर्वाचीनाः । अग्ने । आ । कृणुस्व ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने यस्मिंस्त्वयि विश्वानि वसूनि धनानि सं जग्मुः स्वत्वेन संगतानि
> भवंति । तत्र दृष्टांतः । एवैर्गमनैः सप्तीवंतः सर्पणवंतः शीघ्रगमनसमर्था
> अश्वा वाजे न यथा संग्रामे संगतास्तद्वत् । हे अग्ने स त्वमिंद्रवाततमा
> इंद्रेणाभिगता ऊती रक्षा अस्मे अस्त्रास्वर्वाचीना अभिमुखीरा कृणुष्ट । आदरेण
> कुरु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, in you all wealths come together (सं जग्मुः (*saṃ jagmuḥ*)), becoming united as one's own. The example: as horses (अश्वा (*aśvā*)) swift in their going, running with their gaits (एवैः (*evaiḥ*)), gather together in battle. O Agni, you, make the protections (ऊतीः (*ūtīḥ*)), those approached by Indra, face us; do it with care.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- एवैः (*evaiḥ*)—by their swift movements
- सप्तीवंतः (*saptīvaṃtaḥ*)—swift-going
- अश्वाः (*aśvāḥ*)—horses
- वाजे न (*vāje na*)—as they go and join in battle
- यस्मिन् (*yasmin*)—in which you
- विश्वावसूनि (*viśvāvasūni*)—all wealths
- सं जग्मुः (*saṃ jagmuḥ*)—are gathered together, such you
- इंद्रवाततमाः (*iṃdravātatamāḥ*)—more than Indra
- ऊतीः (*ūtīḥ*)—protections
- अस्मे (*asme*)—to us
- अर्वाचीनाः (*arvācīnāḥ*)—facing us
- अ कृणुस्व (*a kṛṇusva*)—make

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, as swift horses go and gather in battle by their rapid movements, so in you all wealths are gathered together; such as you are, give us protections, more than Indra does.

### Printed p. 289

##### English translation
O Agni, in whom all treasures are brought together as fleet horses are brought together in battle : bestow upon us protections even more effective than those of Indra.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अधा॒ ह्य॑ग्ने म॒ह्ना नि॒षद्या॑ स॒द्यो ज॑ज्ञा॒नो हव्यो॑ ब॒भूथ॑ ।  
> तं ते॑ दे॒वासो॒ अनु॒ केत॑माय॒न्नधा॑वर्धंत प्रथ॒मास॒  ॑
> ऊमाः ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अध । हि । अग्ने । मह्ना । निसद्य । सद्यः । जज्ञानः । हव्यः । बभूथ ।  
> तं । ते । देवास । अनु । केतं । आयन् । अध । अवर्दंत । प्रथमासः । ऊमाः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अधानंतरं हि यस्माद्दे अग्ने त्वं महत्वेन जज्ञानो जायमानो
> ज्वलन्निषद्योपविश्य सद्यस्तस्मिन्प्रजननकाल एव हव्यो हवनार्ह आहुतिप्रक्षेपणार्हो
> बभूथ भवसि तस्मात्कारणाद्देवासो हविषां दातार ऋत्विग्यजमानास्ते तव स्वभूतं
> तं तादृशं केतं प्रज्ञानलक्षणमन्वायन् । अनुगच्चंति । दीप्ते त्वयि हवींषि
> जुह्वतीत्यर्थः । अधानंतरमृत्विगादयः प्रथमासः क्रियाभिजनविद्यागुणैरुत्कृष्टा
> ऊनास्त्वया रक्षिताः संतोऽवर्धंत । वर्धंते !

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"Then, because you, Agni, being born through your greatness, having sat down, become at once, at the very time of birth, fit to be invoked, fit to receive the offered oblation, for that reason the देवासो (*devāso*), the givers of the oblations, the priests and sacrificers, follow that characteristic sign of yours, the mark of recognition, that is, they pour oblations into you when you are kindled. Then the priests and others, the foremost, superior through action, birth, learning and virtues, protected by you, अवर्धंत (*avardhaṃta*), prosper."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- हि (*hi*)—for which reason
- मह्ना (*mahnā*)—as the result of your greatness
- जज्ञानः (*jajñānaḥ*)—having been born
- निषद्य (*niṣadya*)—having sat down
- सद्यः (*sadyaḥ*)—immediately on being born
- हव्यः (*havyaḥ*)—fit to receive the oblation
- बभूथ (*babhūtha*)—you became; for that very reason
- देवासः (*devāsaḥ*)—the priests and sacrificers who offer the oblation
- ते (*te*)—your
- तं केतं (*taṃ ketaṃ*)—that well-known sign
- अनु आयन् (*anu āyan*)—follow
- अध (*adha*)—afterwards
- प्रथमासः (*prathamāsaḥ*)—possessing most excellent qualities
- ऊमाः (*ūmāḥ*)—protected by you
- अवर्धंत (*avardhaṃta*)—prosper

### Printed p. 290

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, for the reason that, as the result of your greatness, you were born, sat down, and at once on being born became fit to receive the oblation, for that very reason the priests and sacrificers who offer the oblation follow your well-known sign. Afterwards, possessing most excellent qualities and protected by you, they prosper.

##### English translation
Since you, Agni, born in your glory, and having sat down (on the altar), are immediately entitled to the oblation : therefore the holy (priests) come to that your ensign and the most worthy (worshippers) prosper protected (by you).

## Sūkta 7 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स्वस्ति न इति सप्तर्चं सप्तमं सूक्तं । ऋष्याद्याः पूर्ववत् । स्वस्तीत्यनुक्रांतं ॥ उक्तः सूक्तविनियोगः ॥ स्वस्त्ययन्यां प्रधानस्य स्वस्ति न इत्येषानुवाक्या ।
> सूत्रितं च । अग्निः स्वस्तिमान् स्वस्ति नो दिवो अग्ने पृथिव्याः । आ. २-१० । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The hymn स्वस्ति न (*svasti na*) has seven Ṛks and is the seventh hymn. Seer and the rest as before. The Anukramaṇī says "svasti". The use of the hymn has been stated. In the Svastyayanī (iṣṭi), at the principal offering, the Ṛk स्वस्ति न (*svasti na*) is the anuvākyā. It is prescribed in the sūtra: अग्निः स्वस्तिमान् स्वस्ति नो दिवो अग्ने पृथिव्याः (*agniḥ svastimān svasti no divo agne pṛthivyāḥ*) (Ā. 2-10).

##### Anuvāda

This hymn "स्वस्ति नः (*svasti naḥ*)" is the seventh hymn in the first anuvāka. It has seven Ṛks. Its seer, deity and metre are the same as those of the previous hymn. In the Anukramaṇī it is described as "svasti". The use of the hymn was stated earlier. In the principal offering of the iṣṭi called Svastyayanī this Ṛk "स्वस्ति नः (*svasti naḥ*)" is to be recited as the anuvākyā mantra, as explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, in the sūtra "Agniḥ svastimān, svasti no divo agne pṛthivyāḥ" (Ā. 2.10).

Particulars: Sūkta 7. Maṇḍala 10, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 7. Aṣṭaka 7, Adhyāya 6, Varga 1. Number of Ṛks in the hymn: 7. Seer: Trita Āptya. Deity: Agni. Metre: Triṣṭup.

### Printed p. 291
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स्व॒स्ति नो॑ दि॒वो अ॑ग्ने पृथि॒व्या वि॒श्वायु॑र्धेहि य॒जथा॑य  
> देव ।  
> सचे॑महि॒ तव॑ दस्म प्रके॒तैरु॑रु॒ष्या ण॑ उ॒रुभि॑र्देव॒ शंसैः॑ ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> स्वस्ति । नः । दिवः । अग्ने । पृथिव्याः । विश्वआयुः । धेहि । यजथाय । देव ।  
> सचेमहि । तव । दस्म । प्रकतैः । उरुष्य । नः । उरुभिः । देव । शंसैः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे देव दानादिगुणविशिष्टाग्ने त्वं दिवः पृथिव्या द्यावापृथिव्योः सकाशान्नोऽस्मभ्यं विश्वायुः सर्वान्नं स्वस्ति पुत्रादि यजथाय देवयागार्थं धेहि ।
> प्रयच्छ । सर्वोपद्रवरहिताः सर्वान्नपुत्रयुक्ताश्च संतो वयं यथा यज्ञानो
> भवेम तथास्मान्कुर्विति वाक्यशेषः । अपि च वयं सचेमहि । हे दस्म दर्शनीय
> देवाग्ने उरुभिर्बहुभिः शंसैः शंसनीयैस्तव संबंधिभिः प्रकेतैः पालनोपायप्रज्ञानैनोर्नोऽस्मानुरुष्य । रक्ष ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O divine Agni, endowed with qualities such as generosity, give us from heaven and earth, from the two, all food, welfare (sons and so forth), for the sacrifice to the gods. The remainder of the sentence: "Make us such that, free of all troubles and possessed of all food and sons, we may be sacrificers." Moreover we would be joined with you. O beautiful (दस्म (*dasma*)) Agni, protect us with your many praiseworthy signs, the means of knowledge for protection.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देव (*deva*)—O divine one
- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—from heaven
- पृथिव्याः (*pṛthivyāḥ*)—from earth
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- विश्वायुः (*viśvāyuḥ*)—all kinds of food
- स्वस्ति (*svasti*)—wealth such as sons
- यजथाय (*yajathāya*)—for worship of the gods
- धेहि (*dhehi*)—provide
- दस्म (*dasma*)—O one of attractive form
- देव (*deva*)—O divine Agni
- उरुभिः (*urubhiḥ*)—abundant
- शंसैः (*śaṃsaiḥ*)—praiseworthy
- तव प्रकेतैः (*tava praketaiḥ*)—by your signs of protection and the like
- सचेमहि (*sacemahi*)—may we be joined
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- उरुष्य (*uruṣya*)—protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O divine Agni, from heaven and from earth provide us all kinds of food and wealth such as sons, for worship of the gods. O Agni, of attractive form and divine, may we be joined with your abundant and praiseworthy signs of protection and the like. Protect us.

### Printed p. 292

##### English translation
Divine Agni, bestow upon us from earth and heaven prosperity and abundant food, that we may be able to offer sacrifices. May we be associated beautiful (Agni), with your indications (of favour) : protect us, divine Agni on account of our many hymns.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒मा अ॑ग्ने म॒तय॒स्तुभ्यं॑ जा॒ता गोभि॒रश्वै॑र॒भि गृ॑णंति॒ राधः॑ ।  
> य॒दा ते॒ मर्तो॒ अनु॒ भोग॒मान॒ड्वसो॒ दधा॑नो म॒तिभिः॑  
> सुजात ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इमाः । अग्ने । मतयः । शुभ्यं । जाताः । गोभिः । अश्वैः । अभि । गृणंति । राधः ।  
> यदा । ते । मर्तः । अनु । भोगं । आनट् । वसो इति । दधानः । मतिभिः ।  
> सुजात ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने इमा ईदृश्यो मतयः स्तुतयस्तुभ्यं त्वदर्थं जाता अस्मदादिस्तोतृमुखादुत्पन्नाः । उच्चारिता इत्यर्थः । गोभिरश्वैश्च सहितं राधस्त्वया दीयमानं हिरण्यादिकं धनमभि गृणंति । अभिष्टुवंति । अस्मत्सुत्यनुरूपान्नमस्सभ्यं
> त्वया दत्तमिति प्रशंसंति । यदा मर्तोऽस्मदादिको मनुष्यस्ते त्वया दीयमानं भोगं भोगार्हं धनमन्वानट् प्राप्नोति लभते तदा हे वसो प्रशस्यवसुमन् । यद्वा । स्वेन तेजसा सर्वस्याच्छादयितः । हे सुजात शोभनकर्मार्थमुत्सन्नाग्ने त्वं दधानोऽस्मभ्यं धनं प्रयच्छन् मतिभिरस्मदीयाभिः स्तूयस इति
> शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, these hymns (मतयः (*matayaḥ*)), praises, born for you, arisen from the mouths of us worshippers, uttered, praise (अभि गृणंति (*abhi gṛṇaṃti*)) the wealth (राधः (*rādhaḥ*)) given by you, gold and the like, together with cattle and horses: they extol "this has been given to us by you, in accordance with our praise". When a mortal such as we obtains (आनट् (*ānaṭ*)) the enjoyable wealth given by you, then, O वसो (*vaso*), possessor of excellent wealth — or else, one who covers everything with his own splendour — O well-born (सुजात (*sujāta*)) Agni, born for good works, you, bestowing (दधानः (*dadhānaḥ*)) wealth on us, are praised by our hymns (the remainder: "are praised").

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- तुभ्यं (*tubhyaṃ*)—addressed to you
- जाताः (*jātāḥ*)—born
- इमाः मतयः (*imāḥ matayaḥ*)—these praises
- गोभिः (*gobhiḥ*)—with cattle
- अश्वैः (*aśvaiḥ*)—with horses, combined

### Printed p. 293
- राधः (*rādhaḥ*)—wealth
- अभि गृणंति (*abhi gṛṇaṃti*)—praise
- वसो (*vaso*)—one who covers everything with your splendour
- सुजात (*sujāta*)—O Agni of excellent birth
- यदा (*yadā*)—when
- मर्तः (*martaḥ*)—a man
- ते (*te*)—given by you
- भोगं (*bhogaṃ*)—enjoyable
- अनु आनट् (*anu ānaṭ*)—obtains; then
- दधानः (*dadhānaḥ*)—you, giving wealth to us also
- मतिभिः (*matibhiḥ*)—by our praises, are praised

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, these praises composed for you ask for wealth together with cattle and horses. O Agni, who cover everything with your own splendour and are of excellent birth, when a man obtains the wealth given by you, the enjoyable wealth, then you, giving wealth to us also, are praised by our praises.

##### English translation
These hymns, addressed, Agni, to you, praise you for wealth, together with cattle and horses : when a mortal obtains the means of enjoyment from you, (then), opulent and well-born Agni, you, who are the granter of benefits, (are celebrated) by our praises.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒ग्निं म॑न्ये पि॒तर॑म॒ग्निमा॒पिम॒ग्निं भ्रात॑रं॒ सद॒मित्सखा॑यं ।  
> अ॒ग्नेरनी॑कं बृह॒तः स॑पर्यं दि॒वि शु॒क्रं य॑ज॒तं सूर्य॑स्य ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अग्निं । मन्ये । पितरं । अग्निं । आपिं । अग्निं । भ्रातरं । सदं । इत् । सखायं ।  
> अग्नेः । अनेकं । बृहतः । सपर्यं । दिवि । शुक्रं । यजतं । सूर्यस्य ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अहं स्तोता पितरमग्निमेव मन्ये । जाने । आपिं बंधुमग्निमेव मन्ये ।
> भ्रातरमग्निमेव मन्ये । सदमित् सदैवाविसंवादिनं सखायमग्निमेव जानामि ।
> बृहतो महतोऽग्नेरनीकमाहवनीयाख्यं मुखं सपर्यं । परिचरामि । तत्र
> लुप्तोपममेतत् । दिवि द्युलोके स्थितं यजतं यष्टव्यं पूजनीयं सूर्यस्य
> संबंधि शुक्रं दीप्तिमन्मंडलं यथा कश्चिदभिप्रेतार्थसिद्धय आराधयति तद्वत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

I, the worshipper, regard Agni alone as father; I regard Agni alone as kinsman (आपिं (*āpiṃ*)) and as brother; always (सदमित् (*sadamit*)) I know Agni alone as the friend who never disagrees. I serve the face (*anīkaṃ*) of great Agni, called Āhavanīya; here there is an omitted simile: as one worships the bright, adorable disc of the sun, placed in heaven, for the attainment of a desired object.

### Printed p. 294

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- अग्निं (*agniṃ*)—Agni
- पितरं (*pitaraṃ*)—as my father
- मन्ये (*manye*)—I consider
- अग्निं (*agniṃ*)—Agni
- आपिं (*āpiṃ*)—I consider a kinsman
- अग्निं (*agniṃ*)—Agni
- भ्रातरं (*bhrātaraṃ*)—I regard as my elder brother
- सदमित् (*sadamit*)—always
- अग्निं (*agniṃ*)—Agni alone
- सखायं (*sakhāyaṃ*)—I know as my friend
- यजतं (*yajataṃ*)—worthy of worship
- शुक्रं (*śukraṃ*)—radiant
- सूरस्य (*sūrasya*)—as one worships the solar disc
- बृहतः (*bṛhataḥ*)—of the great
- अग्नेः (*agneḥ*)—of Agni
- अनेकं (*anekaṃ*)—the face
- सपरं (*saparaṃ*)—I worship

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

I know Agni alone as my father. I know him as my kinsman, as brother, and as a dear friend. As one worships the solar disc, worthy of worship and radiant, so I worship the face of the great Agni.

##### English translation
I regard Agni as a father, as a kinsman, as a brother, as a constant friend. I reverence the face of the mighty Agni, radiant in heaven, adorable as the sun.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सि॒ध्रा अ॑ग्ने॒ धियो॑ अ॒स्मे सनु॑त्री॒र्यं त्राय॑से॒ दम॒ आ नित्य॑होता ।  
> ऋ॒तावा॒ स रो॒हिद॑श्वः पुरु॒क्षुद्युर्भि॑रस्मा॒ अह॑भिर्वा॒मम॑स्तु ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सिध्राः । अग्ने । धियः । अस्मे इति । सनुत्रीः । यं । त्रायसे । दमे । आ । नित्यs.  
> होता ।  
> ऋतवा । सः । रोहित् अश्वः । पुरुक्षुः । द्युभिः । अस्मै । अहभिः । वामं । अस्तु ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने सनुत्रीः संभक्त्र्येऽस्मे अस्माकं संबंधिन्यो धियः स्तुतयः सिद्धाः
> सिद्धा निष्पन्नाः । त्वदर्थमस्माभिः कृता इत्यर्थः । दम आ अस्मद्यज्ञगृहे मर्यावत-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, our praises (धियः (*dhiyaḥ*)), which are सनुत्रीः (*sanutrīḥ*), bestowing, are accomplished (सिद्धाः (*siddhāḥ*)), perfected; that is, they have been made by us for you. In our sacrificial house (दम आ (*dama ā*)) ….

### Printed p. 295

> यावस्थितस्त्वं नित्यहोता सततं होमनिष्पादको देवानामाह्वाता वा सन् यं
> त्रायसे कर्मवैगुण्याद्रक्षसि सोऽहं त्प्रसादात्त्वत्सायुज्यं गतः सन्नृतवा
> यज्ञवान् सत्यवान् ना स्यामिति शेषः रोहिदश्वो रोहित्संज्ञकाश्वः पुरुक्षुर्बह्वन्नस्थ स्यां ! किंचास्मा ईदृग्यूताय तुभ्यं द्युभिर्दिप्तेष्टहभिरहःसु दिवसेषु
> वामं वननीयं हविरस्तु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

(Continuing Sāyaṇa on the preceding Ṛk.) Sāyaṇa says: you who are the constant होता (*hotā*), always accomplishing the oblation, or else the summoner of the gods, whom you protect from the defects of the rite, I, having attained your companionship by your grace, would be ऋतवा (*ṛtavā*), one possessed of sacrifice, possessed of truth. स्यां (*syāṃ*) is to be supplied. रोहिदश्वः (*rohidaśvaḥ*) means one who has a horse named Rohit. पुरुक्षुः (*purukṣuḥ*) means one rich in much food. Moreover, for you, so joined, let a desirable oblation be on the days (अहभिः (*ahabhiḥ*)), the shining (द्युभिः (*dyubhiḥ*)) days.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- सनुत्रीः (*sanutrīḥ*)—full of devotion
- अस्मे धियः (*asme dhiyaḥ*)—our praises
- सिध्राः (*sidhrāḥ*)—have been composed for you alone **NOTE**
- दम आ (*dama ā*)—in our sacrificial house
- नित्यहोता (*nityahotā*)—you who are always the invoker
- यं (*yaṃ*)—whom (me)
- त्रायसे (*trāyase*)—you protect
- सः (*saḥ*)—that I
- ऋतवा (*ṛtavā*)—may I be always possessed of sacrifice
- रोहिदश्वः (*rohidaśvaḥ*)—possessing a golden-coloured horse
- पुरुक्षुः (*purukṣuḥ*)—possessing abundant food; may I become so
- अस्मै (*asmai*)—to you
- द्युभिः (*dyubhiḥ*)—shining
- अहभिः (*ahabhiḥ*)—on days
- वामं (*vāmaṃ*)—an attractive oblation
- अस्तु (*astu*)—let it be offered

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, our devout praises have been composed for you alone. May I, whom you, the ever-present invoker in our sacrificial house, protect, be always possessed of sacrifice; may I have a golden-coloured horse and abundant food. May an attractive oblation be offered to you on shining days.

##### English translation
Agni, our earnest praises have been composed for you. May he whom you, the perpetual invoker of the gods, protect in the sacrificial chamber, be rich in sacrifices, the master of red steeds, the possessor of abundant food ; may the desirable (oblation) be granted him on shining days.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> द्युभि॑र्हि॒तं मि॒त्रमि॑व प्र॒योगं॑ प्र॒त्नमृ॒त्तिज॑मध्व॒रस्य॑ जा॒रं ।  
> बा॒हुभ्या॑म॒ग्निमा॒यवो॑ऽजनंत वि॒क्षु होता॑रं॒ न्य॑सादयंत॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> द्युभिः । हितं । मित्रं इव । पुयोगं । प्रत्नं । ऋत्विजं । अध्वरस्य । जारं ।  
> बाहुभ्यां । अग्निं । आयवः । अजनंत । विक्षु । हॊतारं । नि । असादयंत ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 296

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> द्युभिर्हितं दीप्तैराहितं मित्रमिव स्निग्धमिव प्रयोगं प्रयोक्तव्यं प्रश्नॆं
> पुराणमृत्वजं होतृसंज्ञकं । अग्निर्वै देवानां होतासीदिति श्रुतेः । अध्वरस्य
> जारं समापयितारं एवंभूतमग्निमायवो यजमाना मनुष्या बाहुभ्यामजनंत । जनितवंतः । किंच विक्षु मनुष्येषु । तदाकारेषु देवेष्टित्यर्थः ।
> होतारमाह्वातारं न्यसादयंत । नियमेन सादितवंतः । देवानां यागार्थं
> निरूपितवंत इत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

द्युभिर्हितं (*dyubhirhitaṃ*) means placed (हितं (*hitaṃ*)) by the shining ones. मित्रमिव (*mitramiva*) means like one who is affectionate. प्रयोगं (*prayogaṃ*) means one to be employed. प्रत्नं (*pratnaṃ*) means ancient. The ऋत्विजं (*ṛtvijaṃ*) is the priest called Hotṛ, according to the Śruti अग्निर्वै देवानां होतासीदिति (*agnirvai devānāṃ hotāsīditi*). अध्वरस्य जारं (*adhvarasya jāraṃ*) means the one who brings the sacrifice to completion. Such an Agni the आयवः (*āyavaḥ*), the sacrificers, men, generated (अजनंत (*ajanaṃta*)) with their arms. Moreover, among men (विक्षु (*vikṣu*))—that is, in rites of the gods of that form—they firmly seated him (न्यसादयंत (*nyasādayaṃta*)) as the summoner (होतारं (*hotāraṃ*)); the meaning is that they appointed him for the sacrifice of the gods.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- द्युभिः (*dyubhiḥ*)—by radiances
- हितं (*hitaṃ*)—covered
- मित्रमिव (*mitramiva*)—like a friend
- प्रयोगं (*prayogaṃ*)—helpful
- प्रत्नं (*pratnaṃ*)—ancient
- ऋत्विजं (*ṛtvijaṃ*)—the priest in the form of the Hotṛ
- अध्वरस्य (*adhvarasya*)—for the sacrifice
- जारं (*jāraṃ*)—its accomplisher
- अग्निं (*agniṃ*)—Agni
- आयवः (*āyavaḥ*)—men (the sacrificers)
- बाहुभ्यां (*bāhubhyāṃ*)—with their arms
- अजनंत (*ajanaṃta*)—have produced
- विक्षु (*vikṣu*)—among men
- होतारं (*hotāraṃ*)—as the Hotṛ
- न्यसादयंत (*nyasādayaṃta*)—have established

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The sacrificers have churned out and produced with their arms Agni, who is covered with radiances, helpful like a friend, ancient, the priest in the form of the Hotṛ, the accomplisher of the sacrifice. They have established him among men as the Hotṛ.

##### English translation
Men have generated with their arms Agni, who is decorated with rays of light, selected as a friend, the ancient priest, the accomplisher of the sacrifice, and they have appointed him amongst people, the invoker of the gods.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स्व॒यं य॑जस्य दि॒वि दे॑व दे॒वान्किं ते॒ पाकः॑ कृणव॒दप्र॑चेताः ।  
> यथाय॑ज ऋ॒तुभि॑र्देव दे॒वने॒वा य॑जस्व त॒न्वं॑ सुजात ॥ ६ ॥

### Printed p. 297

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> स्वयं । यजस्व । दिवि । देव । देवान् । किं । ते । पाकः । कृणवत् । अप्रचेताः ।  
> यथा । अयजः । ऋतुभिः । देव । देवान् । एव । यजस्व । तन्नं । सुजात ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे देव द्योतमानाग्ने त्वं दिवि द्युलोके स्थितान्देवानिंद्रादीन् स्वयमात्मनैव यजस्व ! पाकः पक्तव्यप्रज्ञोऽप्रचेता अप्रकृष्टज्ञानो मनुष्यस्ते तव
> किं कृणवत् । किं करोति । त्वयानधिष्ठितः किंचिदपि न जानातीत्यर्थः ! किंच हे देव
> त्वमृतुभिः कालविशेषैर्देवान्यथायजः इष्टवानसि एवैवं हे सुजात शोभनजन्मन्नग्ने तन्नमात्मानमपि यजस्व ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O shining Agni, you yourself, by your own self, sacrifice to the gods such as Indra who stand in heaven (दिवि (*divi*), in the world of heaven). पाकः (*pākaḥ*) is one whose understanding is still to be ripened, अप्रचेताः (*apracetāḥ*) one of undeveloped knowledge, a man: what (किं कृणवत् (*kiṃ kṛṇavat*)) can he do for you? Not presided over by you, he knows nothing at all. Moreover, O Agni, as you have sacrificed to the gods in the ऋतुभिः (*ṛtubhiḥ*), the particular seasons, so too, O well-born (सुजात (*sujāta*)) Agni, sacrifice to your own body (तन्वं (*tanvaṃ*)), your own self.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देव (*deva*)—O shining Agni
- दिवि (*divi*)—dwelling in the world of heaven
- देवान् (*devān*)—the gods
- स्वयं (*svayaṃ*)—yourself
- यजस्व (*yajasva*)—worship
- साकः (*sākaḥ*)—one of unripe understanding **NOTE**
- अप्रचेताः (*apracetāḥ*)—a man without excellent knowledge
- ते (*te*)—knowing your greatness
- किं कृणवत् (*kiṃ kṛṇavat*)—in what way can he worship?
- देव (*deva*)—O Agni
- ऋतुभिः (*ṛtubhiḥ*)—according to each season
- देवान् (*devān*)—the gods
- यथा आयजः (*yathā āyajaḥ*)—in whatever way you have worshipped
- एव (*eva*)—in that same way
- सुजात (*sujāta*)—O Agni of excellent birth
- तन्नं (*tannaṃ*)—yourself also
- यजस्व (*yajasva*)—worship

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O shining Agni, worship the gods who dwell in heaven yourself. A man of unripe understanding and without excellent knowledge, not knowing your greatness, cannot worship you well. O Agni, as you have worshipped the gods in accordance with each season, so, O Agni of excellent birth, worship yourself also.

##### English translation
Offer sacrifice yourself, divine (Agni), to the gods in heaven : what can one who is ignorant and of immature understanding effect for you ? As in due seasons, divine (Agni), you have sacrificed to the gods, so you, well-engendered (Agni), sacrifice to your own person.

### Printed p. 298

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> भवा॑ नो अग्नेऽवि॒तोत गो॒पा भवा॑ वय॒स्कृदु॒त नो॑  ॒
> वयोधाः ।  
> रास्वा॑ च नः सुमहो ह॒व्यदा॑तिं॒ त्रास्वो॒त न॑स्त॒न्वो॒३॒॑ ३ अप्र॑युच्छन् ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> भव । नः । अग्ने । अविता । उत । गोपाः । भव । वयःकृत् । उत । नः । वयःधाः।  
> रास्व । च । नः । सुमहः । हव्यदातिं । त्रास्व । उत । नःतन्न्वः । अप्रयुच्छन् ॥ ७॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने त्वं नोऽस्माकमविता दृष्टभयेभ्यो रक्षको भव । भूयाः ।
> उतापि च गोपा अदृष्टभयेभ्यो गोप्ता रक्षको भव । उतापि च वयस्कदन्नस्य
> कर्ता भव । वयोधा अन्नस्य दाता भव हे सुमह: सुष्टु पूजनीयाग्ने त्वं
> स्वभूतां हव्यदातिं हविषो दातिं नोऽस्मभ्यं रास्व च । प्रयच्छ । उतापि च
> नोऽस्माकं स्वभूतास्तन्वः शरीराण्यप्रयुच्छन्न प्रमाद्यंस्रास्व । पालय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, be our protector (अविता (*avitā*)) from visible dangers. Also be our गोपाः (*gopāḥ*), our guardian from invisible dangers. Also be the maker of food (*vayaskṛt*) and the giver of food (वयोधाः (*vayodhāḥ*)). O सुमहः (*sumahaḥ*), Agni who are well worthy of worship, grant (रास्व (*rāsva*)) to us your own हव्यदातिं (*havyadātiṃ*), the giving of the oblation. And protect (त्रास्व (*trāsva*)), watch over, our own bodies (तन्वः (*tanvaḥ*)), not being negligent (अप्रयुच्छन् (*aprayucchan*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- अविता (*avitā*)—as protector
- भव (*bhava*)—be
- उत (*uta*)—and
- गोपाः (*gopāḥ*)—be our guardian
- उत (*uta*)—and
- वयस्कत् (*vayaskat*)—be the producer of food for us **NOTE**
- भव (*bhava*)—be
- वयोधाः (*vayodhāḥ*)—be the giver of food
- सुमहः (*sumahaḥ*)—O Agni, worthy of excellent worship
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- हव्यदातिं (*havyadātiṃ*)—fitness for giving oblations
- रास्व (*rāsva*)—give
- उत (*uta*)—and
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- तन्वः (*tanvaḥ*)—bodies
- अप्रयुच्छन् (*aprayucchan*)—carefully
- त्रास्व (*trāsva*)—protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, be our protector. Remove all our calamities and protect us; produce food for us and give it. O Agni, worthy of excellent worship, give us fitness for giving oblations. Carefully protect our bodies.

### Printed p. 299

##### English translation
Be our protector, Agni, (in obvious peril) : be our presever (in un, seen danger), be the provider and giver of sustenance ; bestow upon us- mighty Agni, the donation of oblations, and ever attentive, protect our persons.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk, which serves as the conclusion of the sūkta, Agni is prayed to grant protection and food and the like, to create in us the fitness to offer oblations and so forth to the gods, and to protect us at all times. Here the commentator explains two kinds of protection: protection from enemies and wild beasts that can be seen is one kind; protection from invisible things such as disease, and from sins, is the other.

## Sūkta 8 (Maṇḍala 10)
Introduction.
The seer of this sūkta and of the one after it is Triśirā, son of Tvaṣṭṛ. This Triśirā was formerly the family priest of the gods. He had a connection with the Asuras; that is, he was born of an Asura woman. Therefore he also had a fondness for the Asuras. Fearing that this partisan of the Asuras might somehow harm the gods, Indra wished to kill him. In this matter Trita (the seer Trita Āptya) became his helper. In some places it is said that Indra killed him for the sake of this Trita, that is, to please Trita.

In the Ṛk
अस्मभ्यं तत्वाष्टं विश्वरूपमरंधयः साख्यस्य त्रिताय ॥ (*asmabhyaṃ tatvāṣṭaṃ viśvarūpamaraṃdhayaḥ sākhyasya tritāya ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-11-19)
it is said that Indra killed Viśvarūpa out of friendship for Trita. In the Bṛhaddevatā (sixth chapter) this is said about him:
प्र केतुनेति त्वाष्ट्रस्तु त्रिशिराः सूक्तमुत्तरं ॥ १४७ ॥ (*pra ketuneti tvāṣṭrastu triśirāḥ sūktamuttaraṃ ‖ 147 ‖*)
ऋचस्त्वस्य षळाग्नेय्यं तृचस्त्वस्येति यः परः । (*ṛcastvasya ṣaḷāgneyyaṃ tṛcastvasyeti yaḥ paraḥ |*)
तेनेंद्रमभितुष्टाव स्वप्नांत इति नः श्रुतिः ॥ १४८ ॥ (*teneṃdramabhituṣṭāva svapnāṃta iti naḥ śrutiḥ ‖ 148 ‖*)

### Printed p. 300

The seer of this sūkta beginning प्रकेतुना (*praketunā*) is Triśirā, son of Tvaṣṭṛ. In the first six Ṛks of this sūkta this seer praises Agni, and in the next three, Indra.
अभवत्स हि देवानां पुरोधाः प्रियकाम्यया । (*abhavatsa hi devānāṃ purodhāḥ priyakāmyayā |*)
असुराणां स्वसुः पुत्रस्त्रिशिरा विश्वरूपधृक् ॥ १४९ ॥ (*asurāṇāṃ svasuḥ putrastriśirā viśvarūpadhṛk ‖ 149 ‖*)
तमृषिं प्रहितं त्विंद्रो देवेषु बुबुधेऽसुरैः । (*tamṛṣiṃ prahitaṃ tviṃdro deveṣu bubudhe 'suraiḥ |*)
सोऽस्य वज्रेण तान्याशु शिरांसि त्रीण्यथाच्छिदत् ॥ १५० ॥ (*so 'sya vajreṇa tānyāśu śirāṃsi trīṇyathācchidat ‖ 150 ‖*)
This seer Triśirā was formerly the family priest of the gods. Since he was the son of the Asuras' sister, he remained a partisan of the Asuras. The gods came to know of this and suspected that the Asuras themselves, for their own advantage, to learn the secrets of the gods, had installed him, under the name Viśvarūpa, as family priest among the gods, to act as a spy. Therefore Indra took his thunderbolt and cut off the three heads of this Triśiras.
तस्य यत्सोमपानं तु मुखं सोऽभूत्कपिंजलः । (*tasya yatsomapānaṃ tu mukhaṃ so 'bhūtkapiṃjalaḥ |*)
कलविंकः सुरापाणं अन्नादं तित्तिरिस्त्वभूत् ॥ १५१ ॥ (*kalaviṃkaḥ surāpāṇaṃ annādaṃ tittiristvabhūt ‖ 151 ‖*)
Of the three heads of that Triśiras, the mouth that drank Soma became the bird called kapiñjala. The mouth that drank surā became the bird called kalaviṅka. The mouth that ate food became the bird called tittiri.
तं वागभ्यवदद्ब्रह्मी ब्रह्महासि शतक्रतो । (*taṃ vāgabhyavadadbrahmī brahmahāsi śatakrato |*)
प्रपन्नं हतवान्यस्माद्विश्वरूपं पराङ्मुखं ॥ १५२ ॥ (*prapannaṃ hatavānyasmādviśvarūpaṃ parāṅmukhaṃ ‖ 152 ‖*)
At that time a bodiless voice was heard. Addressing Indra it said: "O Indra, you have gone behind Viśvarūpa, who was पराङ्मुखं (*parāṅmukhaṃ*), that is, not facing you but turned in another direction, and have killed him. Since he was a Brāhmaṇa, the guilt of brahmin-slaying has come upon you."
तमभ्यसिंचत्तूक्तेन ऋषिराप इति स्वयं । (*tamabhyasiṃcattūktena ṛṣirāpa iti svayaṃ |*)
सिंधुद्वीप्रोपनुत्त्यर्थं तस्याश्लीलस्य पाप्मन्य ॥ १५३ ॥ (*siṃdhudvīpropanuttyarthaṃ tasyāślīlasya pāpmanya ‖ 153 ‖*)
(Bṛ. De. chapter 6)
To remove this sin, Indra besought Sindhudvīpa, the seer of the next sūkta, and that seer, by reciting the sūkta of nine Ṛks beginning आपोहिष्ठा (*āpohiṣṭhā*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-9th sūkta), water **NOTE**

### Printed p. 301

…took water, consecrated it with mantras and bathed Indra with it. By that Indra's sin was removed, as has been said. In the Taittirīya Saṃhitā this matter is told somewhat differently; we shall explain that too further on.

In this sūkta the seer praises Indra, saying that he slew the son of Tvaṣṭṛ, Viśvarūpa, for the sake of the royal sage Trita. Who this Trita is, what his story is, and many other matters concerning him, we have already explained at length earlier, that is, in this same volume at p. 187, and in Vol. 8 at pp. 443 and 473, and elsewhere. Although the word Trita occurs in many Ṛks, nothing more about him can be learnt from them. Or rather, even in the Veda the word Trita is used in five or six places, but nothing is learnt there either. Only his association with Indra is found in many places. As said everywhere, all that is known is that Trita is a deity dwelling very far away, and that sin or bad dreams are sent to him. (A. Ve. 1-131-1, 3; 19-55-4) **NOTE** In the Taittirīya Saṃhitā he is said to be the giver of long life (Tai. Saṃ. 1-8-10-2). He is the preparer of the Soma juice that confers immortality. Trita is one of three brothers, Ekata, Dvita and Trita. All three are sons of Agni; that Trita arose from water is said in Brāhmaṇas such as Śa. Brā. 1-2-3-1, 2 and Tai. Brā. 3-2-8-10, 11.

As said in the Ṛgveda, in three or four contexts Indra and Trita have done the same deed (the slaying of a demon). Once Indra is the instigator of Trita; in another place it is said that Indra himself is encouraged by Trita. Twice Indra has worked for Trita. As said in the present sūkta, Indra slew for Trita one called Viśvarūpa. Trita seeks Agni; he kindles Agni in heaven; he dwells in the houses of men in place of Agni; his dwelling is hidden far away; Soma is there too. Indra drinks Soma, but Trita prepares Soma. This is the difference between Indra and Trita.

From the points stated above, Trita may be called a lightning deity, that is, the atmospheric form of Agni; like the deities Agni, Vāyu, Indra and Sūrya, he is a deity of the middle region. But since all this deity's deeds are done by Indra, he is a subordinate deity in the Ṛgveda. It may be that the connection of Trita with Soma is stated because, in the form of lightning, he brought Soma from heaven to the earth. Since the evidence is slight, each scholar has formed a different opinion about him.

### Printed p. 302

To mention only a few: the scholar Roth (Roth) says he is a deity of water and wind; Hillbratnd (Hillbratnd) a deity of the kindled sky; Perry (Perry) a deity of the storm. Another scholar, Pischel (Pischel), says that he was first a deity of the sea and water, then a physician among men, and afterwards attained godhood. Hardy (Hardy) says he is a moon deity. In the present sūkta, since Trita has the relation of friendship with Indra, it is said that for his sake Indra killed the son of Tvaṣṭṛ called Viśvarūpa.

The slaying of Viśvarūpa by Indra is told at the beginning of the fifth prapāṭhaka of the second kāṇḍa of the Taittirīya Saṃhitā. In the Ṛgveda too, on this matter, in the Ṛk

> भूरीदिंद्र उदिनक्षंतमोजोऽवाभिनत्सत्पतिर्मन्यमानं ।
> त्वाष्टस्य चिद्विश्वरूपस्य गोनामाचक्राणस्त्रीणि शीर्षा परा वर्क् ॥

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-8-9)

it is said that Indra cut off the three heads of Viśvarūpa, the son of Tvaṣṭṛ. That Indra did this deed for Trita, and that Trita helped Indra in it, is said in the 7th Ṛk of the same sūkta—

> अस्य त्रितः क्रतुना वव्रे अंतरिच्छन् धीतिं पितुरेवैः परस्य ।
> सचस्यमानः पित्रोरुपस्थे जामि ब्रुवाण आयुधानि वेति ।
> स पित्राण्यायुधानि विद्वानिंद्रॆषित आप्त्यो अभ्ययुध्यत् ।
> त्रिशीर्षाणं सप्तरश्मिं जघन्वान् त्वाष्टस्य चिन्निः सस्यजे त्रितो गाः ॥

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-8-7, 8)

In the bhāṣya on these Ṛks Sāyaṇa has also told a narrative: Indra asked Trita's help in killing Viśvarūpa, the son of Tvaṣṭṛ, who had three heads. To increase his own strength Trita asked as a boon that he be given a share of the offering in sacrifices. Indra agreed, and gave him water for washing the hands and a share in the sacrifice. Then Trita, equipped with the weapons that belonged to his father and impelled by Indra, fought with Viśvarūpa, the son of Tvaṣṭṛ, struck him hard with his weapons and made him faint. Afterwards Indra cut off the three heads of that Viśvarūpa. This is what is said in the Ṛks above.

### Printed p. 303

This same matter is told at length in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā (Tai. Saṃ. 2-5-1-1); it is explained here with its meaning—

> विश्वरूपो वै त्वाष्ट्रः पुरोहितो देवनामासीत् स्वस्रियॊसुराणां तस
> त्रीणि शीर्षाण्यासन्तोमपानग्ं सुरापानमन्नादनग्ं स प्रत्यक्षं देवेभ्यो भागमवदत् परोक्षमसुरेभ्यः ॥

In former times Viśvarūpa, the son of Tvaṣṭṛ, was the family priest of the gods. Since he was the son of the asuras' younger sister, he also had a fondness for the asuras. This Viśvarūpa had three heads (śiras). With one of the three heads he drank Soma, with another he drank surā (liquor), with the third he ate food and the like.
